# Overlapping directed triangles: a legal correlated family with an actual payoff obstruction

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max4, round52.
TASK.md and master_guidance4572ef0ed580856f allocate one analytical round
after completed51, with completed ceiling52 and attempted ceiling53.
No fact_submit, numerical experiment, solver, parameter scan, machine algebra,
trajectory replay, installation, consult, or native subagent was performed.
All new arguments are reproduced together below. They are not fact-graph bricks.

## 1. Verdict, scope, and why this is a different construction

The selected object is ONE family of actual static functions: squared positive
edge differences on all overlapping directed triangles of a complete vertex
set. Triangles share their original vertex variables. The construction is
available in every dimension N>=3 and can use, for example, N=2^m+1 for H_m.
Every member has a finite attained minimum and an exactly paid closest-minimum
distance. The family includes the explicitly calculated signed temporal
correlations and changing noncommuting Hessians of Section5.

Every original directed interpolation row has the explicit nonpositive
formula (8), including remote rows, both optimum directions, and terminal
rows. The first original three-point cycle is evaluated in (16).

The family nevertheless fails quantitatively. For EVERY N>=3, every cyclic
orientation of each triangle, and every initial point outside the minimum
set, its actual normalized error on the original H_m satisfies, for m>=8,

 V_tri <= (2/3)*(49/50)^(2^(m-1)).                         (1)

This is an UPPER bound on actual error, not a weak lower certificate. It is
uniform over growing N, all initial directions and all scales of the initial
point. Its ratios to the original benchmark and the complete SAME-word
Huber, K, alternating-ray and full-complex references tend to zero. Each of
these ratios is strictly below 2^(-18) for every m>=12.

The obstruction persists for any positively weighted directed-triangle
system whose triangle-plane projections sum to the identity on the working
space; the complete construction is one explicit such system. It concerns
the fixed curvature normalization in (3), not other curvature scalings
(including a recalibration to a smaller least smoothness constant), other
edge nonlinearities, larger cycles, arbitrary non-tight incidence geometries,
or the unrestricted W_n.

Comparison with recalled failures, used as awareness only:

* 88f3db7b0a2bb506 supplies a Haar/rank-one transcript that fails a remote
  cycle despite passing adjacent and optimum rows. Here the function is
  fixed first, and (8) pays every row, before using any trajectory claim.
* 19f0eaeb0e7c3518 retains covariance but leaves a signed payment missing.
  Here a proved global contraction gives the actual payoff upper bound;
  no covariance identity is promoted to a positive lower witness.
* 38da1067bcd1a0c8 and 1ad14d90628c1312 leave actual tree-Gibbs occupation
  unresolved. This object has no Gibbs clock, conditional restart, or
  expected-oracle averaging.
* aa3c5e1d91baf405 and c93913a3af467ccc warn against fixed support/core
  assumptions for tree-simplex projections. Here all signs may change at
  every physical query. The upper bound holds through all such changes.

The function is not a change of basis of the fixed tree-simplex projection
law: its gradient is a sum of coupled one-sided edge forces. Already the
three-vertex member has the distinct, noncommuting sector Hessians in
Section5. Their spectra on its plane are {0,2/3} and {1/3,1}; an orthogonal
change of coordinates cannot turn these into the {0,1} eigenvalues of an
affine-face Euclidean projection. The growing construction uses overlapping
triangles, not a direct sum of isolated copies.

## 2. Accepted interfaces and notation

All spaces and norms below are real Euclidean. For a scalar t, t_+=max(t,0);
for a vector this operation is componentwise. The vector e_u is the u-th
coordinate unit vector and 1 is the all-ones vector of the indicated size.

Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 defines

 W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2)

over finite-dimensional convex differentiable L-smooth functions with a
minimizer, and physical GD x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Its full directed
interpolation residual is

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.               (2)

It requires every ordered prefix pair. Its terminal-completion theorem is
not permission to delete remote prefix rows. Here all actual rows, including
both terminal directions, are handled explicitly.

Accepted fact0e7e78056dc6258c bounds legal expected-orthogonal-gradient
adversaries by 2/n^2. We do not assume expected orthogonality; (15) gives an
explicit negative temporal inner product inside this deterministic family.

Accepted factb98bb807d514e80d provides the complete single-cut functional
K_n(h), including Huber(h)=1/(1+2 sum h), and the ORIGINAL all-split
benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/B_n with B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3).
Accepted fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives the COMPLETE feasible-checkpoint maximum
F_ray_n(h)>=K_n(h), retaining both remote ray offsets and accumulated areas.
Accepted factdfde2fa326c908bf gives the FULL complex legal domain D_h defined
by all cycle area/energy inequalities, and Gap_h(z), the maximal terminal
payoff at its precise polynomial points and gradients. We write
C_full(h)=sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z). We do not replace D_h by its universal lens.

No other accepted mathematical predecessor is used. Earlier shared drafts
are not assumed correct in any proof below.

## 3. The actual function and the complete incidence identity

Fix N>=3 and let T_N be all three-element subsets of {0,...,N-1}.
For each tau choose one of its two cyclic orientations, independently of
the trajectory, and let E_tau be the resulting three directed edges.
A concrete choice for tau={u<v<w} is u->v, v->w, w->u.
Repeated edges in different triangles remain separately indexed.
Put

 w_tri=3/[N(N-2)],
 E_N={x in R^N: sum_u x_u=0},
 P_N=I-11^T/N,
 F_N(x)=(w_tri/6)*sum_(tau in T_N) sum_(u->v in E_tau)
                          (x_u-x_v)_+^2.                (3)

This finite formula is fixed at every physical query. No set or coefficient
is selected in response to a gradient. Write P_tau for the orthogonal
projection onto the vectors supported on tau whose three coordinates sum
to zero. The sum of the three unoriented edge outer products on tau is
3P_tau. Every unordered edge occurs in N-2 triangles, while
sum_(u<v)(e_u-e_v)(e_u-e_v)^T=N P_N: its diagonal entries are N-1 and
its off-diagonal entries are -1. Consequently

 sum_tau P_tau = N(N-2)P_N/3,
 sum_tau w_tri P_tau=P_N.                                (4)

Let A_tri have one row sqrt(w_tri/3)*(e_u-e_v)^T for every separately
indexed directed edge u->v in every triangle. Then

 A_tri^T A_tri=P_N,
 F_N(x)=|| (A_tri x)_+ ||^2/2,
 grad F_N(x)=A_tri^T (A_tri x)_+.                         (5)

In particular A_tri is an isometry on E_N and has operator norm1 on R^N.
Its range projection is Pi_tri=A_tri A_tri^T: indeed Pi_tri is symmetric,
and Pi_tri^2=A_tri P_N A_tri^T=Pi_tri.

Each scalar function t_+^2/2 is convex and continuously differentiable,
with derivative t_+. Thus F_N is convex and differentiable. Since the
componentwise positive-part map is 1-Lipschitz, (5) gives

 ||grad F_N(x)-grad F_N(y)|| <= ||x-y||.

Hence F_N is an everywhere-defined convex 1-smooth function. No assertion
that this smoothness upper bound is the least possible constant for every
orientation and N is needed or made. The normalization in (3) is fixed.

## 4. Every directed row, the minimum, physical GD and true distance

For arbitrary real t,u direct consideration of their four sign pairs gives

 u_+^2/2-t_+^2/2-t_+*(u-t)
       =(u_+-t_+)^2/2+t_+*(-u)_+.                        (6)

For x,y put r=(A_tri x)_+, s=(A_tri y)_+, d=r-s. Summing (6), and using
grad F_N(x)=A_tri^T r, gives

 F_N(y)-F_N(x)-<grad F_N(x),y-x>
       =||d||^2/2+sum_e r_e*(-(A_tri y)_e)_+.

Because Pi_tri is the range projection,
||A_tri^T d||^2=||Pi_tri d||^2 and
||d||^2=||Pi_tri d||^2+||(I-Pi_tri)d||^2. Therefore the exact residual is

 Q(x,y):=F_N(x)-F_N(y)+<grad F_N(x),y-x>
                       +||grad F_N(x)-grad F_N(y)||^2/2
 =-sum_e (A_tri x)_+,e*(-(A_tri y)_e)_+
  -||(I-Pi_tri)((A_tri x)_+-(A_tri y)_+)||^2/2 <=0.        (8)

The orientation here agrees exactly with (2). It holds for EVERY x,y,
so all ordered original trajectory pairs and every nonlocal cycle pass.
It includes equal points at zero steps and both directions involving any
minimizer. No finite collection of adjacent checks is substituted for (8).

Clearly F_N>=0. Equality forces every directed difference in every triangle
to be nonpositive. Around a directed triangle the three differences sum
to zero, so all three must be zero. Every two vertices occur in some
triangle; all coordinates are therefore equal. Conversely constant
vectors give value0. Hence

 argmin F_N=span{1}, min F_N=0,
 x_star=(sum_u x_0,u/N)*1,
 R_tri=dist(x_0,argmin F_N)=||P_N x_0||.                  (9)

We always take x_0 outside the minimum set, so R_tri>0. The mean coordinate
is unchanged by GD because every edge vector has coordinate sum0.

For ANY finite nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), including zero steps,
the exact physical recursion is

 g_i=(w_tri/3)*sum_tau sum_(u->v in E_tau)
                    (x_i,u-x_i,v)_+*(e_u-e_v),
 x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i, 0<=i<n,
 f_i=F_N(x_i), 0<=i<=n.                                 (10)

There is no compressed or restarted child state. It is permissible to
compute g_n, although it is not used in the recursion. The exact actual
terminal payoff is

 V_tri(h,x_0)=2F_N(x_n)/R_tri^2
   =[w_tri/(3R_tri^2)]*sum_tau sum_(u->v in E_tau)
                                      (x_n,u-x_n,v)_+^2. (11)

One explicit unit-distance start for every N is
v_N=sqrt(N/(N-1))*(e_0-(1/N)*1). Its closest minimizer is0 and ||v_N||=1.
Formula (9), not an arbitrary chosen reference minimum, pays the radius for
all other starts as well.
For any L>0 use L F_N and steps h_i/L; the same x_i and payoff result.

## 5. First original H2: signed correlation, actual sector changes and a cycle

Fix the SAME enclosing m>=8 throughout

 lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda*(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
 n=2^m-1, h=H_m.                                       (12)

Thus the first three physical steps are A=1+a, B=1+beta, A. The symbols
A,B in this section are just these two step lengths, not A_tri or B_n.
Since 7/8<=beta<1 and c<=9lambda/8,
a>=7/(9lambda)>3/10, while a<sqrt(2)-1<1/2.
The strict lower bound follows from lambda<5/2, hence 27lambda<70.

Take the N=3 member with edges0->1,1->2,2->0 and initial point
x_0=v_3=(2,-1,-1)/sqrt(6). Direct substitution in (10) gives

 g_0=(1,-1,0)/sqrt(6),
 x_1=(1-a,a,-1)/sqrt(6),
 g_1=(1-2a,3a,-1-a)/(3sqrt(6)).                          (13)

At x_0 only edge01 has a positive difference. At x_1 both edges01 and12
are positive. Write x_2=(p_2,q_2,r_2)/sqrt(6), where

 p_2=1-a-B*(1-2a)/3,
 q_2=-a*beta,
 r_2=-1+B*(1+a)/3,
 d_2=p_2-q_2=[2-beta+a*(5beta-1)]/3.                     (14)

The coordinate sum is0. Also p_2-r_2=(2-a)*(1-B/3)>0, and
3(q_2-r_2)=2-beta-a*(1+4beta)<9/8-(3/10)*(9/2)<0.
Thus p_2>r_2>q_2 and only edge01 is positive at query2. In particular

 g_2=d_2*(1,-1,0)/(3sqrt(6)),
 <g_0,g_1>=(1-5a)/18<0.                                (15)

This is actual nonzero temporal correlation of one legal deterministic
function. It is not an expected Gram of individually illegal oracle paths.

For this triangle A_tri has rows(e_u-e_v)^T/sqrt(3), and I-Pi_tri is
projection onto the all-ones line of the three edge coordinates. The
positive edge vectors at queries0,1,2 are respectively
(3,0,0)/sqrt(18), (1-2a,1+a,0)/sqrt(18), and (d_2,0,0)/sqrt(18).
In the three directed rows below there is no positive-to-negative edge
contribution in the first term of (8). Thus

 Q_02=-(3-d_2)^2/108,
 Q_21=-(d_2-2+a)^2/108,
 Q_10=-(1+a)^2/108,
 Q_02+Q_21+Q_10
    =-[(3-d_2)^2+(d_2-2+a)^2+(1+a)^2]/108<0.             (16)

This explicitly pays the first nonlocal original cycle. Other cycles are
covered by (8), not by an extrapolation from this one.

The third update has unscaled coordinates
p_3=p_2-A*d_2/3, q_3=q_2+A*d_2/3, r_3=r_2.
Its gaps satisfy

 p_3-q_3=d_2*(1-2a)/3>0,
 9(q_3-r_3)=8-4beta-(2+8beta)a+(5beta-1)a^2>0.            (17)

For the second sign, the right side decreases with beta on 0<a<1/2,
because its beta derivative is -4-8a+5a^2<0. Its value at beta=1 is
4-10a+4a^2=2(1-2a)(2-a)>0. Hence query3 again has positive edges01,12.
Its actual value is [(p_3-q_3)^2+(q_3-r_3)^2]/36.

The sector Hessians are L_01/3 and (L_01+L_12)/3, where
L_uv=(e_u-e_v)(e_u-e_v)^T. They do not commute:
L_01 L_12 e_0=0 but L_12 L_01 e_0=-(e_1-e_2).
The object therefore has genuine switching dynamics and is not a single
normal-matrix polynomial trajectory. No claim about a permanent later
sector, core, or support follows from these first three steps.

## 6. Exact triangle radial bounds and its sharp one-step norm factor

First work on one triangle's mean-zero plane, with
f_triangle(z)=[(z_0-z_1)_+^2+(z_1-z_2)_+^2+(z_2-z_0)_+^2]/6.
For an oriented triangle tau, f_tau denotes this same sum-of-three-edges
formula on the plane range(P_tau), with that triangle's vertex labels.
Reversing the cyclic orientation is obtained by replacing z by -z,
so the following bounds hold for either orientation.

There are two sector types, with their boundaries allowed. In a two-positive
sector, after a cyclic relabelling, let the positive successive gaps be
u=z_0-z_1>=0 and v=z_1-z_2>=0. The centered coordinates give

 ||z||^2=2(u^2+uv+v^2)/3,
 2f_triangle(z)=(u^2+v^2)/3.

In a one-positive sector put p=z_0-z_1>0 and let
s=z_2-z_1 in [0,p], so the other differences are -s and -(p-s). Then

 ||z||^2=2(p^2-ps+s^2)/3,
 2f_triangle(z)=p^2/3.

It follows in both cases, including z=0, that

 ||z||^2/3 <= 2f_triangle(z) <= 2||z||^2/3.               (18)

For a nonnegative step t define the piecewise scalar

 q_tri(t)=1-t/3,                         0<=t<=3/2;
          sqrt(1-t+t^2/3),               3/2<=t<=3;
          2t/3-1,                       t>=3.          (19)

We claim that its value is EXACTLY the maximum of
||z-t grad f_triangle(z)||/||z|| over nonzero triangle-plane vectors z.

In a two-positive sector the Hessian is the path Laplacian divided by3.
Its eigenvectors (1,0,-1) and (1,-2,1) on the centered plane have
eigenvalues1/3 and1 respectively. Writing b=(u-v)/(u+v) in [-1,1]
gives the exact squared ratio

 [3(1-t/3)^2+b^2(1-t)^2]/(3+b^2).                       (20)

Its maximum is (1-t/3)^2 for t<=3/2, because
(1-t)^2-(1-t/3)^2=4t(2t-3)/9<=0.
For t>=3/2 its maximum is the value at b^2=1,
[(1-t)^2+3(1-t/3)^2]/4=1-t+t^2/3.

In a one-positive sector the Hessian has eigenvalue2/3 in the edge
direction (1,-1,0) and eigenvalue0 in the other centered direction.
Writing b=(p-2s)/p in [-1,1] gives the exact squared ratio

 [3(1-2t/3)^2+b^2]/(3+b^2).                             (21)

Its maximum is 1-t+t^2/3 for 0<=t<=3, and (1-2t/3)^2 for t>=3.
Comparing the maxima of (20),(21), using
(1-t/3)^2-(1-t+t^2/3)=t(3-2t)/9 and
(1-2t/3)^2-(1-t+t^2/3)=t(t-3)/9, proves (19).
Each maximum is attained at b=0 or |b|=1 in its stated sector, so the
factor is sharp for one triangle. This is an exact geometric maximization
on paper, not a numerical evaluation or scan.

## 7. Why overlaps and arbitrary dimension do not escape the factor

Restrict to E_N and let the linear map J_tri send x to the tuple
(sqrt(w_tri)*P_tau x)_tau, in the orthogonal direct sum of triangle planes.
By (4) it is an isometry. Its adjoint therefore has norm1.
The triangle gradient lies in its own triangle plane, and (3) gives

 grad F_N(x)=sum_tau w_tri grad f_tau(P_tau x).

For every nonnegative t, including large oversteps, (4) therefore gives

 x-t grad F_N(x)
   =J_tri^* [ (sqrt(w_tri)*(P_tau x-t grad f_tau(P_tau x)))_tau ].

Taking squared norms, using the norm of J_tri^*, and applying (19)
SEPARATELY to each current triangle state gives

 ||x-t grad F_N(x)||^2
   <=sum_tau w_tri ||P_tau x-t grad f_tau(P_tau x)||^2
   <=q_tri(t)^2*sum_tau w_tri ||P_tau x||^2
   =q_tri(t)^2*||x||^2.                                (22)

The triangle states need not be independent and are not evolved separately.
They all come from the SAME x. Their overlaps are exactly what (4) and the
isometry of J_tri account for. The update on the left is the original
coupled update, not independent triangle GD followed by a reset.

Likewise summing (18) and using (4) yields

 ||x||^2/3 <= 2F_N(x) <= 2||x||^2/3, x in E_N.           (23)

For a general start replace x_i by x_i-x_star, with its actual constant
mean x_star from (9). Mean invariance and (22),(23) give the explicit
whole-word bound, for EVERY h>=0,

 0<=V_tri(h,x_0)<=(2/3)*product_(i<n) q_tri(h_i)^2.        (24)

This proof applies verbatim to any finite positive triangle weighting on
a Euclidean working subspace for which sum_tau w_tau P_tau=I on that
subspace, with each triangle plane contained in the subspace. Thus arbitrary
orientations and all other such exact identity decompositions are covered.
No result is asserted after changing this normalization or its hypotheses.

## 8. Uniform actual-error obstruction on the original near-critical words

Continue with (12). The step t_j=1+a*c^j appears exactly 2^(m-1-j) times
in H_m for each j=0,...,m-1. This follows by induction from the defining
concatenation, retaining the SAME enclosing parameters in every child.

For m>=8, a>3/10 and a<1/2 give

 q_tri(t_0)=(2-a)/3<17/30.

The level1 step is t_1=1+beta in [15/8,2), and the middle formula in (19)
is increasing in this interval, so q_tri(t_1)<=1/sqrt(3).
For j>=2, t_j=1+beta*c^(j-1)>3: indeed beta*c>=7lambda/8>2, using
lambda>7/3. Thus

 q_tri(t_j)=[2beta*c^(j-1)-1]/3
           <r_j:=(2beta/3)*c^(j-1), j>=2.               (25)

Every r_j>1, since r_2>=7lambda/12>1. Put D=2^(m-2). The unsquared
product in (24), before its final square, obeys

 product_(i<n) q_tri(h_i)
 = [q_tri(t_0)^2*q_tri(t_1)
      *product_(j=2)^(m-1) q_tri(t_j)^(2^(1-j))]^D
 <=[q_tri(t_0)^2*q_tri(t_1)
      *product_(j=2)^infinity r_j^(2^(1-j))]^D.           (26)

The extension increases the product because each r_j>1. The sums
sum_(j>=2)2^(1-j)=1 and sum_(j>=2)(j-1)2^(1-j)=2 give

 product_(j>=2) r_j^(2^(1-j))=(2beta/3)*c^2.

Both sums follow from the geometric series and its derivative for |z|<1,
evaluated at z=1/2; their absolute convergence justifies the logarithm of
the product. Now beta<1, c<=9lambda/8, lambda^2=3+2sqrt(2)<6, and
sqrt(3)>5/3. Therefore the bracket in (26) is strictly less than

 (17/30)^2*(1/sqrt(3))*(2/3)*(9lambda/8)^2
   =867lambda^2/(3200sqrt(3))
   <15606/16000=7803/8000<49/50.                         (27)

Substitution in (24) proves (1). The proof has used every one of the
2^m-1 physical steps and no assumption about later supports or core entry.
It holds uniformly in N and x_0; even arbitrary growth of dimension with m
cannot improve this family's normalized payoff past the bound.

## 9. Complete same-word reference comparisons, with a finite threshold

Let S_m=sum H_m. Counting the same level multiplicities gives

 1+S_m=2^m+[a/(c-2)]*(c^m-2^m).                         (28)

Here 0<a/(c-2)<1: a<sqrt(2)-1=lambda-2<c-2. Since c>2, (28) yields
1+S_m<c^m, hence 1+2S_m<2c^m. In particular the complete Huber term is
greater than 1/(2c^m). By the accepted scopes in Section2,

 K_n(H_m)>=Huber(H_m),
 F_ray_n(H_m)>=K_n(H_m).                                (29)

For the FULL complex reference take the real z=delta=1/(1+2S_m).
It lies in D_h since delta-delta^2>=0 and its imaginary part is zero.
The scalar quadratic f(x)=delta*x^2/2 realizes exactly the polynomial
data in factdfde2fa326c908bf, with unique minimizer0 and initial point1.
Its actual payoff is delta*product_(i<n)(1-delta*h_i)^2.
For nonnegative numbers b_i with sum b_i<1,
product_i(1-b_i)>=1-sum_i b_i, by induction using the nonnegative cross
term at each multiplication. Applying this to b_i=delta*h_i gives

 product_(i<n)(1-delta*h_i)>=1-delta*S_m>1/2.

Maximality of Gap_h among those precise polynomial jets therefore gives

 C_full(H_m)>=Gap_h(delta)>=delta*product_i(1-delta*h_i)^2
                  >1/[4(1+2S_m)]>1/(8c^m).             (30)

This is a lower bound inside the full domain, not replacement of that
domain by a lens, a single frequency, or a quadratic maximum.

Set rho_tri=49/50 and E_m=2^(m-1). From (1),(28)--(30), the actual ratios obey

 V_tri/Huber < (4/3)c^m*rho_tri^E_m,
 V_tri/K_n <= V_tri/Huber,
 V_tri/F_ray_n <= V_tri/Huber,
 V_tri/C_full < (16/3)c^m*rho_tri^E_m.                   (31)

The ORIGINAL all-split benchmark bound in factb98bb807d514e80d gives

 V_tri/U_n^ZJ=B_n*V_tri
     <=(2/3)(1+4n^p)*rho_tri^E_m
     <(2/3)(1+4*3^m)*rho_tri^E_m, p=log_2(3).           (32)

All these ratios tend to zero. To see this quantitatively without a
computed replay, use c<=9lambda/8<3 for m>=8 and
rho_tri^50<=exp(-1)<1/2, from log(1-u)<=-u and exp(1)>2.
At m=12, E_m=2048>40*50, so rho_tri^E_m<2^(-40).
The two exact inequalities

 (16/3)*3^12=2834352<2^22,
 (2/3)*(1+4*3^12)<2^22

then bound both (31) and (32) by 2^(-18). The upper expressions using
3^m decrease thereafter: the first successive ratio is
3rho_tri^E_m<3*2^(-40)<1, and the successive ratio of the second is smaller
than this. Thus EVERY reference ratio in (31),(32) is strictly below
2^(-18) for EVERY m>=12, uniformly over the entire declared family.

This conclusion is about an actual error upper bound of the explicit
construction, not about an O(n^-2) propagation lower certificate, and
not about an upper bound on unrestricted W_n(H_m).

## 10. Literature, proof status, and the exact remaining interface

Search stage: active_program; mode: mutation/analogy; intent: one static
correlated construction and a repair of all-directed legality. Matlas was
queried for squared one-sided graph energies, finite smooth-convex
interpolation, and positive-part energies under an isometric embedding.
The graph/embedding hits did not supply a theorem specialized to this
construction. No external mathematical theorem is a premise of Sections3--9.

The exact primary background paper downloaded and read is Adrien B. Taylor,
Julien M. Hendrickx and Francois Glineur, "Smooth Strongly Convex
Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance of First-order Methods",
arXiv:1502.05666v6, 2016 manuscript, Theorem4/Corollary1 and their proof.
The local PDF is downloads/1502.05666.pdf relative to this manuscript.
Primary URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1502.05666.
Its mu=0,L=1 specialization requires every ordered smooth-convex row;
its conjugation proof explains sufficiency of the complete row system.
Here the paper supplies context only: accepted1252b8823bb5a290 already
provides the needed interpolation scope, and (8) is derived directly.
The default Python interpreter has no pypdf module, so the exact PDF was
read through the web tool's extracted text; no parser was installed.

The direct-proving plan8104b1717c5560e4 is screened as follows:

* Static function, every directed row, physical recursion and true radius:
  complete joint derivation, Sections3--4.
* Genuine correlated/noncommuting early dynamics and first remote cycle:
  complete joint derivation, Section5.
* Dimension-uniform actual terminal estimate and SAME-word references:
  complete joint family obstruction, Sections6--9.
* Positive benchmark-reaching witness: refuted for the entire declared
  normalization and triangle-identity family, by (1),(31),(32).
* Original every-word/every-horizon target: OPEN. No target fact is supplied.

No claim in this document has been accepted by the verifier. The appropriate
shared-store status is proof_attempt/counterexample/dead_end, not fact.
Any downstream formal use requires a separately authorized fact_submit.

The target interface remains a single static convex smooth function with
all original rows, attained minimum, true paid radius, and terminal payoff
at the original benchmark scale. A repair must change the chronological
radial mechanism: increasing N, changing triangle orientations, choosing a
different initial point, or selecting another positive triangle identity
decomposition retains (22) and cannot work. Candidates with changed local
curvature/nonlinearity or with a geometry outside that identity decomposition
are not excluded. No such repaired scalable witness is proved here; even a
successful original-H_m witness would still need an independent extension
to every word and horizon to resolve the original goal.

Administrative notes: initial broad retrieval outputs reported truncation;
the required accepted scopes and latest guidance were reread in bounded
sections before the proof. One wording-only patch failed with
"apply_patch verification failed: Failed to find expected lines"; it was
reapplied in smaller sections and the saved file was reread.
All artifact paths for this round are absolute
project-public paths. The outer loop owns completion counts and process
exit; this manuscript is not runtime completion evidence and renews no round.

# Bounded formula discovery, owned by main

The persistent Goal authorizes this fresh small discovery batch. It is
separate from every closed historical grant and from the native analytical
allocation, whose mathematical-program allowance remains zero. All model
calls remain Astra/ultra; no model call is made by this numerical batch.

Purpose: screen one explicit midpoint initialization for the correlated
strictly damped Gray dictionary. The program evaluates the frozen formulas,
all prescribed projection-normal rows and the distance to the explicitly
known minimizer. It does NOT solve a projection/PEP/optimization problem,
formally verify a claim, or certify an asymptotic result.

Limits:12 charged child jobs,5 external wall-clock seconds per job,60
reserved/accounted seconds total. The intended batch is2 calibrations and8
discovery cases. The last2 slots are reserved only for one documented code
or backend repair with calibration, or one explicit reconstruction; they
are not additional exploratory grid points. Every failure and timeout is
charged BEFORE execution. No solver, benchmark DP, package installation,
network math service, verifier call or fact submission is authorized.

All case parameters and source are frozen before the first mathematical
import. The public existing numerical Python runtime is reused as software;
no worker-private memory is read. Each external child is terminated after
5 seconds. All jobs are sequential. Numeric output is explicitly evidence
for discovery only; raw signed residuals and arrays are retained without
PSD repair, clipping or omitted rows. Unused reserve is retired at closure.

The finite probes m6 and m8 are OUTSIDE the claimed m>=1536 target domain.
They may reveal algebraic failure patterns; they prove neither feasibility
nor impossibility in the original asymptotic problem. A positive result
still requires a complete exact, uniform proof and independent attack.
Original target OPEN. The worker and native TASK limits are unchanged.

# Closed midpoint formula discovery: no legal candidate found

**Floating-point discovery only; not a proof or an accepted fact.**
The tested m6/m8 words are outside the claimed asymptotic m>=1536 domain.
This report excludes none of that whole domain. Original target OPEN.

The frozen midpoint initialization was evaluated without a solver. All
dictionary coordinates, original positions, all vertex-normal rows, the
zero-vertex rows, and the actual final prescribed jet were retained.
The chosen point(0,-R) is a true polar minimizer; the displayed distance
is to that selected point, not an asserted closest radius for invalid data.

Both calibrations passed. The explicitly legal constant-maximum-ray member
returned its analytic Huber ratio D*E=0.9999999999999998. The undamped
correlated first-root test reproduced the analytically positive remote
normal, with its original orientation. All ten child processes exited0.

Every one of the eight discovery cases failed the normal screen. They
also had negative FORMAL terminal values; those numbers are not function
values of an actual legal trajectory and are not negative W values.

|Case|Largest off-vertex normal|Its original pair|Formal terminal value|
|---|---:|---|---:|
|m6,R2,theta*m0,delta1|23.6705243890|(63,0)|-22.4950511677|
|m6,R2,theta*m0.5,delta1|279.6365741192|(63,0)|-248.3585901116|
|m6,R2,theta*m1,delta4|47.3092358221|(63,16)|-39.7419399888|
|m6,R4,theta*m0.5,delta4|18.9682533056|(63,16)|-10.0259700446|
|m8,R2,theta*m0,delta1|159.2373547103|(255,0)|-138.6435842139|
|m8,R2,theta*m0.5,delta1|26952.2887379064|(255,64)|-25568.0689654315|
|m8,R2,theta*m1,delta4|9203.9121494718|(255,64)|-8438.9772330931|
|m8,R4,theta*m0.5,delta4|2939.3410670443|(255,0)|-2335.3807260469|

Both low-level damping parameters equal the listed delta in these probes.
Remainder weight zeta=1 throughout. The exact frozen cases are in cases.json;
all raw signed arrays and scalar records are in raw/, without repairs or
suppressed rows. Reconstruction errors ranged at roundoff scale; each
failure exceeded its recorded tolerance by many orders of magnitude.

The next analytical question is the initial value choice. Midpoint adjacent
decrements are not forced by the original rows. The lower permitted adjacent
decrements maximize the terminal value under the fixed vertical score sum;
that observation needs its own exact derivation and full remote/zero-row
analysis. The present data cannot certify that alternative or exclude it.

Resources: fresh main-owned grant,12 jobs/60 accounted-second ceiling;
10 jobs consumed (2 calibrations,8 discovery),50 ACCOUNTED seconds,
6.612908944953233 ACTUAL external child seconds. The unused2 jobs and10
accounted seconds were retired. Ledger status closed_completed, all remaining
allowances0. Solver calls0, benchmark DP0, verifier calls0, installations0.
The separate Danus and native analytical task permissions were unchanged.

# max62 final analytical handoff

The allocated round is complete at the level of a joint analytical draft.
**UNVERIFIED; no new fact. Original target OPEN.** The outer loop records
completion after this turn returns; no process-exit claim or new round is
inferred.

Complete evidence: `89e46fc6f580a48b`, public `mixed_history.md`.
Scoped counterexample: `ed82149d6fbc7127`.
Scoped dead end: `50863ee6330e61d0`.
The manuscript and full shared evidence match byte for byte:
33348 bytes, SHA256
`fc8869d454c19c765deb4e167c653b29ee7360f6a7bed6cce6d2fec7fbbdb7a0`.
`shared_receipt.json` records saved statuses and this administrative check.
That check is not mathematical verification.

The unchanged actual physical word is

    h=(alpha_2,alpha_1,2,alpha_2,alpha_1,1+d/a,
                                        alpha_2,alpha_1,2).

The manuscript defines every parameter before the run. It has epsilon1/2,
an explicit nonempty exact domain of r near1, and an explicit finite bound
D_all with d=1+2D_all. There is one static maximum of ten planes in R^4,
including three complete finite arc dictionaries, and one Moreau envelope.
Its least gradient Lipschitz constant is exactly1.

The three ordinary blocks all have changing gradients. The third block's
actual face mixes the first-stage cap p=c*u0 and the third-stage cap q=d*u2,
with positive proximal heights. If v=p-q and
eta_star=d(d-cr^2)/||v||^2, then eta_star>1. The actual two ordinary steps
keep their weights inside(0,1), but the third checkpoint2 would require
eta_next=2eta_star-eta_2>1. Clamping to p is also incorrect. The sole
correction keeps the entire function and word fixed and identifies the
actual terminal gradient as the retained second-stage endpoint a*w1.

For zeta=(1-alpha2)(1-alpha1), the exact terminal data are

    x9=C1+d*(-epsilon*u1-zeta*u2)+R(d),
    g9=a*w1,
    F(x9)=a*d*[zeta*(1+epsilon*r)-epsilon*(epsilon+r)]
                           +a<w1,R(d)>-a^2*omega^2/2.

The remainder R(d) has an explicit bound independent of d. All old and
future planes are checked at every block. Every ordered interpolation row
follows from the same global proximal subgradient identity. The full
minimum polyhedron is given, and the exact closest initial distance is R0,
with0 the unique closest minimizer. The normalized payoff is exactly
2F(x9)/R0^2, preserving W=2R.

A small initial mixing coefficient is not uniformly harmless. For fixed r,
eta0=O(1/d), whereas after two unchanged ordinary steps eta2 tends1-zeta.
The position error from pretending the gradient stayed q is order d.
Along r tending1 and the stated d choice, eta2 tends71/216 and that error
divided by d tends(7/216)e0. The sufficient amplitude and its physical
checkpoint grow as1/sqrt(1-r^2); this cost is expressly charged.

For k stored stages, the exact full-history state has3k+1 planes in
dimensionk+1. The true slope Gram K and all evaluation/slack coordinates
remain. The transfer is z_next=z-hKw with complete active-weight equations;
inside a fixed face every slack changes by

    sigma_mu_next=sigma_mu+h*(<a_mu,g_perp>-||g_perp||^2).

This provides exact bookkeeping, not a closed arbitrary-stage cone.
The missing inequality is nonnegativity of every such old/future slack
and every next weight, with an explicitly proved face-change rule and
useful payoff. The two-plane version is refuted by the example. Larger
history cones, a useful arbitrary-stage construction and the original
all-word/all-horizon comparison at B_n scale remain open. No H_(m,tau)
specialization, all-word lower bound or minimax upper bound is claimed.

Accepted fact5b7ff4253c38b90a remains a scoped original-ruler lower witness.
It is not promoted to the project's answer. All new statements above
belong to the unverified joint draft and cannot serve as accepted facts.

# Actual mixed history through three checkpoints, and a failed two-plane state

Status: complete JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft, worker max62. There is
no new accepted fact and no formal submission. This is the one analytical
round allocated by TASK.md and master_guidance1886f8b90c7bdbb9, after
completed61, with completed ceiling62 and attempted ceiling63. The original
all-horizon/all-word exact-profile problem remains OPEN.

For a nonnegative length-n physical word h, W_n(h) is the supremum of
2*(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite dimensions,
convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a
minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under
x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t). This is the original W=2R convention.

The construction below keeps one static maximum of ten affine planes in
R^4, takes its ONE Moreau envelope with parameter1, and executes the fixed
nine-step word

    (alpha_2,alpha_1,2,alpha_2,alpha_1,1+d/a,
                                      alpha_2,alpha_1,2).          (1)

All three ordinary blocks have changing actual gradients. The third block
has positive proximal heights and mixed gradients. At its final checkpoint
the two-plane mixture formula leaves its simplex. The actual terminal
gradient is the previously visited second-stage endpoint. Thus even
clamping the mixture to its old cap is incorrect. The one correction is
to include that retained old endpoint; neither the function nor any step
in (1) changes.

This is a three-checkpoint continuation of the actual six-step calibration,
not three traversals of freshly prescribed pure arcs. A complete third
arc is present from the start, but its two non-cap planes do not become
active on this trajectory. That failure to progress into the new arc is
part of the result, not an omitted part of the word.

The arbitrary-count calculation in Section8 gives exact all-plane state
and slack transport, with its dimension and Euclidean metric. It is NOT
an unbounded-stage payoff theorem. The explicit three-checkpoint example
refutes the particular two-plane invariant state tested here; it does not
refute all states with additional history. Section9 states the remaining
inequality without advertising it as a proved or negligible interface.

The awareness records2259fcba177f68d1 and f37ec6e9db6c6b5b were read in
full. Their mathematics is rederived below where needed, rather than
used as an accepted predecessor. The accepted original-ruler fact
5b7ff4253c38b90a proves a lower witness for its specified H_m family only.
It is not an all-word result and supplies no premise for this new geometry.
Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the project convention for Q_ij; its
envelope/interpolation calculation is also proved here for this function.

## 1. Euclidean frame and exact local data

Set epsilon=1/2. Let e_0,e_1,e_2,e_3 be orthonormal, 0<r<1,
s=sqrt(1-r^2), u_0=e_0, and

    u_(i+1)=-r*u_i+s*e_(i+1), i=0,1,2.

Then ||u_i||=1 and <u_i,u_j>=(-r)^|i-j|: the new orthogonal
coordinate proves the norm identity, and repeated substitution proves
the cross products. These are actual vectors, not orthogonal coefficient
coordinates. In particular <u_0,u_2>=r^2.

Define the local quantities

    kappa=2*s/r, omega=sqrt(1+epsilon^2+2*epsilon*r),
    theta=arctan(s/(epsilon+r)), c=omega*exp(kappa*theta),
    Delta=theta/2,
    E_i(t)=cos(t)*u_i-sin(t)*e_(i+1),
    T_i(t)=-sin(t)*u_i-cos(t)*e_(i+1),
    V_i(t)=c*exp(-kappa*t)*E_i(t),
    N_i(t)=E_i(t)+kappa*T_i(t).

The curve endpoints are c*u_i and w_i=epsilon*u_i-u_(i+1).
For z,t in [0,theta], writing v=z-t gives

    <N_i(t),V_i(z)>/[c*exp(-kappa*t)]
       =exp(-kappa*v)*(cos(v)+kappa*sin(v)).

Its derivative in v is
-(1+kappa^2)*exp(-kappa*v)*sin(v); its maximum is1 at0.
Thus N_i(t) supports the whole arc and the slope0 at V_i(t).
At the cap t=0, e_(i+1) is another supporting normal. Consequently
every vector X*u_i+Y*e_(i+1) with X>=0 and Y+kappa*X>=0 supports
the cap. These statements also hold for any finite subcollection of
the arc.

Use the following exact two-step local backward path:

    A_D=exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos(Delta)+sin(Delta)/kappa),
    B_D=exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin(Delta)/kappa,
    t_0=0, t_1=B_D, t_2=B_D*(1+A_D),
    theta_l=theta-l*Delta, R_l=c*exp(-kappa*theta_l),
    v_(i,l)=R_l*E_i(theta_l),
    r_(i,l)=R_l*((1+t_l)*E_i(theta_l)
                              +kappa*t_l*T_i(theta_l)),
    alpha_l=1-exp(-kappa*Delta)*cos(Delta)+B_D
                         +(1+kappa^2)*B_D*t_(l-1), l=1,2.        (2)

Resolving radial and tangent coordinates at theta_l gives

    r_(i,l)-r_(i,l-1)=alpha_l*v_(i,l).                          (3)

For example the tangent cancellation is t_l=B_D+A_D*t_(l-1),
and the radial component gives exactly alpha_l in (2). Both alpha_l
are positive. The proximal displacement
r_(i,l)-v_(i,l)=R_l*t_l*N_i(theta_l) is a supporting normal, so the
local chronological block is (alpha_2,alpha_1), with gradients
v_(i,2),v_(i,1),w_i and local envelope values

    R_l^2*(t_l+1/2), with proximal heights R_l^2*t_l.          (4)

Write

    r_(i,2)=A*u_i-B*u_(i+1),
    B=c*kappa*t_2/s, A=c*(1+t_2)-r*B,
    Z=A+r*B=c*(1+t_2),
    R0^2=c^2*((1+t_2)^2+kappa^2*t_2^2),
    ell=1+epsilon*r, beta=ell/(epsilon+r),
    M=A+beta*B=Z+s^2*B/(epsilon+r), a=ell/M.                  (5)

Equation(3) expresses r_(i,2) as w_i plus positive multiples of
the two earlier arc slopes. Writing V_i(t)=p*u_i-q*u_(i+1) gives
p>=epsilon*q>=0, so A>=epsilon, B>=1 and A>=epsilon*B.
All local arc pairs have positive inner product; backward additions
in(3) show ||r_(i,l)||<=R0.

The following explicit admissibility conditions are imposed on r:

    9/10<=r<1,
    1/7<alpha_1<1/5, 1/6<alpha_2<2/9.                       (6)

This is a nonempty parameter domain, defined by the exact formulas(2).
Indeed as r tends1, theta/s tends2/3, kappa/s tends2, and

    B_D -> 1/6, A_D -> 7/6, t_2 -> 13/36,
    alpha_1 -> 1/6, alpha_2 -> 7/36.                         (7)

These follow from sin z/z->1 and exp z->1, without any numerical
evaluation. All limiting alpha inequalities in(6) are strict. Thus all
r sufficiently close1 satisfy(6). No particular decimal r is asserted
to have passed an executed check.

Set

    S=alpha_1+alpha_2, zeta=(1-alpha_2)*(1-alpha_1),
    X= zeta*r-epsilon,
    D_end=2*epsilon^2+3*epsilon*r-zeta*(2+epsilon*r),
    L_end=zeta*ell-epsilon*(epsilon+r).                      (8)

The rational bounds in(6) imply

    28/45<zeta<5/7, X>3/50,
    D_end>37/20-25/14=9/140>0, L_end>0.                     (9)

For the last assertion, ell-r*(epsilon+r)=s^2>0 and zeta*r>
epsilon imply zeta*ell>epsilon*(epsilon+r).

## 2. Fully specified amplitude and one static function

The amplitude restrictions are explicit and are fixed before the run.
They will also allow a uniform bound on the bounded remainders below.
Let

    C_1=-w_0-a*r_(1,2), C_bd=omega+a*R0,
    Hpref=omega+(1+2*a)*(R0+c),
    V_0=c*(r*(a*Z-1)-epsilon),
    E_bd=4*c*epsilon*r+2*abs(V_0)/c,
    Y_bd=C_bd+(3/2)*E_bd+S*c,
    R_bd=3*E_bd+(S+1)*c,
    T_bd=R_bd+a*omega.

Define D_base to be the maximum of

    2*c, 8*c*r*epsilon, sqrt(8*c*r*a*Z), c*r/epsilon,
    [1+2*(epsilon+c)/r]/epsilon,
    [Hpref+r*ell]/(epsilon*r).

Define D_all as the maximum of D_base and the following positive or
nonnegative quantities:

    c/r^2,
    2*abs(V_0)/(c*epsilon*r),
    2*(4*c+E_bd)/zeta,
    4*S*c/(epsilon*r),
    Y_bd/(epsilon*r),
    sqrt(1+kappa^2)*Y_bd/(epsilon*s),
    c*((3/2)*E_bd+S*c)/(epsilon*(epsilon+r)),
    c*sqrt(1+kappa^2)*r*T_bd/(s*D_end),
    omega*T_bd/L_end,
    c*(C_bd+T_bd)/(X*r*(epsilon+r)),
    T_bd/zeta.                                             (10)

All denominators are strictly positive by(6),(9). For example take

    d=1+2*D_all.                                           (11)

Every assertion below holds for ANY d>D_all, including(11). There
is no later amendment to d, alpha_1, alpha_2 or any step after an
active set fails. The actual physical word is exactly(1).

Define x_6=C_1-d*w_1 and C_T=x_6-d*u_2=C_1-epsilon*d*u_1.
For any center C and scale b>0 let

    Phi_(i,b,C)(y)=max{0,b*<v_(i,l),y-C>:l=0,1,2}.

Fix before the first step the SINGLE function

    J(y)=max{Phi_(0,1,0)(y),Phi_(1,a,C_1)(y),
                                      Phi_(2,d/c,C_T)(y)},
    F(x)=min_y[J(y)+||x-y||^2/2].                           (12)

There are nine nonzero planes and the plane0. The third-stage cap
is q=d*u_2, with affine plane <q,y-C_T>. It is exactly the terminal
plane of the earlier six-step calibration. Its two additional arc
planes are present throughout this run. There is no sum of local
envelopes and no overlap-based change to the physical steps.

## 3. The first six steps, including every future plane

Their positions and gradients are

    x_0=r_(0,2), x_1=r_(0,1), x_2=w_0,
    x_3=-w_0=C_1+a*r_(1,2),
    x_4=C_1+a*r_(1,1), x_5=C_1+a*w_1,
    x_6=C_1-d*w_1,
    (g_0,...,g_5)=(v_(0,2),v_(0,1),w_0,
                                      a*v_(1,2),a*v_(1,1),a*w_1). (13)

Equation(3) and the steps2,1+d/a prove every GD update in(13).
The corresponding values and proximal heights are(4), multiplied
by1 for stage0 and by a^2 for stage1. Here is a joint check that
these are jets of the full function(12).

An arc slope written p*u_i-q*u_(i+1) has p>=epsilon*q>=0 and

    (r*p+q)/(p+r*q)<=beta.

For a stage1 slope v=p*u_1-q*u_2 one gets

    <v,-C_1>=-ell*(p+r*q)
                         +a*((p+r*q)*A+(r*p+q)*B)<=0,          (14)

because a*M=ell. Moreover <u_1,N_0(t)><0 on[0,theta]: direct
substitution gives
-r*cos t-s*sin t+kappa*(r*sin t-s*cos t)<0 since
tan t<=s/(epsilon+r)<s/r. Thus every stage1 plane is nonpositive
at every first-block proximal point, and at0.

For an old slope v=p*u_0-q*u_1, the value at the stage1 center is

    <v,C_1>=-epsilon*(p+r*q)-(r*p+q)+a*(r*p+q)*Z.            (15)

The ratio (p+r*q)/(r*p+q) is at least(epsilon+r)/ell, while
a*Z<ell and omega^2-ell^2=epsilon^2*s^2>0. These facts imply(15)<0.
At an earlier stage1 proximal point the extra term equals

    -a*(r*p+q)*R_l*t_l*(cos(theta_l)-kappa*sin(theta_l))<=0.

Indeed kappa*tan theta<=1 follows from r>=9/10>
sqrt(2/3). This checks all old stage0 planes there.

All six prefix proximal points have norm at most Hpref. Also

    <u_2,C_T>=-r*ell+a*(r*A+B)+epsilon*r*d>Hpref             (16)

by D_base. They have zero e_3 coordinate. Every stage2 plane
evaluates to

    (d/c)*R_l*cos(theta_l)*<u_2,y-C_T><0.                  (17)

Thus BOTH newly added future planes, as well as the stage2 cap,
are inactive throughout the prefix and at0. Since the appropriate
local slope is a subgradient of J at each stated proximal point,
(13),(4) are the actual prefix data. In particular J(0)=0 and
min J=0. No queried value has been changed.

## 4. Exact mixed gradients through the third ordinary block

Put

    p=c*u_0, q=d*u_2, v=p-q,
    D=||v||^2=c^2+d^2-2*c*d*r^2,
    V=c*epsilon*r*d+V_0,
    eta_0=V/D,
    eta_star=d*(d-c*r^2)/D,
    g_bar=q+eta_star*v,
    xi=eta_0-eta_star.                                   (18)

One has <v,g_bar>=0. Also g_bar is the shortest vector on the
whole affine line through p and q, so ||g_bar||<=c. Do not impose
a simplex constraint on eta_star: our choice d>c/r^2 implies

    eta_star-1=c*(d*r^2-c)/D>0.                           (19)

Define for j=0,1,2

    P_0=1, P_1=1-alpha_2, P_2=zeta,
    S_0=0, S_1=alpha_2, S_2=S,
    eta_j=eta_star+xi*P_j,
    g_(6+j)=g_bar+xi*P_j*v,
    y_(6+j)=C_T+eta_0*(q-p)-S_j*g_bar,
    x_(6+j)=x_6-S_j*g_bar-xi*(1-P_j)*v.                   (20)

These formulas are actual jets, not just a formal face recurrence.
We prove the proximal conditions against the entire dictionary.

First D>(d-c)^2>d^2/4. By(10), V>0 and

    0<eta_0*d<=E_bd,
    0<eta_star-1<4*c/d.

Hence

    0<eta_j<1-P_j+(4*c+E_bd)/d<1-zeta/2<1                (21)

for j=0,1,2, with the first upper bound also valid for j=0.
In particular xi<0. These inequalities use the bound on d in(10),
not the illegal assumption eta_star<=1.

The p-plane and q-plane tie at y_6: their difference at C_T is
<p,C_T>=V, and its change along eta_0*(q-p) is -eta_0*D.
They continue to tie at every y_(6+j), since <v,g_bar>=0.

Every such y has the representation

    y=-epsilon*d*u_1+E_y, ||E_y||<=Y_bd.                  (22)

Here ||C_1||<=C_bd, ||eta_0*(q-p)||<=(3/2)*E_bd and
||S_j*g_bar||<=S*c. In its (u_0,e_1) coordinates, (22) has
positive first coordinate and

    <e_1,y>+kappa*<u_0,y>
       >=epsilon*s*d-sqrt(1+kappa^2)*Y_bd>0.             (23)

The cap-support criterion of Section1 proves that p dominates
every other stage0 plane. Its value is strictly positive.

Every stage1 slope has u_1 component R_l*cos(theta_l)>=epsilon+r
and norm at most c. Therefore its unscaled evaluation at y-C_1 is
at most

    -epsilon*d*(epsilon+r)
                            +c*((3/2)*E_bd+S*c)<0.      (24)

For stage2, all e_3 coordinates are zero. Its radial coordinate is

    <u_2,y-C_T>
      =[V*(d-c*r^2)-S_j*c^2*d*(1-r^4)]/D>0.            (25)

Indeed V>=c*epsilon*r*d/2 and d-c*r^2>=d/2; the negative
term is at most S*c^2*d, and(10) makes the numerator positive.
At a positive pure radial normal the cap uniquely dominates the
two other arc slopes: R_l*cos(theta_l)<c for l=0,1.

Thus the p-plane and q-plane are exactly the active nonzero planes,
above0. Their convex combination in(20),(21) belongs to partial J(y),
and x-y=g. This proves all the proximal identities. It also proves
the two GD updates with the UNCHANGED ordinary steps alpha_2,alpha_1,
because 1-P_(j+1)=1-P_j+alpha_(2-j)*P_j.

The exact positive heights and values are

    H_(6+j)=<p,y_(6+j)>=V+eta_0*<p,q-p>-S_j*||g_bar||^2,
    F(x_(6+j))=H_(6+j)+||g_(6+j)||^2/2.                 (26)

The equality <p,g_bar>=||g_bar||^2 follows from p-g_bar being
parallel to v. These heights are positive by(22)-(23). At j=0
one may also write F(x_6)=d^2/2+V^2/(2D), by expanding(26).

Since 0<P_2<P_1<P_0, xi<0 and v!=0, the three gradients in(20)
are distinct. Their directions differ as well: g_bar is nonzero
and perpendicular to v because p,q are independent for r<1.
Thus the third ordinary block really has changing ordinary gradients.

## 5. The exact failed face test and its one correction

The third checkpoint is the step2 from x_8. The actual resulting
point, using the already proved g_8, is

    x_9=x_8-2*g_8
       =C_1+d*D_vec+R(d),
    D_vec=-epsilon*u_1-zeta*u_2,
    R(d)=(1+zeta)*eta_0*(q-p)+(zeta-S-1)*g_bar,
    ||R(d)||<=R_bd.                                      (27)

The norm bound follows from(21), ||eta_0(q-p)||<=(3/2)E_bd,
||g_bar||<=c, zeta<1, and |zeta-S-1|<=S+1.

If the two-plane face were invariant at this checkpoint, its next
coefficient would have to be

    eta_9^face=2*eta_star-eta_2>1.                         (28)

The inequality is strict by(19),(21). This is the FIRST failed
full-face consistency test: the coefficient of q would be negative.
No reassignment of values changes this convex-combination defect for
this fixed function and these two slopes.

Clamping to p is not the answer either. At the proposed point x_9-p,
the leading p-plane value is d*<p,D_vec>=-d*c*r*X<0,
whereas the leading stage1 endpoint-plane value is d*a*L_end>0.
All remainder terms are bounded independently of d. More directly,
the complete check immediately below proves that the actual gradient
is a*w_1, which is not on the segment[p,q]. The latter segment has
strictly positive u_0 component, while a*w_1 has u_0 component
-a*r*(epsilon+r)<0.

The ONE correction retains the whole J and word(1) and uses

    g_9=a*w_1, y_9=x_9-a*w_1.                            (29)

Here is the check against every plane, including the future ones.
In the orthonormal (u_1,e_2) frame,

    D_vec=X*u_1-zeta*s*e_2, X=zeta*r-epsilon>0.

For the continuous stage1 arc, its support value on D_vec is

    f_arc(t)=c*exp(-kappa*t)*(X*cos t+zeta*s*sin t).

Its derivative is c*exp(-kappa*t) times

    (zeta*s-kappa*X)*cos t-(X+kappa*zeta*s)*sin t.

At t=theta this bracket divided by cos(theta) is exactly
s*D_end/[r*(epsilon+r)]>0. Since X+kappa*zeta*s>0, the
derivative is positive throughout[0,theta]. Quantitatively,

    f_arc'(t)>=s*D_end/r,
    <w_1-v_(1,l),D_vec>>=(theta-theta_l)*s*D_end/r,
    ||w_1-v_(1,l)||<=c*sqrt(1+kappa^2)*(theta-theta_l)
                                                        (l=1,2). (30)

The first bound uses R(t)>=omega and cos t>=cos theta=(epsilon+r)/omega.
The norm bound integrates ||V_1'(t)||<=c*sqrt(1+kappa^2).
As ||R(d)-a*w_1||<=T_bd, the corresponding condition in(10)
and(30) prove that w_1 strictly dominates the other two stage1
slopes at y_9-C_1. Its value is positive because

    <w_1,y_9-C_1>>=d*L_end-omega*T_bd>0.                (31)

Every stage0 slope has

    <v_(0,l),D_vec>
      =-R_l*X*(r*cos(theta_l)+s*sin(theta_l))
      <=-X*r*(epsilon+r)<0.                            (32)

Its evaluation at y_9 is consequently at most
-d*X*r*(epsilon+r)+c*(C_bd+T_bd)<0 by(10).

Finally

    y_9-C_T=-zeta*d*u_2+R(d)-a*w_1.

It has zero e_3 coordinate and negative u_2 coordinate, by d>T_bd/zeta.
Every stage2 plane is therefore negative, exactly as in(17).
Equations(30)-(32) show that the stage1 endpoint is the unique active
plane at y_9. Its slope is(29), and x_9-y_9=g_9. This proves the
corrected terminal jet for the ORIGINAL nine-step word.

The exact terminal height and value are

    H_9=a*d*L_end+a*<w_1,R(d)>-a^2*omega^2>0,
    F(x_9)=a*d*L_end+a*<w_1,R(d)>-a^2*omega^2/2.          (33)

This is a return to the actual gradient queried earlier at x_5,
at a new point with a positive proximal height. It cannot be
represented as a fresh zero-height stage reset.

## 6. Smoothness, every original row, and the closest distance

J is a finite convex nonnegative maximum with J(0)=0. The objective
J(y)+||x-y||^2/2 is coercive and strictly convex, hence has a unique
minimizer y. Completing the square in the subgradient inequality
proves that its optimality condition is x-y in partial J(y); the
converse follows by a directional difference quotient.

Write g=x-y. For two such pairs, summing the two subgradient
inequalities gives

    ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>.

Also testing y as a trial point at x+z gives
F(x+z)<=F(x)+<g,z>+||z||^2/2, while the subgradient inequality
gives the opposite bound F(x+z)>=F(x)+<g,z>. Thus F is finite,
convex, differentiable and has1-Lipschitz gradient g.
Its minimum is0 and its minimum set is exactly

    C_min={z:<v_(0,l),z><=0,
               <v_(1,l),z-C_1><=0,
               <v_(2,l),z-C_T><=0, l=0,1,2}.            (34)

The positive scales do not affect these inequalities. This is the
whole nonempty polyhedron, not a selected minimizer only. The least
global gradient Lipschitz constant is1: both0 and p are active at
the proximal point0, so every x=t*p,0<=t<=1, has proximal point0
and gradient x. Two distinct such inputs give Lipschitz ratio1.

For every actual query, terminal point and any minimizer write
x_i=y_i+g_i, f_i=H_i+||g_i||^2/2, H_i=J(y_i). The EXACT row identity is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
         =H_i-H_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i><=0.                    (35)

The final inequality is the subgradient inequality for J. Thus
ALL original directed interpolation rows, remote in either direction,
hold. The Euclidean Gram is positive semidefinite because all the
points and gradients are actual vectors in R^4. No sign-changing
coefficient frame is substituted for that metric.

Equation(3) gives x_0=w_0+alpha_1*v_(0,1)+alpha_2*v_(0,2).
For every z in C_min each of these three slope evaluations is
nonpositive, so <x_0,z><=0. Since0 belongs to C_min,

    ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2+||z||^2,
    dist(x_0,C_min)^2=||x_0||^2=R0^2.                   (36)

In particular0 is the unique closest minimizer even in the enlarged
R^4 function. The finite lower witness, in the required W=2R
normalization, is therefore

    W_9(h)>=
       [2*a*d*L_end+2*a*<w_1,R(d)>-a^2*omega^2]/R0^2.  (37)

For arbitrary L>0 the function L*F and steps h_t/L have the same
trajectory, minimum set, closest radius and normalized payoff.
This is a statement about(1), not about the original ruler word or
the unrestricted minimax value.

## 7. Size of the physical correction and the paid parameter regime

For fixed admissible r as d tends infinity, every threshold in(10)
stays fixed and

    eta_0=O(1/d), eta_star ->1,
    eta_1 ->alpha_2, eta_2 ->1-zeta,
    x_9/d ->D_vec,
    F(x_9)/d ->a*L_end.                                (38)

Although the first remote mixture coefficient tends to0, after only
two unchanged ordinary steps its coefficient tends to1-zeta, which
is bounded away from0. For comparison only, let x_8^pure=x_6-S*q
be the point obtained by incorrectly retaining the pure gradient q.
Equations(20) give

    (x_8-x_8^pure)/d ->alpha_1*alpha_2*u_2.             (39)

Thus the error is order d, not an infinitesimal local error. It is
created during two bounded ordinary steps, before a new large checkpoint.

As r tends1, the exact local limits are

    c,omega ->3/2, a ->36/49, R0^2 ->2401/576,
    S ->13/36, zeta ->145/216,
    X ->37/216, D_end ->139/432,
    L_end ->37/144.                                    (40)

The D_end value follows from2-(5/2)*(145/216)=139/432.
All quantities entering(10) remain bounded except its displayed
1/s factors. Their numerators and positive non-s denominators have
finite limits, and the numerator of the cap-normal bound stays
positive. Consequently D_all is of order1/s, and choosing(11)
charges d of order1/s and the physical checkpoint1+d/a of order1/s.
This is a sufficient threshold for this proof, not a lower bound on
the amplitudes required by every construction.

Along(11) as r tends1, the bounded remainders in(27) remain uniformly
bounded, and (38)-(40) yield

    eta_2 ->71/216,
    x_9/d ->-(37/216)*e_0,
    F(x_9)/d ->37/196,
    [2*F(x_9)/R0^2]/d ->10656/117649.                   (41)

Also(39) tends(7/216)*e_0. These are symbolic limits, not executed
recurrences or numerical observations. A growing d changes the
explicit family of words(1); no comparison between different d's is
presented as a correction on the same physical word. Each finite
word is fixed before its gradients are computed.

There is no B_n-scale conclusion here: n remains9. Increasing d or
letting r tend1 at this fixed horizon cannot substitute for an
unbounded-horizon, all-word comparison.

## 8. Exact full-history state and its actual cost

This section records an algebraic state for a fixed arbitrary finite
dictionary. It is included to make the missing closure precise, not
to claim that defining a proximal optimization solves the problem.

Let there be k complete finite arc stages with their centers and
scales fixed before the run. There are N=3k+1 affine planes including
the plane0, in ambient dimension k+1. Write each plane as

    L_mu(y)=<a_mu,y>+b_mu, mu=0,...,N-1,
    a_0=0, b_0=0,
    K_munu=<a_mu,a_nu>, z_mu=<a_mu,x>+b_mu.              (42)

K is the ACTUAL slope Gram. If a slope of stage i is
A_i*u_i-B_i*u_(i+1) and one of stage j>i is
A_j*u_j-B_j*u_(j+1), its inner product is exactly

    -(-r)^(j-i-1)*(r*A_i+B_i)*(A_j+r*B_j).             (43)

Within a stage the orthonormal(u_i,e_(i+1)) formula applies.
Equivalently the vector synthesized from a coefficient column t has
squared norm sum t_i^2+2 sum_(i<j)(-r)^(j-i)t_i*t_j. It is not ||t||_2^2.
The direction Gram has eigenvalues between(1-r)/(1+r) and
(1+r)/(1-r): absolute row sums bound it above, while its inverse
quadratic form on measurements m_i=<u_i,x> is

    ||x||^2=m_0^2+sum_(i=1)^k (m_i+r*m_(i-1))^2/s^2.  (44)

Expanding(44) bounds the inverse by(1+r)/(1-r) and proves the
lower bound. Formula(44) is also an exact radius charge when such
measurements, rather than x itself, are prescribed.

At a current input x, let w be any vector of active convex weights,
and H the common proximal height. Its complete equations are

    w_mu>=0, sum_mu w_mu=1,
    z-K*w<=H*1,
    w_mu*(z_mu-(K*w)_mu-H)=0 for every mu,
    g=sum_mu w_mu*a_mu, y=x-g,
    F(x)=H+(1/2)*w^T*K*w.                              (45)

These conditions are equivalent to the proximal condition:
the inequalities state that H=J(y), the complementarity says every
weighted plane is active, and g is their convex combination.
The vector g is unique even if w is not. With a physical step h>=0,
the exact next evaluation column is

    z^+=z-h*K*w.                                      (46)

For completeness, the subgradients of a finite maximum at y are
exactly the convex hull of its active slopes. A convex combination
gives a subgradient by summing the active affine lower bounds. For
the other inclusion, if g is outside that finite compact convex hull,
its closest point a separates it strictly in direction g-a. The
directional derivative of the finite maximum is the maximum of the
active slope evaluations in that direction, smaller than the
evaluation of g. This contradicts the subgradient inequality.
This justifies the necessity of the weights in(45), as well as their
sufficiency.

There are N dynamic plane evaluations, N candidate weights and one
height. Storing an unrestricted explicit Gram requires N^2 entries;
the frame and stage coefficients compress its representation but do
not delete any inequality in(45). An affinely independent active
set has at most k+2 planes. To see that some such representation
exists, an affine dependence among positively weighted slopes permits
moving the weights along a zero-sum dependence until one weight
vanishes; repeat without changing g. This is a finite reduction in
an argument, not an executed active-set search.

For a fixed affinely independent active set A, the equations for its
weights and height are the explicit linear system

    [ K_AA  1 ][ w_A ] = [ z_A ],
    [ 1^T   0 ][  H  ]   [  1  ].                     (47)

It is invertible: in its homogeneous nullspace, multiplying the first
row by w_A^T and using 1^T*w_A=0 gives
||sum w_mu*a_mu||^2=0, so affine independence gives w_A=0 and H=0.
No matrix in(47) was formed or solved by a program in this round.

There is also a useful coordinate-free form. Let P_A be the orthogonal
projector onto the span of active slope differences, and g_perp=(I-P_A)g.
As long as the proposed next point has this same active face, equality
of its plane values and the affine combination of slopes give

    g^+=g-h*P_A*g,
    y^+=y-h*g_perp,
    H^+=H-h*||g_perp||^2.                              (48)

For the first identity, the change in g lies in the difference span,
and the active-plane equality makes the change in x-g orthogonal to
that span. For the last identity every active slope has the same
projection g_perp onto its orthogonal complement.

Every retained or future plane has slack

    sigma_mu=H-L_mu(y)>=0.

Its EXACT prospective update inside this face is

    sigma_mu^+=sigma_mu
                +h*(<a_mu,g_perp>-||g_perp||^2).        (49)

The barycentric weights from(47) must remain nonnegative, AND every
one of(49) must remain nonnegative. These are the tests; dropping
old slacks gives a different, insufficient state.

For the actual face{p,q}, P_A is projection on v, g_perp=g_bar,
and(48) is exactly(20). It passes both ordinary steps by Section4.
The physical step2 fails the weight test(28). Replacing that test
by clamping fails the old endpoint's slack as well, by Section5.
Thus(46) is an exact state description with an explicitly witnessed
change of face, not an invariant two-plane transfer theorem.

The state retains the full history. Formula(43) has alternating signs
and magnitude r^(j-i-1) times the displayed coefficients. In a regime
k*s^2->0, these weights are 1+o(1) uniformly for all separations up
to k. Indeed 1-r^j<=(j)(1-r) and 1-r=s^2/(1+r).
Their absolute sum is(1-r^k)/(1-r), of order k in that regime.
This is a count of nondecaying constraints, not a proof that their
absolute sum is an unavoidable payoff loss. Cancellations have to be
proved in the actual signed state; they cannot be assumed.

## 9. Exact scope of failure and the missing inequality

The failed candidate is the positive-height two-plane carrier cone:
keep the last cap and its one reactivated old cap, transport the
mixture and common height by(48), and hope to use the same state at
successive checkpoints. It already fails on the fixed three-checkpoint
word(1). This failure uses actual changing gradients, actual positive
heights, the actual Gram, one static global J, and all future planes.
It is distinct from the old pure-stage positive cycle.

The sole correction computes the exact terminal old endpoint(29).
It does NOT establish closure of a three-plane or larger history cone
under subsequent arbitrary-stage additions. No second repair or new
mechanism is asserted.

For any proposed active-face induction, the concrete missing row
inequality is, for EVERY omitted old or future plane,

    sigma_mu >= h*[||g_perp||^2-<a_mu,g_perp>]_+,          (50)

together with nonnegative next weights. Here [t]_+=max(t,0).
Equation(50) is just the necessary and sufficient slack condition
from(49) for that proposed unchanged face; it is not claimed to hold.
The word(1) supplies an exact failed weight test and an old-plane
failure after the simplest clamp. A useful larger state must prescribe
and prove its face changes instead of applying(50) conditionally.

For newly added stages, their offsets would also have to preserve
every earlier proximal plane inequality AND a nonempty minimum set.
Keeping0 in that set and retaining x_0 as a positive combination of
planes active at0 would retain the radius argument(36), but existence
of such offsets with useful terminal payoff is not proved here.
General radius claims using only local stage radii must instead pay
the full measurement identity(44).

If an induction introduced scale slacks lambda_i>=1 to force its
constraints, the loss in a squared multiplicative transfer would be
exactly product_i lambda_i^(-2), or logarithmic loss
2*sum_i log(lambda_i). No such slack policy is introduced in this
round, and no pointwise small slack is declared globally negligible.

Even a successful arbitrary-stage construction would still require a
uniform comparison with the original ALL-split B_n=2*S_bullet(n)+1,
for every sufficiently large physical horizon and every nonnegative
word. No such B_n comparison is present. No diagnostic H_(m,tau)
specialization is claimed or has been evaluated. W=2R throughout.

## 10. Evidence status, retrieval and resource closeout

All displayed new results are part of this JOINT UNVERIFIED draft.
They have not passed fact_submit and are not facts to use downstream.
The full target remains open; the permitted conclusion is the exact
scoped failure above and the corrected finite continuation.

Matlas was queried for affine active-set Moreau structure. Its
2012.15458 Lemma2.1 is about composite Moreau differentiation, and
its other hits2006.07222 and2005.09261 do not supply the needed history
invariant. A web fallback found general polyhedral-envelope background
(including1611.00996 and1909.05795). None is an imported theorem.
The present branch stays within the fully read finite max-affine
mechanism of2259fcba177f68d1; all required envelope and face arguments
are proved above, so no new paper-dependent branch is being rejected.

Resource use: zero mathematical programs, numerical evaluations,
executed recurrences, solvers, benchmark evaluations, installations,
fact_submit calls, consult calls, or subagents. Administrative file
reads/writes, text extraction from existing JSON, serialization and
identity checks are the only local execution. No resource allowance
is renewed. The outer loop records completion after return; this
document does not claim that the process has exited.

## 11. Local notation

All notation in this draft is local except the project W_n,B_n,Q_ij.
epsilon is fixed1/2; r,s,u_i,e_i define the correlated Euclidean frame.
kappa,omega,theta,c,Delta define its support arc; E_i,T_i,N_i,V_i
are its radial, tangent, normal and slope functions. A_D,B_D,t_l,
theta_l,R_l,v_(i,l),r_(i,l),alpha_l are the explicit ordinary block.
A,B,Z,R0,ell,beta,M,a are its coefficients, radius and adjacent scale.
S,zeta,X,D_end,L_end are the explicit third-block quantities(8).
C_1,C_T are fixed centers; C_bd,Hpref,V_0,E_bd,Y_bd,R_bd,T_bd,
D_base,D_all are the exact amplitude bounds in Section2. d is the
fixed amplitude, not a dimension or an executed optimization output.
Phi,J,F are the static finite maximum and its single envelope.
p,q,v,D,V,eta_0,eta_star,g_bar,xi,P_j,S_j,eta_j define the true mixed
face; eta_star is deliberately allowed outside[0,1]. H_i are proximal
heights, g_i,x_i,y_i actual gradients, inputs and proximal points.
D_vec,R(d) give the exact terminal decomposition. C_min is the entire
minimum polyhedron. In Section8 only, k is the number of stored stages,
N the plane count, a_mu,b_mu their slopes/intercepts, K the true Gram,
z the evaluation column, m_i the frame measurements in(44), w active
weights, A an active set, P_A its
difference-space projector, g_perp the orthogonal component, and
sigma_mu the complete plane slacks. Those Section8 symbols do not
redefine earlier locally scoped A,B,P_j. lambda_i in Section9 are
hypothetical scale loss factors, not benchmark constants.
