# Correlated Euclidean stages: a finite calibration and an exact remote cycle

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft, max61. No assertion below is a
new accepted fact. This is one frame mechanism and one explicit terminal
correction, within TASK.md and guidance42998ccb0a64c7b8; the later guidance
2915a98c60a7ec96 leaves this allocation unchanged. No mathematical program,
numerical experiment, solver, executed recurrence, formal submission,
installation, consult or sub-agent was used.

The result is a complete six-step, two-checkpoint static-function
calibration with changing gradients on BOTH incoming ordinary blocks,
followed by a value-independent three-query obstruction to the natural
repeated gluing rule. The calibration is not an all-word checkpoint lemma.
The three-query obstruction does not exclude every correlated realization.

All displayed calculations are joint proofs in this draft, not deductions
from unverified shared claims. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is used for the
project's directed-row convention and scope. We also prove the required
envelope and row identities directly. Throughout the payoff is W=2R:

    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L dist(x_0,argmin f)^2).

## 1. Actual frame, primal metric, dual metric, and support arcs

Let e_0,...,e_k be an orthonormal basis and 0<=r<1. Write
s=sqrt(1-r^2), u_0=e_0 and u_(i+1)=-r*u_i+s*e_(i+1).
Here r is the task's r_corr, not a covariance law for queried gradients.
Induction, using the new coordinate e_(i+1), gives

    ||u_i||=1, <u_i,u_j>=(-r)^|i-j|.                     (1)

Thus, for arbitrary real coefficients a_i,

    ||sum_i a_i u_i||^2
      =sum_i a_i^2+2 sum_(i<j) (-r)^(j-i) a_i a_j.      (2)

The coefficients are NOT orthonormal coordinates. If b_i=<u_i,x>,
the orthonormal coordinates of x satisfy

    <e_0,x>=b_0,
    <e_i,x>=(b_i+r*b_(i-1))/s, i>=1,
    ||x||^2=b_0^2+sum_(i=1)^k (b_i+r*b_(i-1))^2/s^2. (3)

For k>=1 the inverse of the Gram matrix in (1) is tridiagonal:
its two end diagonal entries are 1/s^2, its interior diagonal entries
are (1+r^2)/s^2, and each adjacent off-diagonal entry is r/s^2.
This follows by expanding (3), since b=U^T x for U with columns u_i.
For k=0 both matrices are [1]. In particular constant positive inner
products b_i=d require EXACTLY

    ||x||^2=d^2[1+k*(1+r)/(1-r)].                       (4)

This is a conditional radius cost for that prescribed array, not a
claim that every correlated witness must pay it.

Absolute row sums of the Gram matrix and its inverse give

    (1-r)/(1+r)<=lambda_min(Gram)
       <=lambda_max(Gram)<=(1+r)/(1-r).                (5)

For fixed r and growing k both extremes are approached: insert the
constant and alternating-sign coefficient columns in (2), divide by
k+1, and sum the resulting geometric series. The Rayleigh quotients
tend (1-r)/(1+r) and (1+r)/(1-r), respectively. Consequently the
conditioning can approach ((1+r)/(1-r))^2. If k*(1-r) tends to0,
the alternating-sign Rayleigh quotient is (k+1)(1+o(1)); an interior
diagonal entry of the inverse also implies
lambda_min(Gram)<=s^2/(1+r^2) for k>=2. Both types of degeneration
are relevant, depending on the joint limit.

Fix epsilon>0 and define the endpoint and its actual Euclidean data by

    w_i=epsilon*u_i-u_(i+1)
       =(epsilon+r)*u_i-s*e_(i+1),
    omega=sqrt(1+epsilon^2+2*epsilon*r),
    theta=arctan(s/(epsilon+r)).                        (6)

In the ORTHONORMAL plane (u_i,e_(i+1)), put

    E_i(t)=cos(t)*u_i-sin(t)*e_(i+1),
    T_i(t)=-sin(t)*u_i-cos(t)*e_(i+1),
    c=omega*exp(kappa*theta),
    V_i(t)=c*exp(-kappa*t)*E_i(t), 0<=t<=theta,
    N_i(t)=E_i(t)+kappa*T_i(t), kappa>0.               (7)

The endpoints are c*u_i and w_i. If delta=z-t, then

    <N_i(t),V_i(z)>/[c*exp(-kappa*t)]
      =exp(-kappa*delta)*(cos(delta)+kappa*sin(delta)).

Its derivative in delta is
-(1+kappa^2)*exp(-kappa*delta)*sin(delta). As |delta|<pi/2,
the expression has its maximum1 at0; its inner product with the
additional slope0 is strictly smaller. Thus N_i(t) supports the
convex hull of0 and the whole arc at V_i(t). At t=0, e_(i+1) is
also a supporting normal. This verifies the actual Euclidean arc.

Writing V_i(t)=p*u_i-q*u_(i+1) gives

    p=c*exp(-kappa*t)*(cos(t)-r*sin(t)/s),
    q=c*exp(-kappa*t)*sin(t)/s,
    p>=epsilon*q>=0, p<=c.                            (8)

For t>0 the first inequality follows from cot(t)>=(epsilon+r)/s;
at t=0 it follows from p=c,q=0.
For two such slopes in consecutive stages one has EXACTLY

    <p*u_i-q*u_(i+1), p'*u_(i+1)-q'*u_(i+2)>
       =-(r*p+q)*(p'+r*q').                           (9)

For stage separation d>=1 the right side is multiplied by
(-r)^(d-1). This follows by substituting (1) into the four terms.
In particular adjacent slopes have negative inner products, while
slopes two stages apart have positive ones when r>0. No assertion
about Markov dependence of all gradients follows from (9).

## 2. Two genuinely changing ordinary steps and their exact values

This section specifies a finite block without root finding. Let
Delta=theta/2 and define

    A_D=exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos(Delta)+sin(Delta)/kappa),
    B_D=exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin(Delta)/kappa,
    t_0=0, t_1=B_D, t_2=B_D*(1+A_D),
    theta_l=theta-l*Delta, l=0,1,2,
    R_l=c*exp(-kappa*theta_l),
    v_(i,l)=R_l*E_i(theta_l),
    r_(i,l)=R_l*((1+t_l)*E_i(theta_l)
                            +kappa*t_l*T_i(theta_l)),
    alpha_l=1-exp(-kappa*Delta)*cos(Delta)+B_D
                            +(1+kappa^2)*B_D*t_(l-1), l=1,2. (10)

Both alpha_l are strictly positive. Resolving radial and tangent
components in the basis (E_i(theta_l),T_i(theta_l)) proves

    r_(i,l)-r_(i,l-1)=alpha_l*v_(i,l), l=1,2.          (11)

Indeed, cancellation of the tangent component is exactly
t_l=B_D+A_D*t_(l-1), and the remaining radial coefficient is alpha_l.
The vector r_(i,l)-v_(i,l)=R_l*t_l*N_i(theta_l) supports the
finite slope hull conv{0,v_(i,0),v_(i,1),v_(i,2)} at v_(i,l).
Hence the chronological ordinary word (alpha_2,alpha_1) has gradients
v_(i,2),v_(i,1), followed at its checkpoint query by v_(i,0)=w_i.
These three gradients have different directions when r<1.

Use the following exact coefficients of its initial state:

    r_(i,2)=A*u_i-B*u_(i+1),
    B=c*kappa*t_2/s,
    A=c*(1+t_2)-r*B,
    Z=A+r*B=c*(1+t_2),
    R0^2=||r_(i,2)||^2
         =c^2*((1+t_2)^2+kappa^2*t_2^2).              (12)

By (11), r_(i,2)=w_i+alpha_1*v_(i,1)+alpha_2*v_(i,2).
Consequently A>=epsilon, B>=1 and A-epsilon*B>=0, despite the
subtraction in the formula defining A. The same positive combination
also shows ||r_(i,l)||<=R0: all arc pairs have positive inner product,
and the backward additions in (11) strictly increase the norm.

For any scale a>0 and center C define the finite local nonsmooth piece

    phi_(i,a,C)(y)=max{0, a*<v_(i,l),y-C>:l=0,1,2}.   (13)

At x=C+a*r_(i,l), its envelope with parameter1 has

    g=a*v_(i,l), y=x-g,
    phi_(i,a,C)(y)=a^2*R_l^2*t_l,
    f_local(x)=a^2*R_l^2*(t_l+1/2).                  (14)

These are retained physical values and proximal heights. They will
not be reselected when the pieces are assembled below.

## 3. Complete two-checkpoint calibration, including the terminal correction

For this calibration restrict sqrt(2/3)<=r<1 and 0<epsilon<=1/2, and take

    kappa=2*s/r,
    ell=1+epsilon*r,
    beta=ell/(epsilon+r),
    M=A+beta*B=Z+s^2*B/(epsilon+r),
    a=ell/M, C_0=0, C_1=-w_0-a*r_(1,2).              (15)

The first local scale is1, the second is a. Put

    Hpref=omega+(1+2*a)*(R0+c).                       (16)

Choose ANY real d strictly larger than the following explicit finite
maximum (this is a definition, not a parameter scan):

    max{2*c, 8*c*r*epsilon, sqrt(8*c*r*a*Z),
        c*r/epsilon,
        [1+2*(epsilon+c)/r]/epsilon,
        [Hpref+r*ell]/(epsilon*r)}.                  (17)

Set b_0=2, b_1=1+d/a and choose the actual six-step word

    h=(alpha_2,alpha_1,b_0,alpha_2,alpha_1,b_1).        (18)

The proposed prefix and terminal positions are

    x_0=r_(0,2), x_1=r_(0,1), x_2=w_0,
    x_3=-w_0=C_1+a*r_(1,2),
    x_4=C_1+a*r_(1,1), x_5=C_1+a*w_1,
    x_6=C_1-d*w_1.                                   (19)

The prefix gradients at indices0,...,5 are, in that order,

    v_(0,2),v_(0,1),w_0,a*v_(1,2),a*v_(1,1),a*w_1.  (20)

Equations (11), b_0=2 and b_1=1+d/a prove every GD update
x_(j+1)=x_j-h_j*g_j, including both checkpoints.

Define a terminal center and ONE static max-affine function by

    C_T=x_6-d*u_2,
    J(y)=max{phi_(0,1,0)(y),phi_(1,a,C_1)(y),
                                      d*<u_2,y-C_T>},
    F(x)=min_y [J(y)+||x-y||^2/2].                    (21)

There are seven nonzero affine planes and the plane0, all fixed before
the run. We next prove that (19)-(20), with exactly (14), are the
actual prefix jets of F and compute its actual terminal jet.

### 3.1 Future stage planes at all first-block proximal points

A nonzero first-block normal r_(0,l)-v_(0,l) is a positive multiple
of N_0(theta_l). Directly,

    <u_1,N_0(t)>=-r*cos(t)-s*sin(t)
                          +kappa*(r*sin(t)-s*cos(t))<0, (22)

because tan(t)<=s/(epsilon+r)<s/r. Every stage1 slope has component
p'+r*q'>0 along u_1 and its other component along e_2. It therefore
has nonpositive inner product with every first-block proximal point.
Moreover, for any stage1 slope v=p'*u_1-q'*u_2,

    <v,-C_1>=-ell*(p'+r*q')
                    +a*((p'+r*q')*A+(r*p'+q')*B)<=0. (23)

Here (r*p'+q')/(p'+r*q')<=beta, by (8), and a*M=ell.
Equations (22)-(23) prove that every future stage1 plane is
nonpositive throughout the first block, including its checkpoint.

### 3.2 Every old plane at all second-block proximal points

Let an old slope be v=p*u_0-q*u_1. At the second-block endpoint
proximal point C_1 its inner product is

    <v,C_1>=-epsilon*(p+r*q)-(r*p+q)
                                     +a*(r*p+q)*Z.  (24)

The ratio (p+r*q)/(r*p+q) is at least
(epsilon+r)/ell. Thus (24) is nonpositive provided
a*Z<=omega^2/ell. This holds because

    a*Z=ell*Z/M<ell, omega^2-ell^2=epsilon^2*s^2>0.   (25)

At an earlier second-block proximal point, its displacement from
C_1 is a*(r_(1,l)-v_(1,l)). The old slope has inner product

    <v,r_(1,l)-v_(1,l)>
      =-(r*p+q)*R_l*t_l*(cos(theta_l)-kappa*sin(theta_l)).

The stated restriction r>=sqrt(2/3) gives

    kappa*tan(theta)<=1.                              (26)

Indeed (26) is equivalent to 2*(1-r^2)<=r*(epsilon+r).
Consequently the cosine difference and t_l are nonnegative, so the
old plane value at every earlier proximal point is at most its
value at C_1. Thus every old plane is nonpositive and the old
piece, which includes the plane0, equals0 there. This pays the
actual normal geometry in the second-block maximum.

### 3.3 The terminal plane is inactive throughout the prefix

Every prefix proximal point has norm at most Hpref. For the first
block use ||r_(0,l)-v_(0,l)||<=R0+c. For the second use
||C_1||<=omega+a*R0 and then add a*(R0+c); (16) bounds both.
From (19)-(21),

    <u_2,C_T>=-r*ell+a*(r*A+B)+epsilon*r*d>Hpref,     (27)

by (17) and A,B>0. Hence the terminal plane is negative at every
prefix proximal point and at0. Equations (23)-(25) also show the
second piece is zero at0; the first piece is zero there by definition.
Thus J(0)=0, min J=0, and every claimed prefix proximal point has
the indicated local plane active in the global maximum. Its local
gradient in (20) is a subgradient of J there, so (14) gives the
actual prefix values and heights of F. No earlier plane, value or
height has been removed.

### 3.4 The direct terminal jet fails, and its exact correction

The tempting pure new-direction terminal jet would be

    g_6^ideal=d*u_2, f_6^ideal=d^2/2,
    y_6^ideal=C_T, proximal height0.                   (28)

Let

    K0=a*Z+epsilon*d-1,
    V=c*(r*K0-epsilon),
    Dmix=c^2+d^2-2*c*d*r^2.                           (29)

The first cap plane at C_T has value V. Its original interpolation
row with the retained first jet and (28) is EXACTLY

    Q_(0,6)^ideal=V>0.                                (30)

This follows either by inserting (14) into Q, or by canceling the
first cap's affine height to obtain c*<u_0,C_T>. The fifth entry
in (17) makes K0>2*(epsilon+c)/r, which proves V>0.
This is a failure of the specified terminal jet, not a
value-independent cycle. For six steps it is an incoming terminal
row; using that jet to begin any further ordinary block makes it
a required queried-prefix row. The full-function request cannot
ignore it. We do not claim that (30) alone excludes value reselection.

The ONE correction in this calibration is to keep J unchanged and
use its actual mixed terminal gradient. Put

    eta=V/Dmix,
    g_6=(1-eta)*d*u_2+eta*c*u_0,
    y_6=x_6-g_6.                                     (31)

We prove 0<eta<1 and that these are the actual terminal data. First,
Dmix>=(d-c)^2>d^2/4, while

    V<c*r*a*Z+c*r*epsilon*d<d^2/4

by the first three entries in (17). Thus eta is strictly between0
and1. For every 0<=t<=1 set y(t)=C_T+t*(d*u_2-c*u_0).
The old cap minus the terminal plane at y(t) is V-t*Dmix, so they
tie at t=eta.

To check ALL other planes, project y(t) into (u_0,e_1). Its coordinates
are

    (r*(K0+t*d*r)-epsilon-t*c, -s*(K0+t*d*r)).        (32)

Since kappa*r-s=s and K0>2*(epsilon+c)/r, its first coordinate
is positive and its downward second magnitude is at most kappa
times that first coordinate. Therefore (32) is a nonnegative
combination of N_0(0)=u_0-kappa*e_1 and e_1. Both support the
first-stage hull at c*u_0. The cap dominates every first-stage
plane, and its value is positive.

For any second-stage slope v=p'*u_1-q'*u_2, direct expansion gives

    <v,y(t)-C_1>
       =-d*epsilon*(p'+r*q')-t*d*(r*p'+q')
                                     +t*c*r*(p'+r*q')<=0, (33)

because d*epsilon>c*r. Thus no second-stage plane intervenes.
At y_6 the old cap and terminal plane tie above all remaining
planes, and their convex combination in (31) belongs to partial J(y_6).
As x_6-y_6=g_6, this proves the proximal condition exactly.

Evaluating the envelope yields

    F(x_6)=d^2/2+V^2/(2*Dmix).                        (34)

For an explicit check, the envelope value associated to the mixture
t is d^2/2+t*V-t^2*Dmix/2: at the tie, use the weighted average
of the two active affine values at x_6 and subtract half the squared
norm of their averaged slope. Substitution of eta gives (34).
This changes only the illegal proposed terminal jet (28); every
prefix value and every prefix proximal height in (14) is unchanged.

### 3.5 Global smoothness, every directed row, and the exact radius

The function J is a finite convex maximum, nonnegative and zero at0.
For every x, J(y)+||x-y||^2/2 is coercive and strictly convex, hence
has a unique minimizer y. The optimality condition is x-y in partial J(y):
the forward implication follows by testing y+t(z-y) and letting t
decrease to0, and the reverse follows by completing the squared norm.
For two pairs write g=x-y and g'=x'-y'. The two subgradient
inequalities imply

    ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>.

Using y as a trial point at x+w gives the upper estimate
F(x+w)<=F(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2; the subgradient inequality gives
F(x+w)>=F(x)+<g,w>. These bounds prove differentiability with
gradient g, convexity, and a globally1-Lipschitz gradient.
The minimum is0 and argmin F={J=0}.

For this calibration the least global Lipschitz constant is actually1.
At0 the planes with slopes0,c*u_0,w_0,a*w_1 are all active; the
last one is active because a*M=ell makes its inequality in (23)
an equality. The three nonzero slopes are linearly independent
by (1),(6), so their convex hull with0 has nonempty Euclidean
interior and belongs to partial J(0). At every input in that
interior the proximal point is0 and grad F(x)=x. Thus no global
gradient Lipschitz constant strictly below1 is possible.

For any two actual samples, terminal and minimizers included,
write H_i=J(y_i), f_i=H_i+||g_i||^2/2 and x_i=y_i+g_i. Then

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =H_i-H_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i><=0.                  (35)

This proves every old/new directed interpolation row, in both
orientations, without a matrix of unchecked remote entries.

Finally x_0=r_(0,2)=w_0+alpha_1*v_(0,1)+alpha_2*v_(0,2)
is a nonnegative combination of first-stage slopes active at0.
Every z with J(z)=0 has nonpositive inner product with each of
these slopes, hence <x_0,z><=0. Consequently

    ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2+||z||^2,
    dist(x_0,argmin F)^2=R0^2,                       (36)

and0 is its unique closest minimizer. The complete finite conclusion is

    W_6(h)>=[d^2+V^2/Dmix]/R0^2.                     (37)

The function L*F has smoothness bound L, uses precisely the same
positions for physical steps h_i/L, and gives the same normalized
payoff. There is no half-sum, preconditioning or concealed step rescaling.

## 4. The first remote cycle of the repeated pure-stage rule

The terminal mixture above is not the prescribed initial gradient of
another fresh local arc. Here is the exact failure if one instead
continues the locally maximal, pure-stage zero-height gluing rule.
It uses the SAME frame and arcs, now in R^4 to allow u_3, not a
new mechanism or an attempted second correction.

Retain the parameters and the two-step ordinary block (10). Choose
T0>0 and T1>0, and define

    a_0=1, a_1=T0*ell/M, a_2=T1*ell/M,
    C_0=0,
    C_1=-T0*w_0-a_1*r_(1,2),
    C_2=C_1-T1*w_1-a_2*r_(2,2),
    b_0=1+T0, b_1=1+T1/a_1.                         (38)

The proposed trajectory consists of the three ordinary blocks with
their translated/scaled local data (14), with the two checkpoints
b_0,b_1 between them. Every GD update agrees with (11),(38).
To keep the three decisive samples strictly in the queried prefix,
take the physical nine-step word

    (alpha_2,alpha_1,b_0,alpha_2,alpha_1,b_1,
                                      alpha_2,alpha_1,1). (39)

The final ordinary step1 is executed from the third endpoint query;
it does not introduce a third selected checkpoint. The endpoint
queries at physical indices2,5,8 have proposed data

    X_i=C_i+G_i, G_i=a_i*w_i, F_i=||G_i||^2/2,
    i=0,1,2, corresponding respectively to indices2,5,8. (40)

These are local data, not claimed jets of a common function.
Define the positive quantities

    z=ell*Z/M,
    H=epsilon+r-r*z
       =s^2*[epsilon+r*ell*B/((epsilon+r)*M)],
    K=omega^2-ell*z
       =s^2*[epsilon^2+ell^2*B/((epsilon+r)*M)].      (41)

All equalities follow from M-Z=s^2*B/(epsilon+r) and
omega^2-ell^2=epsilon^2*s^2. In particular H,K>0.
For the original row convention the following is an EXACT
value-independent cycle:

    Q_(2,8)+Q_(8,5)+Q_(5,2)
                 =-T0*K+T1*ell*H.                  (42)

Proof: with the convenient test values in (40), the elementary
cancellation in Q gives Q_(i,j)=<G_i,C_j-C_i>. Now

    <w_1,w_0>=-(epsilon+r)*ell,
    <w_i,r_(i,2)>=(epsilon+r)*M,
    <w_0,r_(1,2)>=-ell*Z,
    <w_0,r_(2,2)>=r*ell*Z.

These follow from (6),(9),(12). Therefore

    Q_(5,2)=a_1*[-T0*(epsilon+r)*ell
                                      +a_1*(epsilon+r)*M]=0,
    Q_(8,5)=a_2*[-T1*(epsilon+r)*ell
                                      +a_2*(epsilon+r)*M]=0,
    Q_(2,8)=-T0*omega^2+a_1*ell*Z
                          +T1*(epsilon+r)*ell-a_2*r*ell*Z
             =-T0*K+T1*ell*H.

The function values cancel in the sum of ANY three original Q rows
around this cycle. Thus calculating it with (40) proves (42) for
EVERY possible reassignment of all values on these same positions
and gradients. Translation of all positions also leaves it unchanged.
There is no minimizer in this cycle.

In particular, take the explicit positive choice

    T1=2*T0*K/(ell*H).                                (43)

Then the cycle equals T0*K>0. At least one of its three original
queried-prefix rows is positive, independently of the entire value
array, of any choice or relocation of the minimizer, and of all
unqueried terminal data. No convex1-smooth function realizes these
jets. This is stronger than the single failed terminal row (30).

The rejected hypothesis is precise: identical chosen local arcs and
ordinary blocks, local endpoint proximal heights0, Euclidean GD,
and maximal adjacent scales a_(i+1)=T_i*ell/M as in (38), with
T1/T0>K/(ell*H). It is not a theorem about all scale choices, all
height-bearing interpolants, arbitrary arcs, or all correlated frames.
The local blocks themselves remain legal. Their pairwise gluing
does not extend to the third block by that rule.

The six-step calibration in Section3 is not advertised as a repair
of the nine-step word (39). It gives the exact static function and
actual mixed terminal jet BEFORE demanding a third pure local block.
No part of the failed third block is silently discarded from a claim
about that same longer word. No second correction of (42) is attempted.

## 5. Local activation, transfer, radius, and turning costs

The following accounts for the apparent small-angle gain and its
limits. It does not turn the failed repeated rule into a lower bound.

For a general ordered nonnegative ordinary word, a backward arc
trajectory can be constructed in the actual orthonormal plane.
Start at w=(epsilon+r,-s). Suppose the current backward point is
(X,-Y), projected to V(theta_j), with positive X,Y. For a step alpha,
if

    Y<=kappa*[X+(alpha-1)*c],

the preceding projection is c*u and the preceding point is obtained
by adding alpha*c*u. Put mu=X+(alpha-1)*c; its normal is
mu*N(0)+(kappa*mu-Y)*e_new, with nonnegative coefficients.
Otherwise choose the unique angle t in (0,theta_j] for which

    <(X,-Y),E(t)-T(t)/kappa>/[c*exp(-kappa*t)]=1-alpha. (44)

The left side equals1 at theta_j. Its value at0 is
(X-Y/kappa)/c<1-alpha, and its derivative is
(kappa+1/kappa)*(X*cos(t)+Y*sin(t))/[c*exp(-kappa*t)]>0.
The root therefore exists uniquely. Resolving radial and tangent
components shows that adding alpha*V(t) gives a nonnegative
supporting normal at that slope. The tangent coefficient is
nonnegative because the polar angle of the current point is at
least theta_j and the new angle is no larger. Backward addition
preserves X>0,Y>0 and Y/X<=s/(epsilon+r). A capped point stays
capped in all earlier steps. These observations inductively prove
the asserted local construction, including alpha=0.

At an uncapped point use the representation R*((1+t)*E+kappa*t*T).
For two consecutive backward angles separated by Delta>=0, its
normal coefficient obeys exactly

    t_old=B(Delta)+A(Delta)*t_new,
    A(Delta)=exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos(Delta)+sin(Delta)/kappa),
    B(Delta)=exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin(Delta)/kappa.        (45)

Let d0=1-kappa^2. Differentiation gives
A'-(d0/kappa)*A=-(1+1/kappa^2)*exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin(Delta)<=0.
For kappa<1, A<=exp(d0*Delta/kappa) and
1+d0*B<=exp(d0*Delta/kappa); the latter follows from
B<=Delta/kappa and exp(t)>=1+t. Hence telescoping (45) yields

    t<=T=[exp((1-kappa^2)*theta/kappa)-1]/(1-kappa^2).

For kappa>=1, A<=1 and B<=Delta/kappa instead give t<=T=theta/kappa.
These bounds also bound the vertical coordinate at the first cap,
which is unchanged by subsequent backward cap additions.

Write the resulting initial point as A_b*u_i-B_b*u_(i+1), and let
S be the physical sum of this ordinary block. Equation (8) implies

    A_b=epsilon+P, B_b=1+Q,
    0<=P<=c*S, Q>=0, P>=epsilon*Q,
    B_b<=Bmax=c*[(1+T)/omega+(kappa/s)*T].             (46)

For the last bound use sin(t)<=sin(theta)=s/omega and R<=c in
the downward orthonormal coordinate R*((1+t)*sin(t)+kappa*t*cos(t)),
then divide by s. These are actual vector coefficients, with (2)
still required for all norms.

If the local trajectory starts at D*u_i, is scaled by a, and its
center is D*u_i-a*(A_b*u_i-B_b*u_(i+1)), its required zero value
at the old origin is equivalent to

    D/a>=M_b:=A_b+beta*B_b.                            (47)

Indeed divide each endpoint or arc plane inequality at0 by its
positive factor p+r*q. The ratio (r*p+q)/(p+r*q) is maximized
at the endpoint p/q=epsilon, giving beta. Thus (47) is necessary
as well as sufficient for that zero set; it is not only a loose
displacement estimate. In particular

    M_b<=c*S+Aadd, Aadd=epsilon+beta*Bmax.             (48)

For the source-style buffer point which retains the coefficient
of u_(i+1) just before a checkpoint, the plane residual is proportional to

    (1-b)*epsilon*(p+r*q)+(r*p+q).

Consequently that particular buffer has the exact threshold

    b>=1+beta/epsilon.                               (49)

The actual post-checkpoint point, with its additional b*a*u_(i+1),
is in the current piece's zero set already for b>=1. Equation (49)
prices an extra zero-region buffer. It must not be imposed as a
necessary threshold for every correlated construction: Section3
uses b_0=2 and proves its cross-plane conditions directly.

For consecutive stages, a preceding checkpoint with T_i=(b_i-1)*a_i
can supply the next scale only up to

    a_(i+1)<=T_i*ell/M_(i+1)                           (50)

if the next endpoint plane is to vanish at the preceding center.
This follows by exactly the calculation (23). When (26) holds,
the other direction's old-plane conditions also hold for the local
arc normals. Thus (50) is a legitimate ADJACENT transfer bound.
The gain ell is real Euclidean geometry and cannot be dropped or
replaced by an orthogonal-coordinate transfer formula.

The full local initial norm is

    ||A_b*u_i-B_b*u_(i+1)||^2
       =A_b^2+B_b^2+2*r*A_b*B_b.                    (51)

For the actual calibration (36) supplies its closest-minimizer
interpretation; (51) alone does not establish such an interpretation
for an arbitrary global gluing. If an initial state includes other
stages, its full norm must instead use (2), or (3) for prescribed
inner products. Neither can be replaced by a sum of stage squares.

### 5.1 Limits as r tends to1

Keep 0<epsilon<=1/2 fixed and kappa=2*s/r. Then

    theta/s -> 1/(1+epsilon),
    theta/kappa -> 1/[2*(1+epsilon)],
    c -> 1+epsilon,
    T -> exp(1/[2*(1+epsilon)])-1,
    Bmax -> (1+T)+2*(1+epsilon)*T,
    beta -> 1.                                      (52)

Thus the LOCAL activation allowance (48) stays finite: the
orthogonal spiral's exponential in 1/kappa has not been imported
into this different Euclidean angle. This observation is paid for
by the actual geometry, but it is not a global checkpoint theorem.

For the specific two-step ordinary block let

    tau=1/[4*(1+epsilon)], Ttwo=2*tau+tau^2.

Equations (10)-(12),(15) give, without executing a recurrence,

    alpha_1 -> tau, alpha_2 -> tau*(1+tau), t_2 -> Ttwo,
    B -> 2*(1+epsilon)*Ttwo,
    M,Z -> (1+epsilon)*(1+Ttwo),
    a -> 1/(1+Ttwo),
    R0^2 -> (1+epsilon)^2*(1+Ttwo)^2.                 (53)

These limits are finite and positive. The threshold (17) remains
bounded for each fixed epsilon; near r=1 it is O(1/epsilon) as
epsilon tends to0, with constants bounded for 0<epsilon<=1/2.
Thus a fixed sufficiently large d satisfies (17) for all r close
enough to1. For such a fixed d,

    V -> (1+epsilon)*epsilon*d,
    Dmix -> [d-(1+epsilon)]^2,
    eta -> (1+epsilon)*epsilon*d/[d-(1+epsilon)]^2.    (54)

The correction is not identically zero in this small-angle limit.
For a prospective next physical step h, the actual next position
minus the position predicted using the pure jet is EXACTLY

    h*(g_6^ideal-g_6)=h*eta*(d*u_2-c*u_0),
    norm = h*V/sqrt(Dmix).                            (55)

For large d and then r near1 this has scale h*(1+epsilon)*epsilon.
There is no uniform bound over arbitrary subsequent physical h.
One cannot carry the improved terminal value (34) into a further
ordinary block while silently restoring the pure terminal gradient.

### 5.2 Accumulated factors and the exact surviving cycle cost

An adjacent-only multiplication of (48),(50), for positive ordinary
sums S_i, would incur the squared coefficient factor (c/ell)^(2k),
activation factors and the checkpoint numerators b_i-1. Its exact
logarithmic loss relative to the purely diagnostic product using
b_i/S_i is

    2*k*log(c/ell)+2*sum_i log(1+Aadd/(c*S_i))
                                      -2*sum_i log(1-1/b_i). (56)

This is only an accounting identity for the proposed adjacent
estimate. Because (42) can be positive, it is NOT an actual
lower bound for a repeated k-stage witness. When S_i=0 use the
undivided denominators in (48); the diagnostic (56) is not defined.

The Euclidean coefficient in (56) satisfies EXACTLY

    log(c/ell)=kappa*theta
                    +(1/2)*log(1+epsilon^2*s^2/ell^2). (57)

The ell transfer cancels the leading endpoint-norm factor. In
particular, with epsilon fixed and r tending to1,

    log(c/ell)/s^2
      -> 2/(1+epsilon)+epsilon^2/[2*(1+epsilon)^2].   (58)

Keeping this coefficient loss negligible as k grows would require
k*s^2 small. But then r^d is near1 for all stage separations
d<=k: none of the remote stages decouples.

This is not just a conditioning warning. Set
zeta=2*Ttwo/(1+Ttwo). The exact remote cycle constants in (41)
obey

    H/s^2 -> epsilon+zeta,
    K/s^2 -> epsilon^2+(1+epsilon)*zeta,
    K/(ell*H)
      -> [epsilon^2+(1+epsilon)*zeta]
                          /[(1+epsilon)*(epsilon+zeta)] < 1. (59)

The strict inequality follows because the denominator exceeds the
numerator by epsilon. In particular even the equal-budget choice
T1=T0 fails for every r sufficiently close to1, and

    [Q_(2,8)+Q_(8,5)+Q_(5,2)]/(T0*s^2) -> epsilon>0. (60)

A small positive interpolation defect is still incompatible with
an exact common function. Equation (60) is not permission to omit
it at each stage or to average illegal realizations. The specific
saturated repeated pilot is already excluded at this finite length;
it has no legal unbounded-stage scaling to price as a payoff.

For reference, the entire old-plane memory is also explicit. With
possibly differing local starts A_i*u_i-B_i*u_(i+1), let
Z_i=A_i+r*B_i, T_i=(b_i-1)*a_i and retain the center update
C_(i+1)=C_i-T_i*w_i-a_(i+1)*r_(i+1,start).
For every j<i,

    <u_j,C_i-C_j>
      =-(epsilon+r)*T_j
       -sum_(t=j+1)^(i-1) (-r)^(t-j)
                                  [a_t*Z_t+(epsilon+r)*T_t]
       -(-r)^(i-j)*a_i*Z_i.                          (61)

Every old cap at a proposed zero-height endpoint requires the
right side to be nonpositive. It follows by expanding the center
recursion with <u_j,r_(t,start)>=(-r)^(t-j)*Z_t and
<u_j,w_t>=(-r)^(t-j)*(epsilon+r). Thus all old centers and all
physical displacements remain present. Absolute geometric weights
sum to (1-r^(i-j))/(1-r); their effective number grows on the scale
min{i-j,1/(1-r)}. Their signs may matter; this absolute sum is not
claimed to be an unavoidable radius loss. In the small total-turning
regime the full old-plane memory has order k, not order1.

## 6. Smoothness overlap if a sum were substituted

The actual construction (21) uses one envelope and pays global
smoothness1 exactly as proved, irrespective of r. There is no
overlap factor in (37). A half-sum argument from orthogonal stages
would be invalid here without a new proof.

To quantify that statement, let P_i be the orthogonal projector onto
span(u_i,e_(i+1)). Since these two vectors are orthonormal,
P_i=u_i*u_i^T+e_(i+1)*e_(i+1)^T. Consequently

    sum_(i=0)^(k-1) P_i+u_k*u_k^T
       =U*U^T+sum_(j=1)^k e_j*e_j^T=:Aov,
    lambda_max(Gram)<=lambda_max(Aov)
                              <=lambda_max(Gram)+1. (62)

For the concrete half-sum of local quadratic functions

    Fquad(x)=(1/4)*[sum_(i<k)||P_i*x||^2+<u_k,x>^2],

the TRUE least gradient Lipschitz constant is
lambda_max(Aov)/2, because its Hessian is Aov/2. For fixed k and
r tending to1, Aov tends
(k+1)*e_0*e_0^T+sum_(j=1)^k e_j*e_j^T, so this constant tends
(k+1)/2. In particular it exceeds1 when k>=2 and r is sufficiently
near1. This refutes the generic old half-sum bound in these spans.
It does not assign that least constant to our particular local
envelopes or claim they all attain their largest Hessians together.
A sum construction would have to prove its own global constant
and charge the corresponding division of every physical step.

## 7. Source audit, status, and the exact missing interface

The immutable assemblies c8fcf4ced5a34046 and6be9249882fd0868 were
read before this construction. Their single-envelope and half-sum
proofs use orthogonal physical stage coordinates and particular
zero-region bounds. They are not premises here. The full evidence
of138b57940a77871b was recovered from GM after its proposed shared
mirror path returned "No such file or directory". Its fixed endpoint
and prescribed zero-height minimum impose its own original endpoint
row. Changing the actual Euclidean angle changes that local row to
(47), but does not erase the new global cycles (42).

The primary source Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, Silver Rate Is (Almost)

# R11 handoff: genuine coupled returns, failed in a precise geometric class

**UNVERIFIED. Original target ACTIVE / OPEN. No complete candidate for formal review.**

Grant `3b9683ee8aaee8c8`, TASK SHA256 `832c4fbeadaeb4fa84199eeaa36fedb18b57f99f8a730ecace7d4848644f4ef5`. One native gpt-6-astra / ultra invocation; two substantive analytical approaches; mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal/verifier/additional agents/additional models all zero. Only PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and this HANDOFF.md were written. Parent owns budget/shared administration. Stop at this handoff.

The original target remains `lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ=1`, W=2R, or a full-class asymptotic disproof. Every sufficiently large integer horizon, every nonnegative predetermined word, exact all-split C_n/B_n, original selected minimum/radius and actual endpoint remain required. No restricted witness ceiling is promoted to an upper bound on W.

## What was actually constructed

1. A fixed compact two-ray cone truncation realizes alternating slanted faces at EVERY physical step of the complete inherited H_m. Its radius is exactly 1 and terminal payoff lies between Pi_m^2/4 and Pi_m^2. The general homogeneous-cone argument already forces the upper bound Pi_m^2, even with the original closest polar minimum. The parent identified this as an instantiation of an existing public route boundary (including finding 21d26350c9141917), so it is not new novelty or a promising mechanism.

2. A SINGLE translated triangle genuinely couples a transverse drift to repeated returns between two nonorthogonal edges. Write `u=(a,s), v=(-a,s), a^2+s^2=1`, vertex `e=(0,-b)`, and `K=conv{e,e+Ru,e+Rv}`, with `R>b/s`. The origin is interior and is the UNIQUE original minimum of the projection-envelope objective. At points `x_t=((-1)^t r_t,-b-w_t)` with `w_t>=0` and `alpha_t=a r_t-s w_t>0`, nonbinding third face gives the exact coupled recurrence

       r_(t+1)=(h_t-1) r_t-h_t s(s r_t+a w_t),
       w_(t+1)=w_t-h_t b+h_t s alpha_t,
       2F(x_t)=b^2+2b w_t+alpha_t^2.

   This is one body, not an orthogonal product or independent Huber bank. The same alpha drives both physical coordinates. The full original radius is `r_0^2+(b+w_0)^2`.

For literal H_m, let `P_t=prod_(k<t)(h_k-1)`, `p=min_t P_t>0`, `S=S_m`, `D=1+2S`. The explicit choice

    b=1, r_0=1, w_0=2S,
    s=min(1/4,p/[16 S(3S+1)]), R=2/s+2

is proved feasible through the entire word. It has `S<=w_t<3S`, `(15/16)P_t<=r_t<=P_t`, and `alpha_t>P_t/2`. Thus every sign flip and projection face is real. Its original start is `(1,-D)`, original radius `D^2+1`, terminal shape `((-1)^n r_n,-1-S-sQ)`, and EXACT payoff

    [D+2sQ+alpha_n^2]/[D^2+1],  Q=sum h_t alpha_t.

All geometry is fixed before the forward run. No wall increase, overwritten future, auxiliary query, free height, child radius, or alternative invalidated minimum is used.

## Decisive full-hierarchy result for this policy

The proof then permits ARBITRARY b>0, angle 0<s<1, original point, and nonbinding truncation length within the entire geometric policy: all queries lie at or below the horizontal line through the translated vertex; the two slanted edges alternate at every step; every projection is on an open slanted edge; the third face never binds.

On complete H_m with the one enclosing m>=1536 and its inherited parameters, it derives

    2F(x_n)/||x_0||^2 <= 33/(32 D_m).

With the supplied accepted comparison `B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50`, the benchmark-normalized payoff is strictly below `1551/1600`. This is a complete scoped manual failure proof, still UNVERIFIED.

The quantitative mechanism is explicit. Set `A_m=sum h_t P_t`. Whole-word recursion gives `A_m<3(3/2)^m`. The original-radius identity yields

    payoff <= [1+s^2 a^2 A_m^2]/D_m + Pi_m^2.

For `s A_m<=1/8`, this is at most `33/(32D_m)`. Otherwise the actual final H_(m-1) child starts with radial amplitude at most `q r_0`, where `q=beta^(2^(m-1))/c<2^(-m^2)`. Keeping its w coordinate nonnegative charges

    b/r_0 <=9 q A_m,

and the terminal face condition gives `w_n/r_0<8 A_m Pi_m`. These estimates make the entire payoff smaller than `1/D_m`. The right-child argument uses its actual inherited prefix product and original radius, with no child normalization.

## Scope that remains open

The new ceiling does not cover trajectories that go above the translated vertex, bind the third face, enter the body, remain on a face for several steps, or project to the vertex. It does not cover more complicated higher-dimensional coupling or the general multivalue K_a inverse construction. The origin is unique in the second geometry, so there is no hidden improvement from another original minimum inside that stated family.

No all-word lower construction, full-class upper certificate, or resolution of the exact limit-one target was obtained. The low-mass Huber region remains covered by the already accepted result. Neither this packet nor its bound changes the exact all-integer benchmark. Do not read `33/(32 D_m)` as a W(H_m) upper bound.

No further approach or renewal is included. All new mathematics remains UNVERIFIED. Preserve the full proof for scope and the feasibility induction; its final hashes are supplied in the child closeout message.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002385 -->
# R11: actual repeated-edge coupling and a complete-hierarchy failure bound

**UNVERIFIED direct mathematical draft. The original minimax target remains ACTIVE / OPEN.**

Grant `3b9683ee8aaee8c8`. One assigned gpt-6-astra / ultra invocation; two substantive analytical approaches; no mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers, formal submissions, additional agents, or additional model calls. This packet is an actual-construction attempt with a scoped failure theorem. It contains no complete original-target candidate.

The genuinely coupled construction in Section 4 uses one fixed triangle, alternates between its two slanted edges at every physical step of the complete inherited word, and charges the original radius. Translating its vertex produces a real coupling between the transverse drift and the alternating tangential component. Nevertheless, Section 5 bounds the entire specified trajectory class by `33/(32 D_m)`, below the exact benchmark on the supplied stress family. This is a bound on a restricted construction, not on the full worst-case error.

## 1. Target, evidence, and boundaries

The unchanged target is either

\[
 \lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathcal W_n^*}{U_n^{\rm ZJ}}=1,
 \qquad
 \mathcal W_n(h)=\sup\frac{2(f(x_n)-f_*)}{L\|x_0-x_*\|^2},
 \quad U_n^{\rm ZJ}=B_n^{-1},
\]

or a rigorous asymptotic separation certified over the full smooth-convex function class. An affirmative result must hold for every sufficiently large integer n and every predetermined nonnegative word, with a schedule-independent error term. We preserve W = 2R, every original selected minimizer, the original denominator, the actual endpoint, finite dimensions, and the exact all-integer benchmark:

\[
 C_0=B_0=1,\quad
 C_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}\Omega(C_k,C_l),\quad
 B_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}G(C_k,B_l),
\]
\[
 \Omega(x,y)=\frac{x+y+\sqrt{x^2+6xy+y^2}}2,
 \qquad G(x,y)=2x+\frac y2+\frac{\sqrt{y^2+8xy}}2.
\]

Read in full: TASK.md; OPERATOR.md; PROBLEM.md; `MATHEMATICAL_ELABORATION_20261009_R10B.md`; `R10B_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md`; `variable_wall_recursive_witness_20261009_r10b/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md`; `R10_PARENT_VARIABLE_WALL_NOTE_20261009.md`; and accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d`, including its statement. The designated TASK SHA256 is `832c4fbeadaeb4fa84199eeaa36fedb18b57f99f8a730ecace7d4848644f4ef5`, and the designated R10b proof SHA256 is `004ec6c3bf51b7c924bbf159f2b7b1b35b1a42347fa9ca84d5cff6d57693efce`; both were checked and matched.

The benchmark comparison `B_(2^m-1)/D_m < 47/50` is imported only at the scope supplied by the signed-fact handoff for `931b9653ce0f7201`. Its proof was not independently repeated in this call. The single-cut formula from `b98bb807d514e80d` is old; no independent bank of those witnesses is counted as a new approach.

The parent additionally reported that the general conic obstruction used in Section 3 is already a public route boundary, including finding `21d26350c9141917`. Accordingly Section 3 is explicitly an actual instantiation and sanity boundary, not a new promising mechanism or a novelty claim. The translated, nonhomogeneous triangle in Sections 4--5 is the second and final approach in this grant.

All results derived here are manual, source-bound research drafts, not verifier-accepted facts. No conclusion about failure of the original minimax proposition follows from failure of either policy.

## 2. The literal stress word and elementary transport quantities

Fix one enclosing m >= 1536 and retain its parameters in every child:

\[
 \sigma=1+\sqrt2,\quad \epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad
 \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad c=\sigma(1+\epsilon),\quad a_h=\frac\beta c,
\]
\[
 H_0=\varnothing,\qquad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
 \quad d_j=a_hc^j,\quad S_j=\sum H_j,\quad D_j=1+2S_j.
 \tag{2.1}
\]

Every physical step is strictly larger than 1. For a word of length n write

\[
 P_0=1,\qquad P_t=\prod_{k<t}(h_k-1),\qquad
 A(h)=\sum_{t=0}^{n-1}h_t P_t.
 \tag{2.2}
\]

These are analytic finite sums and products, not numerical evaluations or an executed array. Let `Pi_j` be the complete product for H_j and `A_j=A(H_j)`. With `u=a_h/(c-2)` the exact identities are

\[
 S_j+1=uc^j+(1-u)2^j,
 \quad \Pi_j=\beta^{2^j-1}c^{-j},
 \quad q_j:=d_j\Pi_j=\frac{\beta^{2^j}}c.
 \tag{2.3}
\]

Here `1/2<u<1` and `2<c<5/2`; in particular `D_j<2c^j` for j>=1. Every prefix product of excesses in H_j is at most 1. To check this by induction, a prefix either lies inside the left child, ends just after the bridge, or consists of that left child and bridge followed by a right-child prefix. The latter two cases have prefix multiplier `q_(j-1)<1/c`. The empty prefix has product 1.

Splitting the actual sum in (2.2) into its left child, bridge, and right child gives

\[
 A_{j+1}=(1+q_j)A_j+\Pi_j+q_j.
 \tag{2.4}
\]

Since `Pi_j<=1` and `q_j<1/2`,

\[
 A_j<3(3/2)^j<2^{j+2}\qquad(j\ge1).
 \tag{2.5}
\]

Also `A_(j+1)>A_j`. None of these estimates resets the inherited parameter at a child.

At the entrance to the final H_(m-1) child in H_m, the true prefix product is

\[
 q:=d_{m-1}\Pi_{m-1}=\frac{\beta^{2^{m-1}}}{c}.
 \tag{2.6}
\]

For m>=16, `2^(m-1)>=4m^3`: it holds at 16, and the ratio between successive left and right sides is at least one because `2m^3>=(m+1)^3` for m>=4. Therefore

\[
 q<2^{-m^2},\qquad \Pi_m<2^{-m^2},\qquad
 D_m<2^{2m+1}.
 \tag{2.7}
\]

Indeed `(1-1/(4m))^(2^(m-1))<=exp(-2^(m-1)/(4m))<=exp(-m^2)<2^(-m^2)`. The very strong loss in (2.7) is specific to this same-parameter complete word; it is not claimed for arbitrary schedules.

## 3. Approach 1: actual repeated faces on a truncated cone

### 3.1 The general homogeneity boundary, including the original minimum

For a closed convex cone C, let

\[
 F_C(x)=\max_{g\in C}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\tfrac12\|g\|^2\}
       =\tfrac12\|P_Cx\|^2.
\]

The equality follows from the projection variational inequality: if `x=p+z`, with `p=P_Cx`, then `z` belongs to the polar cone `C^o` and `<p,z>=0`. Thus the minimum set is `C^o` and the distance from x to this entire minimum set is `||p||`. This also proves the asserted original closest-minimum radius; it is not a denominator replacement at a child.

For h=1+d>=1 and the next gradient `q=P_C(x-hp)`, write `x-hp=z-dp`. Cone projection gives

\[
 \|q\|^2=\langle q,z-dp\rangle
 \le-d\langle q,p\rangle\le d\|q\|\|p\|.
\]

Hence `||q||<=d||p||`. For any word with every step at least 1, and any original selected minimum x_*,

\[
 \frac{2F_C(x_n)}{\|x_0-x_*\|^2}
 \le\prod_t(h_t-1)^2.
 \tag{3.1}
\]

The same conclusion applies to a compact truncation when all queried projections coincide with the cone projections and the polar minimum cone is unchanged. This is the old homogeneity obstruction in direct form. Merely arranging many face reentries does not remove it.

### 3.2 A finite two-dimensional realization at every physical step

This realization makes the failure concrete rather than merely conditional on a possible itinerary. For the complete H_m let `S=S_m`, `n=2^m-1`, and

\[
 p=\min_{0\le t\le n}P_t>0,
 \qquad s^2=\frac{p}{16S(S+1)},\quad a=\sqrt{1-s^2},
 \quad u=(a,s),\quad v=(-a,s).
 \tag{3.2}
\]

Let C be the cone generated by u,v and use the compact triangle

\[
 K=\operatorname{conv}\{0,2u,2v\},\qquad x_0=u,
 \quad F_K(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\tfrac12\|g\|^2\}.
\]

The function is convex, globally 1-smooth, and has minimizer 0. Its full minimum set is C^o, and 0 is the original closest minimizer to x_0, so the original squared radius is exactly 1.

At time 0 the gradient is u. At time 1 the point is `-(h_0-1)u`. Thereafter write the candidate physical point as

\[
 x_t=((-1)^t r_t,-w_t),\quad r_t>0,\quad w_t>0,
 \quad \alpha_t=ar_t-sw_t>0.
\]

Projection onto C is then `alpha_t u` for even t and `alpha_t v` for odd t. To check this, its residual is orthogonal to the selected ray; its inner product with the other ray is nonpositive, since r_t,w_t>=0. Projection onto K is identical whenever `alpha_t<2`.

With the notation `r_0=a,w_0=-s,alpha_0=1`, the exact alternating update is

\[
 r_{t+1}=(h_ta^2-1)r_t-h_tasw_t,
 \qquad w_{t+1}=w_t+h_ts\alpha_t.
 \tag{3.3}
\]

The first transition gives `r_1=aP_1,w_1=sP_1`. Induction using every actual step gives

\[
 \frac{15}{16}aP_t\le r_t\le aP_t,\qquad
 0<w_t\le sS,\qquad \frac12P_t<\alpha_t\le P_t
 \quad(1\le t\le n).
 \tag{3.4}
\]

For the lower estimate, expand the first recurrence after time 1:

\[
 r_t=aP_t-P_t\sum_{k=1}^{t-1}
 \frac{h_ks(s r_k+a w_k)}{P_{k+1}}.
\]

Using `r_k<=a`, `w_k<=sS`, and `P_(k+1)>=p`, its relative loss is at most `s^2 S(S+1)/p=1/16`. Then

\[
 \alpha_t\ge(15/16)a^2P_t-s^2S
 \ge(225/256)P_t-p/[16(S+1)]>P_t/2,
\]

because `a^2>=15/16` and S>1. This proves positivity at the next query and closes the induction, including the assumed sign alternation. The upper estimates follow from `w_t>=0`, `r_(t+1)<=(h_t-1)r_t`, and `alpha_t<=a r_t` after the first query. Thus the truncating third edge never binds.

The trajectory really revisits the same slanted face after every two steps; it is not a collection of independent single-cut paths. Its complete terminal payoff satisfies

\[
 \Pi_m^2/4<\frac{2F_K(x_n)}{\|x_0\|^2}=\alpha_n^2\le\Pi_m^2.
 \tag{3.5}
\]

By (2.7) this is far below `1/D_m`. This policy is fully realizable at all depths, but conic homogeneity makes it quantitatively unusable. The parent identified this mechanism as an instantiation of an old route boundary; it is not credited as a new lower-bound advance.

## 4. Approach 2: translate the vertex and couple drift to repeated returns

### 4.1 One fixed triangle and its exact projection law

Fix b>0 and `0<s<1`, put `a=sqrt(1-s^2)`, and retain the rays `u=(a,s),v=(-a,s)`. Let

\[
 e=(0,-b),\qquad
 K=\operatorname{conv}\{e,e+Ru,e+Rv\},
 \qquad R>b/s.
 \tag{4.1}
\]

The origin lies strictly inside this triangle. Consequently F_K is convex, globally 1-smooth, and has unique minimum 0 with value 0: for x nonzero a sufficiently small positive multiple of x belongs to K and gives a strictly positive envelope value. There is no minimum-cone reset in this approach.

At a query

\[
 x=(\zeta r,-b-w),\qquad \zeta\in\{-1,1\},\quad r>0,\quad w\ge0,
 \qquad \alpha=ar-sw>0,
 \tag{4.2}
\]

provided `alpha<R`, its gradient is

\[
 g=e+\alpha(\zeta a,s).
 \tag{4.3}
\]

Indeed `x-e=(zeta r,-w)` projects onto the selected cone ray with coefficient alpha. That projected point lies on the corresponding finite triangle edge, so it is also the triangle projection. The opposing-ray normal inequality is the same as in Section 3. No projected point touches the third face.

The value at this query is exactly

\[
 2F_K(x)=b^2+2bw+\alpha^2.
 \tag{4.4}
\]

This follows by shifting the maximization variable to `g=e+z`: the value is `<x,e>-||e||^2/2+||P_C(x-e)||^2/2`. The shift has a genuine cross effect in the dynamics because e is not orthogonal to either ray.

Suppose the sign flips at the next physical step while (4.2) remains valid. Then the exact recurrence is

\[
 r_{t+1}=(h_ta^2-1)r_t-h_tasw_t
         =(h_t-1)r_t-h_ts(s r_t+a w_t),
\]
\[
 w_{t+1}=w_t-h_tb+h_ts\alpha_t,
 \qquad \alpha_t=ar_t-sw_t.
 \tag{4.5}
\]

The same alpha feeds both equations. This coupling is absent in an orthogonal sum of Huber components. There is no splitting into radius-weighted independent single-cut payoffs.

### 4.2 An explicit complete-H_m instance with no unproved itinerary condition

For H_m, take

\[
 b=1,\quad r_0=1,\quad w_0=2S_m,\quad
 s=\min\left\{\frac14,\frac{p}{16S_m(3S_m+1)}\right\},
 \qquad R=2/s+2,
 \tag{4.6}
\]

where p is the positive finite minimum in (3.2). All of these choices depend on the whole physical word and are fixed before running it. Use the one triangle (4.1) and original start

\[
 x_0=(1,-D_m),\qquad \|x_0-0\|^2=D_m^2+1.
 \tag{4.7}
\]

The candidate recursion (4.5) is feasible through every depth and every physical step. More precisely,

\[
 S_m\le w_t<3S_m,\qquad
 \frac{15}{16}P_t\le r_t\le P_t,\qquad
 \alpha_t>P_t/2>0.
 \tag{4.8}
\]

Here is an induction that proves feasibility rather than assumes it. Let `T_t=sum_(k<t)h_k` be the physical prefix sum, distinct from the hierarchy sums S_j, and let `Q_t=sum_(k<t)h_k alpha_k`. The second recurrence gives

\[
 w_t=2S_m-T_t+sQ_t.
\]

When the preceding gradients are valid and `r_k<=P_k<=1`, their positive coefficients obey `alpha_k<=1`. Hence `S_m<=w_t<=2S_m+sS_m<3S_m`. Unrolling the first recurrence gives

\[
 r_t=P_t\left[1-\sum_{k<t}
 \frac{h_k s(s r_k+a w_k)}{P_{k+1}}\right]
 \ge P_t\left[1-\frac{s(s+3S_m)S_m}{p}\right]
 \ge(15/16)P_t.
\]

Because `a>=3/4`, `S_m>1`, and `3sS_m<=3p/[16(3S_m+1)]<=3p/64`, the next alpha is at least `(45/64)P_t-(3/64)p>P_t/2`. Thus the next r is positive, the sign really flips, and the next point satisfies (4.2). This closes the induction. All alpha values are below 1<R, so the third face is inactive for the entire forward run.

The terminal shape and exact payoff are

\[
 x_n=((-1)^n r_n,-1-S_m-sQ),\qquad
 Q=\sum_{t=0}^{n-1}h_t\alpha_t>0,
\]
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \frac{2F_K(x_n)}{\|x_0\|^2}
 =\frac{D_m+2sQ+\alpha_n^2}{D_m^2+1}.
 \quad}
 \tag{4.9}
\]

The original radius is fully paid, including the transverse drift. The two edges are visited in the pattern `u,v,u,v,...` throughout the literal complete H_m. There is one objective and one original minimizer; no future trajectory is overwritten, no wall is enlarged after use, and no child is renormalized.

At fixed finite word, sending s to zero through positive legal values gives the precise limiting payoff `(D_m+Pi_m^2)/(D_m^2+1)`: the tangential endpoint tends to Pi_m and must not be dropped. Thus the translated policy recovers the baseline order while keeping actual repeated-edge coupling. The next section shows that enlarging its angle or changing its original b,w_0,r_0 within the whole specified policy cannot pass the benchmark stress test.

## 5. A whole-class ceiling for this coupled policy

The class in this section permits arbitrary b>0, `0<s<1`, r_0>0, w_0>=0, and a finite R for which the whole physical trajectory satisfies (4.2)--(4.5), with strict alternation of the two slanted edges and no third-face projection. It is not restricted to the tiny-angle choice (4.6).

The conditions are geometric: every physical query, including the endpoint, lies on or below the horizontal line through the translated vertex; every projection lies strictly along the appropriate slanted edge; and the transverse sign changes at every step. Querying the vertex, entering the triangle, reaching above the vertex, holding one edge for several steps, or activating the third face is outside this policy. The selected minimum is necessarily the unique original 0.

### 5.1 Exact radius identity and two elementary consequences

Write `S=S_m`, `D=D_m`, `A=A_m`, `P=Pi_m`, `Q=sum h_t alpha_t`, and `v_0=b+w_0`. From (4.5),

\[
 0<r_t\le P_t r_0,\quad 0<\alpha_t\le ar_t,
 \quad Q\le aA r_0,
 \quad w_n\le ar_n/s\le aP r_0/s.
 \tag{5.1}
\]

The strict alpha inequality gives the last bound. The original radius and terminal twice-value are

\[
 R_0^2=r_0^2+v_0^2,
 \qquad N=b^2+2bw_n+\alpha_n^2
           =2b(v_0+sQ)-Db^2+\alpha_n^2.
 \tag{5.2}
\]

Completing the square in the actual b, followed by Cauchy--Schwarz in the original coordinates, gives

\[
 \frac{N}{R_0^2}
 \le\frac{1+s^2a^2A^2}{D}+P^2.
 \tag{5.3}
\]

This is only one estimate for an already specified legal trajectory. The proof below obtains a uniform quantitative ceiling from the actual final child, rather than treating (5.3) as an unproved global cost condition.

### 5.2 Small angle

If `sA<=1/8`, then by (2.7)

\[
 \frac{N}{R_0^2}\le\frac{65}{64D}+P^2
 \le\frac{33}{32D}.
 \tag{5.4}
\]

The last inequality uses `DP^2<2^(2m+1-2m^2)<1/64`, valid already at m>=16. This controls even angles chosen to depend on the entire horizon and word.

### 5.3 Larger angle: charge the full final child

Suppose `sA>1/8`, so `1/s<8A`. At entrance time tau to the final H_(m-1), (2.6) and (5.1) give

\[
 r_\tau\le q r_0,\qquad
 w_\tau\le a q r_0/s.
\]

Let `S_L=S_(m-1)` and `A_L=A_(m-1)`. Since the entire right child also obeys (4.5), its accumulated ray coefficient satisfies

\[
 Q_{\rm right}\le aA_L r_\tau\le aqA_Lr_0.
\]

Its terminal w is nonnegative, so the exact second recurrence requires

\[
 bS_L\le w_\tau+sQ_{\rm right},
 \qquad
 \frac b{r_0}\le\frac{aq(1/s+sA_L)}{S_L}\le9qA.
 \tag{5.5}
\]

Here `S_L>=1`, `A_L<=A`, `a<=1`, and `s<=1`. This is the cost of sustaining the drift through the actual final child. It retains the actual inherited product q; the denominator is still R_0.

Furthermore `w_n/r_0<8AP` and `alpha_n/r_0<=P`. Since `R_0^2>=r_0^2`,

\[
 \frac N{R_0^2}
 \le81q^2A^2+144qPA^2+P^2
 <2^{2m+12-2m^2}<\frac1D.
 \tag{5.6}
\]

For the middle inequality insert `q,P<2^(-m^2)` and `A<2^(m+2)` from (2.5)--(2.7); the coefficient is bounded by 226<256. For the last inequality multiply by `D<2^(2m+1)` and use `4m+13-2m^2<0` for m>=16. Every estimate applies to the whole physical suffix, regardless of how the original geometry was chosen.

Combining (5.4) and (5.6),

\[
 \boxed{\qquad
 \frac{2F_K(x_n)}{\|x_0-0\|^2}\le\frac{33}{32D_m}
 \quad\text{for the entire below-vertex, alternating-two-edge policy on }H_m.
 \qquad}
 \tag{5.7}
\]

Using only the supplied signed benchmark comparison,

\[
 B_{2^m-1}\frac{2F_K(x_n)}{\|x_0\|^2}
 <\frac{33}{32}\frac{47}{50}
 =\frac{1551}{1600}<1.
 \tag{5.8}
\]

Thus this actual coupled family misses the benchmark by a fixed factor even after arbitrary horizon-dependent choices of its angle, translated vertex, initial point, and sufficiently long sides. Its failure is stronger than merely observing that the particular small-angle witness resembles Huber. It does not invoke monotone active sets or a decomposition into independent reservoirs.

## 6. Audit and exact remaining scope

Both geometries are finite-dimensional compact projection bodies after their explicit truncations. Their envelope functions are convex and globally 1-smooth with the displayed original minima. Projection optimality, strict branch signs, every physical step, terminal value, and original radius were derived explicitly. For the cone, the closest original polar minimum was accounted for; for the translated triangle the original minimum 0 is unique. All side lengths, angles, and starts are fixed before the forward run. No auxiliary-query or wall-freedom assumption is used.

The second approach is genuinely coupled: the common alpha in (4.5) affects the alternating component and the transverse drift, and the `2bsQ` term in (5.2) is retained and paid. Its exclusion uses the low-product final child when that coupling is too large for (5.3). It is not the old single-reflection Huber formula, an independent positive sum, or the nested-active-set decomposition.

The exact scope is essential. Nothing here excludes a triangle trajectory that reaches above the translated vertex, binds the third face, enters the interior, pauses on a face, or visits the vertex. Nothing excludes a higher-dimensional body with more complex returns, a globally coupled multivalue K_a inverse policy, or any full-function-class hard instance. The first cone observation is a known boundary and has no novelty claim. The second policy has a complete actual all-depth construction and a complete scoped failure calculation, but supplies no viable all-word lower rule.

In particular, (5.7) is NOT `W_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=33/(32D_m)`. Replacing the restricted left side by W would be invalid. No full-class asymptotic separation has been obtained. No uniform all-word lower bound has been obtained. The already accepted Huber witness still covers `sum h <= (B_n-1)/2`; this packet does not close its high-mass complement. The exact all-integer C_n/B_n benchmark remains unchanged.

There is no complete original-target candidate for formal review. The two allotted approaches have reached their explicitly scoped outcomes. Continuing through an additional geometry or another model call would require a fresh allocation; no such continuation is performed here.

## 7. Resource and closeout record

- Assigned native invocation: 1, gpt-6-astra / ultra.
- Substantive analytical approaches: 2; the first is explicitly classified as an old-boundary instantiation after the parent novelty check.
- Mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers, formal submissions, verifier calls: 0.
- Additional agents/models, renewals, worker starts, service starts: 0.
- Access: designated public project material, selected public fact text, operating instructions, mathematical-writing instructions, and a bounded memory-registry check. No private worker memory was read.
- Writes: only this PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and HANDOFF.md in the assigned packet.
- Goal, budgets, truth stores, OPERATOR, CURRENT_RUN, all other packets, publishing, finalization, and revocation: untouched.
- One read-only search initially returned Windows path error 123 because a shell wildcard was passed literally; a corrected bounded directory search succeeded. There was no mathematical program failure or silent claim of successful execution.
- Exact child token usage is not exposed. The parent owns the authoritative budget/backend receipt; no token count is invented.

Stop after this bounded allocation. All new mathematics remains UNVERIFIED; the original target remains OPEN.

# R11 direct lower construction: coupled returns, not independent reserves

Fresh allocation: ONE native gpt-6-astra / ultra invocation, at most TWO substantive analytical approaches, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions ZERO. No extra agents/models, old grants, services/workers or automatic renewal. The parent owns budget and shared administration. Write complete PROOF_ATTEMPT.md and HANDOFF.md only here, label all new mathematics UNVERIFIED, provide meaningful progress and final hashes/resource counts, then stop. Never read private/local worker memory or change Goal, truth stores, OPERATOR, CURRENT_RUN, or other packets.

The human explicitly authorizes direct mathematical reasoning and asks for independent judgment and direct all-depth/all-word closure inspired by Zhang--Jiang. Original Goal ACTIVE/OPEN; Astra/ultra, consult OFF. Preserve lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ=1 or a certified full-class asymptotic separation, W=2R, exact all-integer C_n/B_n, original selected minimizer/radius, and actual endpoint. A lower result must ultimately cover every sufficiently large n and every h>=0 uniformly. A restricted witness ceiling is NOT a full W upper bound.

Read OPERATOR.md, the complete PROBLEM.md, research/MATHEMATICAL_ELABORATION_20261009_R10B.md, research/R10B_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261009.md, and COMPLETE research/variable_wall_recursive_witness_20261009_r10b/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md (SHA004ec6c3bf51b7c924bbf159f2b7b1b35b1a42347fa9ca84d5cff6d57693efce). Read research/R10_PARENT_VARIABLE_WALL_NOTE_20261009.md for actual inverse/wall identities, and the previous inverse proof only as needed. Read accepted b98bb807d514e80d's statement: the independent one-reflection formula is OLD and cannot be renamed a new reservoir.

The new requirement is REAL COUPLING across repeated face exits/reentries. The complete monotone-face K_a class is now excluded in draft at original selected0: every forward nested-active-set trajectory on actual H_m has payoff<=1/D_m. If all walls are positive,0 is unique; with zero walls, OTHER original minima are NOT covered. Independent positive orthogonal reservoir banks are exactly weighted averages of accepted single-cut Huber payoffs and offer no gain. Do not use them as your second new approach.

Concrete geometry: on finite positive weights, E={X:EX=0}, K_a={g:Eg=0,g>=-a}, a>=0, Ea>0. The actual gradient is g=max(X-t,-a), Eg=0. For h=1+d>1, choose Z=t+d*a-Y>=0 and E[omega Z]=d*Ea; use X_L=Y-h*a and X_R=t-a+Z/d. All branch splits duplicate the ENTIRE built future. Wall increases are free only on coordinates unsaturated at every already-built physical/required auxiliary query. Frozen walls may be reused unchanged by an upper branch, but new positivity does NOT restore wall freedom. Compute the initial radius at the FINAL fixed body's ORIGINAL chosen minimizer, never at a child.

Try an actual multivalue coupled policy that chooses cap inverses jointly with whole preceding blocks, allows more than one distinct selected lower value, and deliberately avoids locally minimizing variance when that hurts later cost. One concrete direction is a depth frontier: treat entire low-level children jointly and spend reflection only at larger bridges, while coupling the branch law across children. The cap resource constraint limits selected mass when its gap is huge; a rare large wall or coordinate is not free. This is a suggestion, not an assumption or a valid invariant.

Choose an explicit recursive terminal shape and actual branch/face pattern, then PROVE feasibility and original variance through ALL depths. The terminal shape and dimension may depend on the whole horizon and word. Do not return an abstract Bellman formula, another coefficient match, or an equivalent global cost condition. If the first specific coupled policy fails quantitatively, change its actual coupling/geometry in the second approach instead of returning to monotone or independent components.

The decisive whole-word stress family is: same enclosing m>=1536, eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps), a_h=beta/c, H0 empty, H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_h*c^j,H_j), S_j=sum H_j, D_m=1+2S_m. Accepted931b gives B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50. An actual whole-H_m benchmark-scale witness would pass a viability test but NOT prove all-word optimality. Continue toward an all-word rule if such a witness succeeds. The low-mass case sum h<=(B_n-1)/2 is already covered exactly by Huber; focus on the complement.

Other closed policies: fixed terminal +/-2 cannot give a standalone all-word bound (unit steps force Var0>=(n+2)^2); exact current-variance greedy inversion from +/-(B_n+1)/2 has Var0>Q*(7/5)^(2^(m-3)-1) on H_m; primitive-form look-ahead from that two-point law stays Huber with benchmark ratio<(94/97)^2. True global prefix optimization remains open. Common-normal height lifts are excluded only in their exact prior scope; no denominator reset or free height is permitted.

If using a different original minimizer of a zero-wall body, compute its validity and radius for the FINAL body before the forward run. Do not claim the monotone ceiling excludes it, but do not use a minimum invalidated by later wall growth. A genuinely coupled non-simplex body is allowed if its full projection geometry and original radius are proved; two independent Hubers are not coupling.

Seek a complete actual all-depth/payoff derivation. Every unproved uniformity or joint-attainability condition stays explicit. No formal review until a complete original-target candidate exists. End with the exact scope of any failure, not an inference that the original theorem is impossible.
