# Actual boundary absorption defeats the free heat profile

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL DRAFT, max4 round55, 2026-10-01.
No fact_submit is authorized or made. This is the one analytical profile
attempt allocated by TASK.md and guidance3ae9deefeb364de7. It uses no numerical
evaluation, trajectory replay, symbolic program, solver, benchmark DP,
installation, consult, or subagent. Algebra below was derived textually.

The precise new result is an ACTUAL-RUN counterexample to the proposed
unit free-heat envelope at every physical suffix query. For every enclosing
m>=10, the envelope holds at queries0,1,2,3 and fails at query4, immediately
after the original transition

    H_2=(t_0,t_1,t_0), followed by t_2.

At each of the two edges at distance3 from the initially positive closing
edge, the actual positive edge value exceeds the free heat comparison by

    5 mu z < E_m < 9 mu z,
    E_m/(mu z) -> 6 sqrt(2).                          (A)

The actual active sets are exactly {-k,...,k} for k=0,...,4 in the relabeling
below. The comparison kernels are also nonnegative through query4. Thus this
failure coexists with actual sign nesting and positive comparison profiles.
It is caused by the propagation of earlier boundary absorption through the
first large step, not by an arbitrary state outside the run.

This does NOT refute a terminal-only free-kernel comparison, positivity of
all complete-block kernels, an envelope with extra slack, or an actual
whole-block estimate. None of those is proved here. In particular no useful
terminal upper bound or benchmark-scale lower law for J_m is obtained.
The fixed family and the original all-word target remain OPEN.

## 1. Unchanged object and the selected quantitative envelope

Retain the exact law specified in shared37ec6ea9a52bfbdf:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
    t_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,t_j,H_j),
    d=t_(m-1)=1+beta c^(m-2),
    N=ceil(pi sqrt(d/6)), theta=pi/(2N), mu=sin(theta)^2,
    z=cos(theta), v_i=cos((2i+1)theta), 0<=i<N,
    (D x)_i=x_i-x_(i+1 modulo N), F(x)=||(D x)_+||^2/8.

The declared smoothness is L=1, the original start is v, and the original
word is H_m, always with the SAME enclosing m. No child is independently
retuned. All vector norms below are Euclidean. Positive parts are taken
coordinatewise. Let X_0=-v, and apply the actual physical suffix H_(m-1)
to this normalized suffix start. It is the positive-amplitude factor of
the original inherited state; the short justification is in Section2.

Relabel the closing edge N-1 as edge0, original edge i as edge i+1 modulo N.
Let y_k be D X_k in this relabeling and r_k=(y_k)_+. Put

    L_e=DD^T, P_h=I-(h/4)L_e,
    ell=2^(m-1)-1, h=(h_0,...,h_(ell-1))=H_(m-1),
    K_0=e_0, K_(k+1)=P_(h_k) K_k, R_k=2z K_k.        (1)

Here e_i is the edge-coordinate unit vector at relabeled index i, and I
is the identity. R_k is ONLY a proposed comparison profile. It is not the
actual trajectory of F. The proposed all-query spatial invariant was

    0 <= r_k <= R_k coordinatewise, 0<=k<=ell.         (2)

It starts at equality r_0=R_0=2z e_0. This is an amplitude and shape
assertion, stronger than a mass cap or knowledge of the active interval.
It retains every original physical step in its comparison kernel.

To make its quantitative purpose explicit, if (2) held at the terminal
query, it would imply

    J_m=N||r_ell||^2 <= 4z^2 N||K_ell||^2.            (3)

The right side is a linear spatial-kernel quantity. For example, a uniform
bound N||K_ell||^2<=C would give J_m<=4z^2 C. Finite cyclic Fourier
orthogonality gives the exact identity

    N||K_ell||^2
      =sum_(q=0)^(N-1) product_(j=0)^(m-2)
           (1-t_j sin^2(pi q/N))^(2^(m-1-j)).         (4)

Indeed the q-th cyclic Fourier vector is an eigenvector of L_e with
eigenvalue4sin^2(pi q/N); expand e_0 in these N orthogonal vectors and
square the eigenvalue product. The multiplicity of t_j in H_(m-1) is
2^(m-2-j). Formula (4) retains finite-cycle aliasing exactly; it is not an
infinite-lattice replacement or an evaluated numerical sum.

Write A_m=product_(j=0)^(m-2)(1-mu t_j)^(2^(m-2-j)) and
b_m=A_m(mu d-1)>0. The actual full-word payoff and original benchmark obey

    V_m=2F(x_(2^m-1))/dist(v,argmin F)^2=b_m^2 J_m/(2N^2),
    B_(2^m-1)V_m=b_m^2 B_(2^m-1) J_m/(2N^2).         (5)

Here B is the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark, defined in accepted
fact04219554e41d9750 by C_0=B_0=1,
Omega(s,t)=(s+t+sqrt((s+t)^2+4st))/2,
G(s,t)=2s+t/2+sqrt(t^2+8st)/2,
C_n=max_(0<=i<n)Omega(C_i,C_(n-i-1)),
B_n=max_(0<=i<n)G(C_i,B_(n-i-1)), and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.
All splits remain present. Consequently (2) at the terminal query and
the bound C would be strong enough for an eventual deficit whenever, for
some fixed delta>0 and all sufficiently large m,

    (2z^2 b_m^2 B_(2^m-1)/N^2) C <= 1-delta.         (6)

Conditions (2), the bound C, and (6) are the intended proof obligations,
not conclusions. The result below refutes (2) as an all-query invariant.

## 2. Minimal inherited-state and parameter checks

These checks fix the connection to the original run; static legality and
the old first-child analysis are not being offered as new progress.
Differentiation gives grad F(x)=D^T(Dx)_+/4, so the actual edge update is

    y' = y-(h/4)L_e y_+,
    y'_i = y_i-(h/2)(y_i)_+
                   +(h/4)((y_(i-1))_++(y_(i+1))_+).  (7)

The gradient is 1-Lipschitz since ||D||<=2 and the positive-part map is
1-Lipschitz. F vanishes exactly at constant vectors, and the mean is
preserved. Pairing the coordinates of v gives mean zero; summing
cos^2((2i+1)theta) using the finite geometric series gives ||v||^2=N/2.
Thus the true squared initial radius is N/2, without changing the declared L.

For m>=10, the inequalities lambda>12/5 and (12/5)^3>13 imply

    d > (7/8)(12/5)^8 > (7/8)13^2(144/25) > 768.

Hence theta^2<=3/(2d)<1/512 and

    0<mu<1/512.                                      (8)

For completeness let b=pi sqrt(d/6). Since pi>sqrt(6) and sqrt(d)>12,
b<=N<b+1<(13/12)b. Integration of cos(s)>=1-s^2/2 gives
sin(theta)/theta>=1-theta^2/6>99/100. Therefore

    5/4 < (3/2)(12/13)^2(99/100)^2 < mu d < 3/2.

Also t_(m-2)/d=1/c+(1-1/c)/d<5/12+1/768, so
mu t_j<2/3 for every j<=m-2. Substitution of the cosine addition formulas
in the active path at v gives grad F(v)=mu v. All factors1-mu t_j in
the original first child are positive. Successive physical updates thus
give A_m v, followed at the largest step d by -b_m v. Positive homogeneity
then shows that the original suffix state after k updates is b_m X_k.
This proves the inherited-state assertion and (5); it does not reset a child.

For the first four actual suffix steps define

    u=t_0/4, w=t_1/4, eta=t_2/4,
    p=1-2u, q=1-2w=1/(2m).

Their order is u,w,u,eta in units of h/4. The following bounds hold:

    1/3<u<3/8,       2/7<p<1/3,
    19/40<=w<1/2,    0<q<=1/20,
    3/4<eta<7/8,     0<1-beta^2<1/5.                 (9)

To check the less immediate lower bound on u, write
t_0=1+((m-1)/(m+1))(sqrt(2)-1). Since (m-1)/(m+1)>=9/11
and sqrt(2)>38/27, t_0>4/3. The upper bound t_0<sqrt(2) and
sqrt(2)<10/7 give p>2/7. The other bounds follow from
t_1=2-1/m and t_2=1+lambda(1-1/m^2), with 3<t_2<7/2.

There is also an exact relation essential below:

    eta p-(2eta-1)u=(1-beta^2)/4.                    (10)

Indeed (4eta-1)u-eta
=beta c(1+beta/c)/4-(1+beta c)/4=(beta^2-1)/4.
This retains the finite enclosing-m deformation rather than replacing it
by its limiting Silver parameters.

## 3. The actual inherited profile through the first H_2

At relabeled edge i, the initial profile is

    y_(0,0)=2z,
    y_(0,i)=-nu_i, 1<=i<N,
    nu_i=2sin(theta)sin(2i theta)=nu_(N-i)>0.          (11)

The exact first boundary depths needed below are

    nu_1=4mu z,
    nu_2=8mu z(1-2mu),
    nu_3=mu z(12-64mu+64mu^2),
    nu_4=16mu z(1-2mu)(1-8mu+8mu^2).                 (12)

These follow from the sine double-angle formulas. In particular
nu_2<=8mu z, nu_3<=12mu z, nu_4<=16mu z and
nu_3>(95/8)mu z by (8).

Let Delta_k=R_k-r_k. For k=1,2,3 all old positive coordinates remain
strictly positive: each of these steps has h<2, and (7) retains at least
(1-h/2) times each old positive value. Only the immediately adjacent
negative edges can activate. Their actual candidate values are

    r_(1,1)=2uz-nu_1 > z(2/3-4mu)>0,
    r_(2,2)=2zuw-(w nu_1+nu_2)
                              > z(19/60-10mu)>0,
    r_(3,3)=2zu^2w-u(w nu_1+nu_2)-nu_3
                              > z(19/180-16mu)>0.   (13)

The reflected edge indices have equal values. Every other
outside edge remains at its strictly negative initial value. Since N>27,
none of these indices overlaps its reflected counterpart. Induction using
(13) proves that the strict active set is exactly {-k,...,k} for k=0,1,2,3.

Write k_i=K_(3,i)=k_(-i). Multiplying the three three-point kernels gives

    k_0=q(p^2+2u^2)+4puw,
    k_1=2puq+w(p^2+3u^2),
    k_2=u^2q+2puw,
    k_3=u^2w,                 k_i=0 for |i|>3.        (14)

For example, these are the coefficients of
(p+u(s+s^(-1)))^2(q+w(s+s^(-1))) for a formal variable s.
The full symmetric kernel has positive coefficients on its support and
total mass one. At the first birth Delta_1 has value
nu_1 at edges+1,-1. At the second birth,

    Delta_(2,0)=2w nu_1, Delta_(2,1)=q nu_1,
    Delta_(2,2)=w nu_1+nu_2.

Propagate these deviations through the next u-step. Subtracting the new
boundary depth nu_3 from the actual profile adds nu_3 to its deviation
from the free profile. The exact deviations after the original entire H_2 are

    Delta_(3,0)=(2pw+2uq)nu_1,
    Delta_(3,1)=(pq+3uw)nu_1+u nu_2,
    Delta_(3,2)=(pw+uq)nu_1+p nu_2,
    Delta_(3,3)=u(w nu_1+nu_2)+nu_3.                  (15)

They are reflection invariant and zero off {-3,...,3}. All entries of
Delta_k for k<=3 are nonnegative, proving (2) through query3. No state is
reset to an impulse at the end of this H_2: (15) is retained at its bridge.

We will also use simple bounds following term by term from (9), (12),
and (14)--(15):

    19/105 < k_0 < 9/32,
    19/120 < k_1 < 9/32,
    19/210 < k_2 < 3/20,
    19/360 < k_3,

    0<=Delta_(3,0)<(3/2)mu z,
    0<=Delta_(3,1)<6mu z,
    0<=Delta_(3,2)<(7/2)mu z,
    0<=Delta_(3,3)<16mu z.                            (16)

For clarity the upper coefficient bounds before rounding are
k_0<1553/5760, k_1<1607/5760 and k_2<169/1280.
The corresponding deviation bounds before rounding are
(89/60)mu z, (319/60)mu z, (409/120)mu z and (63/4)mu z.
They result respectively from p<1/3, u<3/8, w<1/2, q<=1/20
and the displayed bounds for nu_i. The lower coefficient bounds use
p>2/7, u>1/3 and w>=19/40.

## 4. Exact envelope failure at the actual t_2 bridge

Consider relabeled edge3. At the incoming query3 it is strictly positive,
edge2 is strictly positive, and edge4 is strictly negative with positive
part zero. Also K_(3,4)=0. Thus (7), with the actual inherited profile,
gives

    y_(4,3)=2z K_(4,3)+E_m,
    E_m=(2eta-1)Delta_(3,3)-eta Delta_(3,2).           (17)

Substitute (15), then use (10). The exact excess simplifies to

    E_m=(2eta-1)nu_3
          -((1-beta^2)/4)(w nu_1+nu_2)-eta u q nu_1,

    E_m/(mu z)
      =(2eta-1)(12-64mu+64mu^2)
          -(1-beta^2)(w+2-4mu)-4eta u q.              (18)

This formula uses the finite-m parameters, the actual starting sine
profile, and the actual chronological t_0,t_1,t_0,t_2 transition.
Its lower bound is completely explicit. By (8)--(9),

    E_m/(mu z)
      > (1/2)(95/8)-(1/5)(5/2)
                       -4(7/8)(3/8)(1/20)
      =1719/320 > 5.                                 (19)

Both subtracted terms in (18) are positive. Since 2eta-1<3/4 and
nu_3/(mu z)<=12, it also follows that E_m/(mu z)<9.

The free comparison value at this coordinate is itself positive. Set
epsilon=(1-beta^2)/4>0. By (10) and (14),

    K_(4,3)=(1-2eta)u^2w+eta(u^2q+2puw)
            =uw(eta p+epsilon)+eta u^2q >0.           (20)

Therefore y_(4,3)>0, so its positive part is y_(4,3) and (17)--(19)
prove the ACTUAL strict violation

    r_(4,3)-R_(4,3)=E_m>5mu z.                       (21)

Reflection gives the same violation at edge-3. In the original edge
indices these are edges2 and N-4. At the corresponding original full-run
query, each excess is b_m E_m>5b_m mu z; the true radius, function and
declared smoothness have all remained unchanged.

Finally, as m tends to infinity, mu tends to zero, beta tends to1,
q tends to0, and eta tends to(2+sqrt(2))/4. Formula (18) then gives
E_m/(mu z)->6sqrt(2), proving all of (A). The unnormalized excess is of
order N^(-2). This does not itself exclude a terminal O(N^(-1)) profile
or any comparison with extra slack.

## 5. Sign nesting and positive comparison kernels survive this failure

We now check all remaining coordinates at query4, rather than supposing
that the failure in (21) was accompanied by a hidden sign reversal.
Put a_eta=2-1/eta<6/7. For i=0,1,2 the old positive values update by

    y_(4,0)=eta(2r_(3,1)-a_eta r_(3,0)),
    y_(4,1)=eta(r_(3,0)+r_(3,2)-a_eta r_(3,1)),
    y_(4,2)=eta(r_(3,1)+r_(3,3)-a_eta r_(3,2)).

Using r_(3,i)=2z k_i-Delta_(3,i) and (16) gives

    y_(4,0)>eta z(127/840-12mu)>0,
    y_(4,1)>eta z(17/280-5mu)>0,
    y_(4,2)>eta z(52/315-22mu)>0.                     (22)

For example the constants in the first two lines are respectively
4(19/120)-(6/7)2(9/32)=127/840 and
2(19/105+19/210-(6/7)(9/32))=17/280.
For the third they are
2(19/120+19/360-(6/7)(3/20))=52/315.
The upper deviation losses are respectively12mu z,5mu z,22mu z.
Each bracket is positive because mu<1/512.

Edge3 was already checked in (20)--(21). At the newly reached edge4,

    y_(4,4)=eta r_(3,3)-nu_4
             >z(19/240-28mu)>0,                     (23)

using (13), eta>3/4 and nu_4<=16mu z. All edges outside {-4,...,4}
still have no positive neighbor at the incoming time3 and remain
strictly negative. Reflection treats the negative indices. Thus the
actual strict active sets are nested through this transition and are
exactly {-k,...,k}, k=0,...,4.

The comparison kernel K_4 is nonnegative as well. For its coordinates
0,1,2 repeat the first parts of (22) with all deviations set to zero;
the displayed positive constant remains. Equation (20) treats3, and
K_(4,4)=eta u^2w>0. It vanishes outside {-4,...,4} and has total mass1
because each P_h preserves sums. Kernels K_0,K_1,K_2,K_3 are nonnegative
by their short-step definitions. Thus kernel positivity through the
transition does not imply comparison (2).

There are no boundary ties in these four steps: every named active
coordinate and every unreached negative coordinate has a strict sign.
The exact update (7) nevertheless includes ties correctly at arbitrary
later times by assigning positive part zero. No tie is deleted or given
an artificial Hessian continuation.

There is also no hidden creation of positive mass during this prefix.
The only absorption at update k, for k=1,2,3,4, is the depth nu_k at
each newly activated edge+k,-k. Hence

    sum_i r_(k,i)=2z-2sum_(j=1)^k nu_j, 0<=k<=4.      (24)

This is an actual-prefix identity, not a proposed global mass principle.
It underscores why a mass cap does not repair the failed pointwise shape
comparison.

## 6. Why the absorption comparison fails, and the exact surviving gap

For an arbitrary actual state y write r=y_+, n=(-y)_+, and U=P_h r.
If y_i<=0, then U_i=(h/4)(r_(i-1)+r_(i+1))>=0. Define

    a_i=min(n_i,U_i) if y_i<=0, and a_i=0 otherwise;
    b_i=(-U_i)_+ if y_i>0, and b_i=0 otherwise.

Directly taking the positive part of y'=U-n proves

    r_next=P_h r-a+b,      a>=0, b>=0.                (25)

At an old tie n_i=0, so a_i=0 and the same formula applies. A zero step
also satisfies it. The vector b records the repair needed when an old
positive coordinate becomes strictly negative; strict sign nesting
implies b=0. The converse may have ties and is not asserted.

For the four-step actual prefix above b=0 throughout. At its third
update the new absorption includes nu_3(e_3+e_(-3)). Propagating this
deviation through P_(t_2) multiplies its entry at3 by1-2eta<0.
Equations (17)--(19) show that the earlier absorption does not offset
this sign change. That is the precise reason the larger free profile
ceases to majorize the smaller actual incoming profile.

For a GENERAL terminal index ell, define P_(ell,k+1) as the ordered
product P_(h_(ell-1))...P_(h_(k+1)), with the empty product I. Iterating
(25), with the actual a_k,b_k, gives

    r_ell=R_ell-sum_(k=0)^(ell-1)P_(ell,k+1)a_k
                   +sum_(k=0)^(ell-1)P_(ell,k+1)b_k.  (26)

All these linear factors are on the same finite cycle; the nonlinear
sources still depend on the actual chronological states. Equation (26)
is a diagnosis of the missing source estimate, not a terminal result.

If every remaining-suffix product P_(ell,k+1) were entrywise nonnegative
and b_k=0 for all physical queries, then (26) would yield terminal-only
domination r_ell<=R_ell. This conditional implication is elementary and
is NOT refuted by (21): a later complete block might change the sign of
an earlier absorption contribution again. Nonnegativity of the full
prefix kernel K_k alone is insufficient, as (20)--(21) demonstrate.

The all-query profile induction selected here is refuted. The weaker
terminal-only route is still blocked at three explicit obligations:
control of b on the entire actual run, nonnegativity (or a paid bound
for negative entries) of every relevant remaining-suffix kernel, and a
quantitative bound for N||K_ell||^2 meeting (6). No such new bound is
claimed. Guidance1fc56054c08bd0ac assigns a distinct signed-kernel/source
analysis to another worker; this round does not start that second mechanism.

## 7. Literature check and status

The search-math-results workflow used Matlas first for positivity of
products of explicit heat steps. Its hits concerned different implicit,
cross-diffusion or entropy schemes. Web fallback located Zheng Tan,
Tariq D. Aslam and Andrea L. Bertozzi, *Explicit monotone stable
super-time-stepping methods for finite time singularities*,
arXiv:2507.17062v2 (2025),
https://arxiv.org/html/2507.17062v2 . The exact HTML was downloaded and
text extracted under this artifact directory. Section5's definitions,
Lemma2's coefficient proof, Theorem7 and the alternating-sum proof in
AppendixD were read. They concern particular Legendre/Gegenbauer
stability polynomials for a constant-coefficient heat equation. They
give neither the specified deformed H_j kernels nor control of the
positive-part sources in (25). No external theorem is imported here.

The mathematical new output is the explicit actual transition
(17)--(23), including the strict margin5mu z, its exact formula and
limiting coefficient. It refutes one specified spatial invariant,
while proving actual sign nesting only for this four-update prefix.
It is stronger in scope than a generic-state counterexample but much
weaker than a family obstruction. It gives no all-depth assertion about
actual nesting or J_m, no statement over every original schedule, and
no new verified fact.

All necessary components for this counterexample have been derived
jointly above. Earlier shared findings are awareness and object
specifications, never proof predecessors. Accepted04219554e41d9750 is
used only to identify the unchanged benchmark in (5)--(6).

This ends the single allocated analytical round with its exact blocker.
There is no self-renewal and no numerical or formal credit left in this
assignment. Actual process completion is recorded by the outer loop
after return; this manuscript is not process-exit evidence.

# Independent complete audit of the decoupled spiral extension

Worker max3, analytical round53, 2026-10-01. This is one JOINT UNVERIFIED
analytical audit. PASS below means that the stated interface survived the
independent derivation recorded here. It does not mean verifier acceptance.
No fact_submit, mathematical program, arithmetic replay, parameter scan,
solver, executed dynamic program, installation, or sub-agent was used.

## 1. Frozen object, scope, and verdict

The complete audited object is finding **c8fcf4ced5a34046**, at

`/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/c8fcf4ced5a34046.md`

Its length is **38,669 bytes** and its SHA256 is
`4d1e99db853eba3751a3f4761fc2c400c68eacdc9c7912b17c0b0c82d6975d2e`.
All 884 lines, including the statement, both realizations, obstruction,
loss formulas, source comparison, target limitation, and glossary were
read. The file was not edited. Section and equation references below refer
to this frozen object unless expressly stated otherwise.

The task and initial guidance **447c24caaa57c41d** authorize this one audit,
after completed52, with completed ceiling53 and attempted ceiling54. The
latest guidance read before closeout, **60da2cf01388bce5**, retains this
same max3 audit scope while assigning a separate integration to max4; it
does not enlarge this worker's allocation. The
earlier baseline cdd7f057606aeb18 and audit5787501015a5a361 were recalled
only as background. Their conclusions are not accepted premises here.
The full changed geometry and the unchanged interfaces it depends on are
rederived jointly below.

**Overall analytical verdict: PASS for the claimed finite-word extension.
No mathematical failure or missing load-bearing step was identified.**
The three domain conclusions are separate:

1. **Main rectangular domain: PASS.** For independently
   0<epsilon<=1/2 and 0<kappa<=1, the original main threshold
   b_i>=1+epsilon^(-2) suffices. For kappa<1 the stated c_sp,H_sp,A_sp
   formulas are exactly the old formulas; the boundary kappa=1 uses the
   continuous positive definition of T_sp.
2. **General-curvature main construction: PASS.** For every kappa>0 and
   0<epsilon<=1/2 the main construction works at the explicit sufficient
   threshold b_i>=B_sp. The old threshold is also sufficient whenever
   epsilon N_sp<=1. Neither necessity of B_sp nor a maximal domain for the
   old threshold is asserted.
3. **Source-format construction: PASS.** For every kappa>0 and
   0<epsilon<=1/2, the separate half-sum construction works at
   b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)), without an N_sp threshold. Its physical/local
   time conversion, exact payoff about0, and separate closest radius are
   all consistent.

The original unrestricted all-n/all-h comparison with B_n remains **GAP /
OPEN**, exactly as the frozen source says. It is not a defect in the finite
construction claim and is not settled by this audit. There is no first
failing mathematical step to report. No repair to the frozen mathematics
is required by this audit. A narrow clarification about the words
"smaller A" is suggested separately in Section12 below; it changes no
displayed claim.

## 2. Accepted conventions and primary-source check

The accepted fact **1252b8823bb5a290** was read for the original normalized
class and ordered residual convention

    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2.

The accepted fact **04219554e41d9750** was read for the original benchmark
definitions. Neither proves any spiral assertion here. No other
unverified finding is used as a correctness premise. The present
derivations form one unverified audit rather than a chain of purported
new accepted lemmas.

Primary-source comparison, paraphrased: Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu,
*Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152v2,
2026, [exact primary HTML](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2), equation(1),
Lemma2.2, Section3.1, and AppendixA.2--A.3 were reread. Their normalized
objective is the project's W. Their circular local function has threshold
1+epsilon^(-2) and activation constant epsilon+(H+1)/epsilon. Their
half-sum uses local half-steps, doubled physical threshold, and terminal
value D^2/[4(1+s_tail)] at distance1 from the specified minimizer0.
Normalization gives twice that value; the published lemma deliberately
weakens the factor. These statements support the claimed comparison.
They do not establish the spiral extension or its closest radii. The
larger recursion assumptions in their AppendixB are not used here.

The exact previously downloaded public HTML and text were reused, copied
without modification into this audit's `downloads/`, and the named
statements and proofs were read again. The primary arXiv page was also
opened again during this audit. The HTML SHA256 is
`d0a51e1c7d9990e4f28eb71cffd515ca405703460b446a0aa0a19824d2028d1e`;
the extracted-text SHA256 is
`dc573e7b90ebf5937585426ac34f4b509e32a3e40c4ac387daa2399417bb9d99`.
No prior audit verdict was reused as proof. A new Matlas query returned
Frank--Wolfe and gradient-dominated results with different hypotheses;
none was imported.

## 3. Arc, exact vertical extremum, and every supporting normal

For this report only, abbreviate theta_sp by theta_*, c_sp by c,
R_sp(theta) by R(theta), and the vectors e_sp,t_sp,n_sp by e,t,n.
These are scoped aliases, not changes to the project glossary. Set

    theta_*=arctan(1/epsilon),
    c=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa theta_*),
    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta), R(theta)=c exp(-kappa theta),
    n(theta)=e(theta)+kappa t(theta),
    K=conv({0} union {v(theta):0<=theta<=theta_*}).

Thus v(0)=(c,0), v(theta_*)=(epsilon,-1), and K is compact and convex.
Writing v=(p,-q), differentiation gives

    p'=-R(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,
    q'=R(cos theta-kappa sin theta).

Consequently epsilon<=p<=c, q>=0, and q/p=tan theta<=1/epsilon.
If kappa<=epsilon then theta_*<=arctan(1/kappa), so q attains its
maximum1 at theta_*. Otherwise its sole interior maximum is at
theta_c=arctan(1/kappa), and evaluation gives exactly

    qmax_sp=sqrt((1+epsilon^2)/(1+kappa^2))
              exp(kappa[theta_*-arctan(1/kappa)]).

At kappa=epsilon this expression equals1, so the piecewise definition
matches at equality. In the overshoot case define g(epsilon)=log qmax_sp
with kappa fixed. Then g(kappa)=0 and
g'(epsilon)=(epsilon-kappa)/(1+epsilon^2). Integration gives

    log qmax_sp=integral_epsilon^kappa (kappa-t)/(1+t^2) dt.

It is strictly positive, bounded below by
(kappa-epsilon)^2/[2(1+kappa^2)] and above by
(kappa-epsilon)^2/2. No step later silently replaces qmax_sp by1.

For delta=phi-theta the normalized support pairing is

    <n(theta),v(phi)>/R(theta)
      =exp(-kappa delta)(cos delta+kappa sin delta).

Its derivative is -(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa delta)sin delta. Since
|delta|<=theta_*<pi/2, the expression increases up to delta0 and
decreases thereafter, with maximum1. The origin has strictly smaller
pairing0. Thus n(theta) supports the entire K, not just nearby arc
points. At theta0 the vector (0,1) also supports K because q>=0.

The horizontal component cos theta-kappa sin theta has derivative
-sin theta-kappa cos theta<0. It becomes negative precisely after
theta_c when theta_c<theta_*. The vertical component of n is
-sin theta-kappa cos theta<0. These statements are valid for all
positive kappa, even when the arc dips below vertical height-1.
They prove the full claimed support geometry without importing the
published circular geometry.

## 4. Actual backward projections, including all boundary cases

Let alpha_1,...,alpha_m be the ordered nonnegative ordinary steps and
r_j=(u_j,-y_j). Start at r_m=v(theta_*)=(epsilon,-1). At an uncapped
stage retain

    r_j=R(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e(theta_j)+kappa t_j t(theta_j)], t_j>=0,
    u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon.             (A1)

Here the scalar t_j is the normal coefficient, not the vector t(theta).
The endpoint starts with t_m=0. Assume the induction at j and put
alpha=alpha_j.

If y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)c], put mu=u_j+(alpha-1)c. Then
mu>=y_j/kappa>0, and at r_old=r_j+alpha(c,0),

    r_old-(c,0)=mu n(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j).

Both terms belong to supporting normal rays at (c,0). Their sum
certifies its Euclidean projection: its inner product with every
v-(c,0), v in K, is nonpositive. Also
y_old<=kappa(u_old-c). Increasing u_old by any further alpha c preserves
the cap inequality for every alpha>=0. The cap is permanent backward,
including equality in the cap test; there is no unexplained endpoint
switch.

Otherwise the current point cannot already be capped. Define

    F_j(theta)=<r_j,e(theta)-kappa^(-1)t(theta)>/R(theta).

Equation(A1) gives F_j(theta_j)=1. The failed cap test gives
F_j(0)=(u_j-y_j/kappa)/c<1-alpha. Direct differentiation gives

    F_j'(theta)=(kappa+kappa^(-1))
                  (u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R(theta)>0.

Therefore precisely one theta in (0,theta_j] satisfies F_j(theta)=1-alpha.
This is a mathematical existence argument, with no executed root search.
Since u_j,y_j>0, the clockwise polar angle of r_j lies in (0,pi/2).
In(A1) the radial coefficient is positive and the tangent coefficient
nonnegative, so that polar angle is at least theta_j. It follows that
<r_j,t(theta)>=0 for the selected theta<=theta_j.

Let w=r_j+(alpha-1)v(theta). The root equation says
<w,e(theta)>=kappa^(-1)<w,t(theta)>, while
<w,t(theta)>=0. Hence w is a nonnegative multiple of n(theta).
This proves projection of r_old=r_j+alpha v(theta) onto K at v(theta),
and gives(A1) with the new nonnegative coefficient.

The coordinate invariants follow by addition of alpha(p,-q): both u,y
increase weakly, and y<=u/epsilon is preserved since q<=p/epsilon.
The induction is therefore closed for every finite ordinary word.
If alpha=0 at an uncapped point, the root is exactly theta_j and the
state and gradient repeat. If alpha=0 at a cap, the capped state repeats.
The uncapped endpoint can also repeat when trailing ordinary steps are0.
No physical entry is removed. Empty ordinary blocks need no induction.

The vertical assertion that replaces q<=1 is an identity:

    y_j-q_j=R(theta_j)t_j(sin theta_j+kappa cos theta_j)>=0. (A2)

It holds at the initial endpoint with equality, at every repeated
uncapped endpoint, and at every interior point. At a cap q_j=0 and
y_j>=1. This checks all normal cases, not just generic interior ones.

For the horizontal displacement, if n_1(theta_j)>=0 then(A1) gives
u_j-p_j>=0. In the negative-normal part, theta_j>theta_c and p decreases,
so p_j<=p(theta_c)=kappa qmax_sp. Together with u_j>=epsilon this gives

    u_j-p_j>=epsilon-kappa qmax_sp>=-N_sp,
    N_sp=max(0,kappa qmax_sp-epsilon).                    (A3)

At a cap u_j-c>=y_j/kappa>0. Thus(A3) covers that case as well. The
argument proves a sufficient upper bound on a deficit. It does not
identify N_sp as the exact largest deficit, and neither the draft nor
this audit relies on such an identification.

## 5. Turning bounds, displacements, and thresholds

For two consecutive uncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new set
Delta=theta_new-theta_old. Resolving the exact backward update along
t(theta_old) cancels the added gradient term. It yields

    t_old=B(Delta)+A(Delta)t_new,
    A(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.

All coefficients are nonnegative because 0<=Delta<=theta_*<pi/2.
Put d=1-kappa^2. Direct differentiation gives

    A'-(d/kappa)A=-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.

Since A(0)=1, A<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Always B<=Delta/kappa.
For kappa<1, d>0 and
1+dB<=1+d Delta/kappa<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Therefore

    1+d t_old<=exp(d Delta/kappa)(1+d t_new).

The sum of backward angle decrements is at most theta_*, and the
endpoint coefficient is0. Telescoping proves

    t_j<=[exp(d(theta_*-theta_j)/kappa)-1]/d<=T_sp.

For kappa>=1, the same differential estimate gives A<=1. Using
t_old<=Delta/kappa+t_new instead proves
t_j<=(theta_*-theta_j)/kappa<=theta_*/kappa=T_sp.
No division by d at kappa=1 or multiplication of an inequality by a
negative d occurs in this branch.

T_sp is positive and finite for each allowed pair. For fixed epsilon,
lim_(kappa->1-) [exp(d theta_*/kappa)-1]/d=theta_*;
the right branch theta_*/kappa has the same limit and value at1.
Continuity does not require matching derivatives. Also c>1, so
H_sp=c[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]-1>0 and
A_sp=epsilon+(H_sp+1)/epsilon>0. All subsequent positive scales have
strictly positive finite denominators. No uniform boundedness as
kappa->0 or epsilon->0 is claimed.

At an uncapped point,

    y_j=R(theta_j)[(1+t_j)sin theta_j+kappa t_j cos theta_j]
       <=c[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]=1+H_sp.

The transition into a cap and every subsequent capped step preserve y.
Thus the bound holds everywhere. Summing the actual updates gives
r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), and

    0<=P<=c S, 0<=Q<=H_sp, P>=epsilon Q,

where S=sum_j alpha_j. These are uniform in the finite word length and
individual sizes; there is no small-step assumption.

For the unchanged-threshold rectangle, when kappa<=epsilon we have
N_sp=0. Otherwise the derivative in epsilon computed in Section3 is
negative. Letting epsilon decrease to0 yields

    qmax_sp<=exp(kappa arctan kappa)/sqrt(1+kappa^2).

The logarithmic derivative of this right side in kappa is arctan kappa.
For 0<kappa<=1 it is bounded by exp(pi/4)/sqrt(2)<2. The source's
elementary numerical-constant proof is valid: pi<4,
e=1+1+1/2+sum_(j>=3)1/j!<=5/2+(1/6)/(1-1/4)=49/18,
and (49/18)^2<8 by 2401<2592. These are textual inequalities, not
executed arithmetic. Therefore N_sp<2<=1/epsilon and

    B_sp=1+max(epsilon^(-2),N_sp/epsilon)=1+epsilon^(-2).

This includes kappa1 and epsilon1/2. For arbitrary kappa>0 the same
equality follows whenever epsilon N_sp<=1, including equality in that
condition. Outside it the displayed larger B_sp supplies exactly the
horizontal bound used below. No unproved larger-domain claim is needed.

## 6. Finite local objective and proximal buffers

Fix a local block, a checkpoint b>=1+epsilon^(-2), and sigma>0 with

    sigma(P+epsilon b)<=1,
    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.

Set z_circ=(1,0)-sigma(epsilon+P,-1-Q),
U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b)>=0, Y=sigma Q, and

    V_fin={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_m},
    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>.

The set V_fin is finite; its hull lies in K and contains every v_j.
Every projection inequality from Section4 therefore remains true on this
smaller hull. At x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j and z_j=x_j-sigma v_j,

    <r_j-v_j,v-v_j><=0 for every v in V_fin.

Consequently the plane with slope sigma v_j is active at z_j and
sigma v_j belongs to the subdifferential of phi there. The strongly
convex proximal objective is uniquely minimized at z_j. This identifies
the actual envelope gradient, with all local competing planes retained.

For every nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin,

    <v,-z_circ>
      =-p+sigma[p(epsilon+P)+q(1+Q)]
      <=-p+sigma p[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=0.

Since p>0 and q>=0, all planes are nonpositive on z_1<=0,z_2>=0.
The zero plane shows phi=0 there. At(U,Y),

    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0

because q<=p/epsilon and b>=1+epsilon^(-2). Increasing the second
coordinate can only decrease these plane values. Thus phi(U,y)=0
for y>=Y. A nonnegative envelope has exactly the same zero set:
zero value forces both the nonnegative original value and the quadratic
distance at its attained proximal point to vanish. This proves both
required zero regions for phi and its envelope, including equalities.

The local path starts at(1,0), uses every ordinary alpha_j, and reaches
(1-sigma P,sigma Q)=(U+sigma epsilon b,Y), with endpoint gradient
sigma(epsilon,-1). The checkpoint reaches(U,Y+sigma b). Along the
ordinary part, the first physical coordinate decreases and the second
increases, so x_1>=U and 0<=x_2<=Y.

At local proximal points the second coordinate instead satisfies

    (z_j)_2=sigma(1+Q-y_j+q_j).

Backward monotonicity gives 1<=y_j<=1+Q, proving its nonnegative
lower bound. Identity(A2) proves the upper bound

    0<=(z_j)_2<=sigma(1+Q).                              (A4)

This is exact despite qmax_sp>1; no extra buffer error is accumulated.
For the first proximal coordinate, direct subtraction gives

    (z_j)_1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]
       >=sigma[epsilon(b-1)-N_sp]>=0                    (A5)

when b>=B_sp. This is the only place the additional general-kappa
threshold is used. The source-format assembly below uses physical
coordinates and does not need(A5).

## 7. One global max-affine envelope and every actual row

For the main realization set

    M_i=P_i+max(epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon), sigma_i=1/M_i,
    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1), ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).

All D_i are positive because b_i>=B_sp>1. Define

    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,
    delta_tail=D_k/(1+2s_k),
    Phi_k(z)=delta_tail max(z_k-ell_k,0),
    J_sp=max_(0<=i<=k)Phi_i, f=env_1 J_sp.

Each Phi_i is a finite affine maximum with a zero plane. Thus J_sp
is a single finite affine maximum, fixed from the entire supplied word
before GD starts. Its construction does not change along the path.
It is nonnegative and vanishes at0 by the pre-activation zero regions.

For completeness, the envelope properties follow directly. A proximal
point z exists uniquely since J_sp is continuous, convex, nonnegative,
and its addition to ||x-z||^2/2 is coercive and strictly convex. Write
g=x-z in partial J_sp(z). For another input x', monotonicity of the two
subgradients gives

    ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>.

Hence g is 1-Lipschitz. The trial point z at x+w gives the upper bound
f(x+w)<=f(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2. Using the actual proximal point z' at
x+w and the subgradient inequality at z gives the lower bound

    f(x+w)>=f(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.

The two estimates prove differentiability with gradient g; the lower
supporting inequality proves convexity. Minimum0 is attained at0.

At the start of block i the candidate state has frozen coordinates
x_j=ell_j+D_j U_j for j<i, current coordinate x_i=ell_i+D_i,
and x_j=0 for j>i. Apply the local path in coordinates i,i+1. At its
candidate global proximal point z, subtraction of its proposed gradient
changes only those two coordinates. The following checks establish that
the local path is the global one:

* The current Phi_i has the proposed scaled local subgradient at z.
* For Phi_(i-1), when it exists, its first input remains U_(i-1).
  By(A5), z_i>=ell_i+D_i U_i>=ell_i, so its second input is at least
  ell_i/D_(i-1)=sigma_(i-1)(1+Q_(i-1))>=Y_(i-1). It is zero.
* Any Phi_j with j<=i-2 sees both its coordinates frozen. Its first
  input is U_j and its second is at least ell_(j+1)/D_j>=Y_j.
  Those earlier pieces remain zero.
* For Phi_(i+1), when it exists, (A4) gives
  z_(i+1)<=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i)=ell_(i+1). Its first input is
  nonpositive and its second is0. It is zero.
* Every later local piece has first input -ell_j/D_j<=0 and second
  input0. The terminal hinge is also zero before activation; if it is
  the immediately next piece, the same(A4) bound applies to it.

The current Phi_i is nonnegative, so it attains J_sp at this point even
if its value is0 and several pieces tie. A subgradient of an active
piece is a subgradient of their maximum: its supporting affine bound
lies below that piece and hence below J_sp everywhere. Therefore the
candidate z satisfies the global proximal condition. Uniqueness proves
the claimed gradient is the actual global gradient. No inactive plane
has been deleted.

At the checkpoint the current coordinate freezes at ell_i+D_i U_i.
The next is D_i(Y_i+sigma_i b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1). This proves the
induction across all original physical steps, including consecutive
checkpoints and empty or all-zero blocks.

During the terminal block let u be the sum already executed. It lies in
[0,s_k], and the moving coordinate is ell_k+D_k-u delta_tail. Subtracting
the proposed gradient leaves it at least ell_k because

    D_k-(u+1)delta_tail
      =D_k(2s_k-u)/(1+2s_k)>=0.

All previous pieces are zero at this proximal point. The terminal
gradient is delta_tail e_k even at a boundary tie, since the terminal
hinge admits that subgradient at0. This proves the actual terminal
gradient and value, including the unqueried last sample:

    f(x_n)=delta_tail D_k-(s_k+1/2)delta_tail^2
          =D_k^2/[2(1+2s_k)].

For every ordered pair of samples a,b, including any minimizer and the
terminal sample, let z_a=x_a-g_a. The exact envelope identities give

    Q_ab=J_sp(z_a)-J_sp(z_b)+<g_a,z_b-z_a><=0.

Indeed, the norm and gradient cross terms cancel upon substituting
f_a=J_sp(z_a)+||g_a||^2/2 and x_a=z_a+g_a. The remaining inequality
is the subgradient inequality at z_a. It proves each orientation, all
remote rows, both optimum orientations, and all terminal rows. At a
minimizer g_star=0, J_sp(z_star)=0; repeated samples from zero steps
cause no exception. The gradients and positions are explicit vectors
in R^(k+1), so their Gram matrix is PSD on the actual data.

At k0 use J_sp(z)=delta max(z,0), delta=1/(1+2s_0), from x_0=1.
The same terminal argument holds, including an empty physical word.

## 8. Exact main radius and both main payoffs

For k>=1 define x_star with coordinates

    (x_star)_0=1-sigma_0(epsilon+P_0),
    (x_star)_1=sigma_0(1+Q_0)=ell_1,
    (x_star)_j=0 for j>=2.

The first local component is at its center. The next local component,
if any, sees(0,0), and all later components are in their zero regions.
The terminal hinge is zero, also when k1 and its coordinate equals
ell_1. Hence J_sp(x_star)=0. Nonnegative attained proximal terms imply
argmin f={J_sp=0}.

Both endpoint planes of the first local component are active at x_star.
The exact vector identity is

    e_0-x_star
      =[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/c] sigma_0(c,0,...)
         +(1+Q_0) sigma_0(epsilon,-1,0,...).

Both coefficients are nonnegative. Every minimizer z has nonpositive
inner product with each active endpoint slope after subtracting x_star.
Thus <e_0-x_star,z-x_star><=0. Expansion gives

    ||e_0-z||^2>=||e_0-x_star||^2+||z-x_star||^2.

This proves uniqueness of the closest minimizer and exactly

    dist(e_0,argmin f)^2
      =sigma_0^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2]>0.

Dividing the terminal value by this radius cancels the first sigma_0^2
and gives exactly formula(6), with its first numerator(b_0-1)^2 and
subsequent factors[(b_i-1)/M_i]^2. This is not merely a chosen-minimizer
bound.

For epsilon<=1,

    sqrt((epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2)
      <=epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon<=M.

Also M_i<=c s_i+max(epsilon b_i,A_sp). Therefore the exact payoff is
at least the mass-only expression(7). Equivalently the first radius is
at most1, as it must be because0 is another actual minimizer. Each
inequality has the direction needed for a lower payoff bound. At k0
the closest minimizer is0 and the exact payoff is1/(1+2s_0).

## 9. Separate source-format function and separate exact radius

Use b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)) and, for each ordinary block, rerun the
backward construction with steps divided by2. Thus the P_i,Q_i in this
section are half-step displacements; they are not the main construction's
displacements. Put beta_i=b_i/2 and

    sigma'_i=1/[c s_i/2+epsilon b_i/2+A_sp].

This satisfies both local scale conditions because P_i<=c s_i/2 and
epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon<=A_sp. Define local envelopes f_i=env_1 phi'_i
and their U'_i,Y'_i as in Section6 with beta_i. Set

    G_i=sigma'_i beta_i=b_i/[c(s_i+a_sp)+epsilon b_i], a_sp=2A_sp/c,
    D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,
    D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i, ell'_(i+1)=D'_i Y'_i,
    delta'=D'_k/(1+s_k).

The objective is the single fixed function

    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k) (D'_i)^2
           f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)
         +1/2 Hub_(delta')(x_k-ell'_k).

Each scaled local envelope is convex and 1-smooth on its two
coordinates. In the sum of its quadratic upper inequalities, each
coordinate occurs at most twice. The outer factor1/2 therefore gives
F(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2. For clarity this bound indeed
implies 1-smoothness here: subtract <grad F(y),z> to obtain a convex
function V minimized at y; apply the upper bound at x with
w=-grad V(x), and compare its value to V(y). This gives

    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>
      >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.                      (A6)

Exchanging x,y and adding gives cocoercivity, and Cauchy--Schwarz
gives a 1-Lipschitz gradient. The function is nonnegative and F(0)=0.
Thus0 is a specified actual minimizer, not an assumed normalization.

At a physical query in block i, the local first input is at least
U'_i>=0 and the second lies in[0,Y'_i]. The previous component sees
first input U'_(i-1) and second at least
ell'_i/D'_(i-1)=Y'_(i-1); it is zero. Earlier components have frozen
zero-region coordinates. The next component has first input<=0 and
second0; all later ones and the unactivated hinge are zero. Every zero
component has gradient0 because it is differentiable and nonnegative.
Only the current half-weighted term contributes to the gradient.

The chain rule makes a physical step h exactly a local step h/2.
The selected physical b_i similarly uses beta_i. Its transition leaves
next coordinate ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1), establishing the full induction.
This argument checks physical points only. It uses neither(A5) nor
u_j>=p_j, which is why every kappa>0 is allowed at the doubled old
threshold. No factor is lost to a presumed proximal coordinate bound.

In the terminal block the physical gradient is delta'/2. At partial
sum u<=s_k, the final coordinate above ell'_k is
D'_k-u delta'/2>=delta'(1+s_k/2)>=delta'. Hence the Huber term stays
in its affine region through the unqueried last sample. Substitution
gives F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[4(1+s_k)]. Initial point e_0 has distance1
from0, so its exact payoff about this minimizer is

    2F(x_n)=1/[2(1+s_k)] product_i
                [b_i/(c(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i)]^2.

This verifies formula(8) with no discarded epsilon b_i term. At k0,
F=Hub_(1/(1+s_0))/2 from1 gives the same formula. Inequality(A6),
with x=x_b,y=x_a, proves every original Q_ab<=0, including all
minimizer and terminal rows. Dimension and physical length are unchanged
by the local half-steps. For either realization, multiplying the whole
objective by L>0 and using physical steps h_t/L preserves the
trajectory and normalized payoff.

It remains to check the different closest radius. Let c_(i,1),c_(i,2)
be the two coordinates of the local center z'_circ,i. Since every slope
(p,-q) has the decomposition

    (p,-q)=[(p-epsilon q)/c](c,0)+q(epsilon,-1),

with nonnegative coefficients, the endpoint inequalities alone describe
the local zero set:

    z_1<=c_(i,1), epsilon(z_1-c_(i,1))-(z_2-c_(i,2))<=0.

They are also necessary because both endpoint slopes are actually
included. The local envelope has the same zero set. A sum of the
nonnegative terms vanishes exactly when each vanishes. Thus the full
minimum set C_rad is exactly

    x_i<=zeta_i,
    epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta_i-D'_i c_(i,2), i<k,
    x_k<=ell'_k,
    zeta_i=ell'_i+D'_i c_(i,1).

It contains0 and is a nonempty closed polyhedron. Let A_rad x<=t_rad
be these 2k+1 inequalities, precisely with row vectors e_i and
epsilon e_i-e_(i+1), followed by e_k. For independent row subsets S
write A_S for that submatrix, r_S=A_S e_0-(t_rad)_S,
K_S=A_S A_S^T, and lambda_S=K_S^(-1)r_S. Independence makes K_S
positive definite. The source's finite formula is

    rho_source^2=max_(S independent,lambda_S>=0) r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S,

including the empty subset with value0. No extra feasibility condition
on e_0-A_S^T lambda_S is needed: every entry is a dual lower bound,
and an optimal independent active subset occurs in the list.

Here is a direct verification. For x in C_rad and any lambda>=0 on S,
put w=A_S^T lambda. Then <w,e_0-x>>=lambda^T r_S. Completing the
square gives

    ||e_0-x||^2>=2lambda^T r_S-||w||^2.

For lambda=lambda_S the right side equals the listed entry. Thus
each listed entry is at most the true squared distance.

Conversely take the unique projection x_* of e_0 onto C_rad and set
w_*=e_0-x_*. If a direction d has nonpositive pairing with all active
rows, x_*+t d remains feasible for sufficiently small t>0: active
constraints do not increase and the finitely many inactive constraints
have positive slack. The projection inequality gives <w_*,d><=0.
It follows that w_* lies in the cone of active rows. To justify this
without an imported optimization theorem, remove linear dependence from
any nonnegative generator representation by subtracting a suitable
multiple of an oriented dependence, maintaining nonnegativity until a
coefficient vanishes. An independent representation results. The cone
is therefore a finite union of images of closed orthants under injective
linear maps, and is closed. If w_* were outside it, its residual after
projection onto that closed cone would have nonpositive pairing with
every generator but strictly positive pairing with w_*, a contradiction.
The residual assertion follows from the cone projection inequality and
orthogonality to its projected vector, obtained by testing0 and twice
that vector.

Choose an independent active subset representing w_*=A_S^T lambda
with lambda>=0. Activity implies r_S=A_S w_*=K_S lambda; hence
lambda=lambda_S. Its listed entry equals||w_*||^2, proving equality.
The empty-subset case covers a zero residual, although the present
initial point actually has positive distance. For k>=1 its first
constraint would require
1<=1-sigma'_0(epsilon+P_0), which is false. For k0 the set is the
nonpositive half-line. Thus 0<rho_source^2<=1, the upper bound using
0 in C_rad, and the exact closest-distance payoff is(8)/rho_source^2.
The main radius from Section8 has not been borrowed.

## 10. Finite equal-angle witness, paid cost, and uniformity

Fix positive kappa and 0<epsilon<=1/2. For a positive integer M set
Delta=theta_*/M, theta_l=theta_*-l Delta, and use the A,B of Section5
at Delta, denoted A_D,B_D. Define

    t_0=0, t_l=B_D+A_D t_(l-1),
    r_l=R(theta_l)[(1+t_l)e(theta_l)+kappa t_l t(theta_l)],
    alpha_l=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D
                   +(1+kappa^2)B_D t_(l-1).

Every alpha_l>=0. Resolving r_(l-1) in the basis at theta_l makes
its tangent component R(theta_l)kappa t_l; the radial difference from
r_l is exactly alpha_l R(theta_l). Hence
r_l-r_(l-1)=alpha_l v(theta_l), with no approximation. The support
normal proves projection at every r_l. The actual chronological word
(alpha_M,...,alpha_1) therefore runs from r_M to r_0=(epsilon,-1).
At its last backward state theta_M=0 the cap inequality is equality;
this is one of the valid cap cases in Section4. Thus this is an actual
finite family in the construction, not a continuous path asserted to
approximate one.

Let S_M=sum alpha_l, P_M=(r_M)_1-epsilon and
Q_M=-(r_M)_2-1. At theta_M=0,

    P_M=c(1+t_M)-epsilon, 1+Q_M=c kappa t_M.              (A7)

For kappa!=1 put d=1-kappa^2 and

    T_eq=[exp(d theta_*/kappa)-1]/d,
    S_eq=(1+kappa^2)(T_eq-kappa theta_*)/d.

For kappa1 use T_eq=theta_*, S_eq=2theta_*+theta_*^2.
The distinction matters: when kappa>1, T_eq is an exact family limit,
while the sufficient T_sp is theta_*/kappa. In that region
0<T_eq<theta_*/kappa, because 1-exp(-a)<a for a>0.

For fixed kappa!=1,
A_D=1+(d/kappa)Delta+O(Delta^2), B_D=Delta/kappa+O(Delta^2).
For sufficiently large M, A_D-1 is nonzero, and geometric summation and
telescoping the scalar recurrence give exactly

    t_M=B_D(A_D^M-1)/(A_D-1),
    S_M=M a_D+(1+kappa^2)B_D(t_M-MB_D)/(A_D-1),
    a_D=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D.

Since M Delta=theta_*, A_D^M->exp(d theta_*/kappa),
B_D/(A_D-1)->1/d, and M a_D->(kappa+kappa^(-1))theta_*,
these expressions converge to T_eq,S_eq. Negative d presents no
inequality issue, since these are exact identities and limits.

At kappa1, A_D=1+O(Delta^2), B_D=Delta+O(Delta^2). Uniformly over
l<=M, A_D^l=1+O(1/M), so t_l=l Delta+O(1/M). Consequently
t_M->theta_* and Delta sum_(l=0)^(M-1)t_l->theta_*^2/2. The exact
formula for alpha_l then gives S_M->2theta_*+theta_*^2. These are
also the removable limits of the neighboring expressions: the function
t(x)=[exp((d/kappa)x)-1]/d, continuously extended at kappa1, has
t(x)=x there, and
S_eq=(1+kappa^2)/kappa integral_0^theta_* (1+t(x)) dx.
All fixed-parameter limits are finite and positive. None gives a
uniform bound on the finite-M error as parameters vary.

For a local endpoint with scale sigma, its actual proximal point is
the center z_circ, so its jet is

    x_end=(1-sigma P,sigma Q),
    g_end=sigma(epsilon,-1), f_end=sigma^2(1+epsilon^2)/2.

Prescribing the zero-height minimum jet at0 and applying the original
ordered residual convention yields exactly

    Q_(end,star)
      =sigma[-epsilon+sigma(epsilon^2+epsilon P+1+Q)].     (A8)

The row is nonpositive if and only if
sigma^(-1)>=epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon. This is the actual original
row, not an inequality obtained by ignoring other planes or changing
the radius.

Subtract the horizontal allowance c S_M before discussing an additive
cost. By(A7), the remaining exact finite excess is

    E_M=epsilon+P_M+(1+Q_M)/epsilon-c S_M
       ->E_eq=c[1+(1+kappa/epsilon)T_eq-S_eq].             (A9)

For an inverse scale c S_M+A, substitution in(A8) gives the more
transparent exact residual sigma^2 epsilon(E_M-A).
For inverse scale c S_M+epsilon beta+A, it is
sigma^2 epsilon(E_M-A-epsilon beta). Thus universality with A>=0
forces, respectively,

    A>=max(0,E_eq), or A>=max(0,E_eq-epsilon beta).

If the applicable strict bound fails, E_M's fixed-parameter convergence
produces a finite M with a positive original residual. No conclusion
about equality at the limiting bound, a uniform M, or positivity for
all large kappa is required or asserted. Positive sigma is always
available in the intended denominators; S_M>0 for this nonempty family.

For 0<epsilon<=kappa<=1/2, d>0, T_eq=T_sp, and direct algebra gives

    E_eq=c[1+(kappa/epsilon-2kappa^2/d)T_sp
                    +kappa(1+kappa^2)theta_*/d].         (A10)

The coefficient of T_sp is at least1-2kappa^2/d>=1/3, so E_eq>0.
To independently check uniformity in epsilon<=kappa, write T=T_sp.
For sufficiently small kappa, theta_*>=arctan(1/kappa)>=pi/4 and
d>=3/4. Thus T grows at least exponentially in a positive constant
times1/kappa, uniformly in epsilon. Also theta_*<pi/2 and c->1
uniformly. Dividing(A10) by c(kappa/epsilon)T gives the exact ratio

    1-2kappa epsilon/d+epsilon/(kappa T)
                        +epsilon(1+kappa^2)theta_*/(d T).

Its difference from1 is bounded in absolute value by

    2kappa^2/d+1/T+kappa(1+kappa^2)theta_*/(d T),

which tends to0 uniformly. For A_sp the exact ratio is

    A_sp/[c(1+kappa)T/epsilon]
      =1+(epsilon^2+c)/[c(1+kappa)T],

also uniformly tending to1. Their quotient proves
kappa A_sp/E_eq->1, with positive denominators eventually bounded
away from0 in the ratio comparison.

Let X=d theta_*/kappa. Then X->infinity uniformly and

    log T=X-log d+log(1-exp(-X)).

Multiplying by kappa, the last two terms vanish uniformly, and
d theta_*->pi/2 uniformly because epsilon<=kappa->0. The preceding
ratio identities now prove

    kappa log(epsilon A_sp)->pi/2,
    kappa log((epsilon/kappa)E_eq)->pi/2.

The epsilon factors have not been dropped; indeed epsilon may shrink
so fast that kappa log(1/epsilon) is unbounded. These are uniform
asymptotics of explicitly displayed limiting constants, not uniform
finite-M approximation statements.

At the smallest legal local checkpoint beta=1+epsilon^(-2),

    E_eq/(epsilon beta)
      =[c kappa T/(1+epsilon^2)](1+o(1))->infinity

uniformly over epsilon<=kappa as kappa->0. This verifies that its
explicit epsilon beta term does not remove the small-curvature
activation obstruction. It does not claim the same for arbitrarily
growing beta; the exact cost in(A9) retains epsilon beta.

## 11. Accumulated losses, examples of scaling, and equality cases

For s_i>0 at each selected checkpoint define the formal main diagnostic

    I_env=product_i(b_i/s_i)^2/(1+2s_k).

If L_env denotes exactly the lower expression(7), direct cancellation
of the terminal factor and each numerator gives

    log(I_env/L_env)
      =2sum_i log[c+max(epsilon b_i,A_sp)/s_i]
                         -2sum_i log(1-1/b_i).

Since c>1 and b_i>1 this is at least2k log c. The inequality
log z<=z-1 for z>0 bounds the first terms by
2sum_i[c-1+max(epsilon b_i,A_sp)/s_i]. Applying the same inequality
to b_i/(b_i-1) bounds the remaining terms by2sum_i1/(b_i-1).
This gives the exact upper bound(41).

For the source diagnostic
I_source=product_i(b_i/s_i)^2/[2(1+s_k)] and source payoff L_source
given by(8), the exact cancellation gives

    log(I_source/L_source)
      =2sum_i log[c+(2A_sp+epsilon b_i)/s_i].

Its lower and upper bounds follow in the same way. The source diagnostic
uses its own terminal factor; it is not I_env. The identity concerns
the specified-minimizer payoff(8); the better closest-radius payoff is
explicitly divided by rho_source^2 and should not be silently substituted
into this diagnostic identity.

Both lower coefficient contributions are exactly

    2k log c=k log(1+epsilon^2)+2k kappa theta_*.

This identity remains true for arbitrarily large finite k. It does not
turn a small single-block coefficient or vertical overshoot into a
uniformly negligible total. No analogous logarithmic diagnostic is
defined at s_i0; the actual payoff formulas remain positive and defined
there. All threshold inequalities and scale inequalities permit
equality, and all arguments above use non-strict inactivity or support
inequalities wherever a boundary tie can occur.

Along the specific law epsilon=kappa^2, theta_*=pi/2-kappa^2+O(kappa^6).
Therefore log c=(pi/2)kappa+O(kappa^3), which implies the displayed
c=1+(pi/2)kappa+O(kappa^2). More importantly, the exact identity

    c exp(d theta_*/kappa)
      =sqrt(1+epsilon^2)exp(theta_*/kappa)

shows that the exponential multiplier relative to exp(pi/(2kappa))
tends to1 on this law. Combining this with(A10) and the exact A_sp
formula yields A_sp~kappa^(-2)exp(pi/(2kappa)) and
E_eq~kappa^(-1)exp(pi/(2kappa)). These equivalences have not lost a
constant in an exponent. The unchanged rectangle threshold gives
B_sp=1+kappa^(-4).

Finally, for any fixed kappa>0, theta_*->pi/2 as epsilon->0. Both
branches of T_sp remain bounded by a constant depending on this fixed
kappa, c->exp(kappa pi/2), and
A_sp=epsilon+c[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]/epsilon=O_kappa(1/epsilon).
This claim is valid also at kappa1. Its constants need not be uniform
as kappa varies. The fixed coefficient c>1 contributes exactly the
displayed loss repeated k times. It gives no all-word negligible-loss
claim.

## 12. Repairs and clarification, separate from the frozen text

No mathematical repair is required by the audit. The source was left
unchanged.

For a future revision only, the sentence at frozen lines737--738 saying
"a proposed smaller A" could explicitly say
"A<E_eq for denominator c_sp S+A, or A<E_eq-epsilon beta for
denominator c_sp S+epsilon beta+A, with the proposed A nonnegative."
Those are already the exact conditions in frozen lines702--705 and in
the residual derivation above. The sentence is read in that immediate
context. It does not justify replacing them by the much stronger and
generally unsupported condition A<A_sp. This is a clarity suggestion,
not a recorded FAIL or GAP in the existing displayed mathematics.

The exact-versus-sufficient distinctions should be retained in any
subsequent version: qmax_sp is exact; N_sp, T_sp, H_sp, and B_sp are
sufficient bounds; T_eq is the equal-angle family's exact limit and
differs from T_sp above kappa1. Both radius statements concern their
own separately defined objectives.

## 13. Remaining target and operational closeout

The accepted original benchmark definitions are

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,
    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

The still-open original obligation requires, for each delta>0, one
horizon threshold independent of h, and for every larger physical
length n and every nonnegative word h a legal actual-adversary payoff
at least(1-delta)/B_n. This audit supplies no such comparison, no
optimal checkpoint selection, no positive primitive seed, and no
target theorem fact. Enlarging a parameter domain and preserving the
finite construction do not prove that original uniform statement.

All newly reasoned mathematics above is joint unverified audit evidence.
No formal gate was called; no fact was promoted. The first-run main-agent
setup workflow is not a worker-round task. Reads were limited to the
assigned worker state, explicitly assigned shared candidate and primary
source artifacts, shared memory/facts, and the explicitly supplied skill
instructions. No sibling's private local memory was read.

Operational observations: the first broad fact/memory display was
truncated, so the complete candidate and relevant accepted conventions
were subsequently read in bounded, direct excerpts. The targeted shared
glossary query for the new scoped spiral symbols returned empty output
with exit status1; all such symbols are defined in this report and the
frozen candidate. Matlas returned unrelated hypotheses, so the exact
primary paper was used for the limited comparison stated in Section2.
These were not treated as successful theorem matches.

The only executed programs performed file reading/writing, text
retrieval, source copying, and integrity/metadata checks. There were
zero mathematical evaluations, arithmetic replays, solvers, executed
DPs, formal submissions, consults, sub-agents, or installations. This
finishes the one assigned analytical audit; it grants no automatic
renewal. Original target: **OPEN**. Audit formal status: **UNVERIFIED**.

## 14. Scoped notation index

The original source's sp-suffixed names are retained. theta_*,c,R,e,t,n,K
are local aliases for theta_sp,c_sp,R_sp,e_sp,t_sp,n_sp,K_sp. The scalar
t_j is a nonnegative normal coefficient. p,q are the positive horizontal
and nonnegative downward coordinates of the arc; qmax_sp is their exact
vertical maximum. N_sp bounds a negative horizontal normal displacement;
B_sp is the sufficient main physical checkpoint threshold. d=1-kappa^2;
T_sp,H_sp,A_sp,a_sp are the source's sufficient constants. P,Q,S are
actual local displacements and ordinary-step sum; primed scales and

J_sp,f belong to the main envelope. G_i,D'_i,ell'_i,sigma'_i,F belong

C_rad,A_rad,t_rad,K_S,r_S,lambda_S,rho_source are the explicit source

Q_ab is the original ordered smooth-convex residual of accepted

# Dense-history family obstruction handoff

**JOINT UNVERIFIED — STOP.** One invocation completed; no renewal, formal submission, or accepted fact. Original minimax target OPEN. Parent closes allocation `6c03ab372c46e5bb`.

## Result to audit

`REPORT.md` independently checks the exact variable-step multiplier identity, all cut/divergence conventions, physical/terminal diagonal coefficients, and the full-class threshold

\[
\Gamma\ge\frac{225}{289}(S+\tfrac12)^2-\tfrac14
\implies \mathcal W_n(h)\le\frac{17}{16(1+2S)}.
\]

It then excludes the entire specified free monotone `A/D` family on the actual same-enclosing-`m` hierarchy for every `m>=5`, stronger than the requested range `m>=1536`. Arbitrary nonnegative diagonal slack and arbitrary terminal penalty are included. Zero explicit laws were instantiated because the family-level obstruction settles their feasibility jointly.

## Short proof chain

Define `delta_k=q_k-r_k^2 q_(k+1)`, `T_k=sum_(i<k)h_i`,

\[
B_k=\sum_{i<k}h_iA_i,\qquad E_k=\sum_{i\ge k}h_iD_{i+1}.
\]

All cuts imply the exact identity

\[
q_k+\sum_{i<k}\delta_i=(T_k+1)^2-2B_kE_k.
\]

The proof is an increment calculation using

\[
A_kE_k-D_{k+1}B_k-2r_kA_kD_{k+1}=T_k+h_k.
\]

Nonnegative slack gives `q_k<=(T_k+1)^2`, strictly at every interior index. Strictness follows because the last positive cut forces `D_n>0`, hence all `D_i>0`, and both `B_k,E_k` are positive in the interior.

At the central bridge of a palindrome with side mass `s` and step `1+r`, the central cut and decreasing `D` give

\[
s+1+r<A_kD_{k+1}(s+1-r).
\]

Thus `s+1-r>0` and

\[
q_{k+1}>\frac{3(s+1)+r}{s+1-r},\qquad
r^2q_{k+1}\le q_k<(s+1)^2.
\]

With `x=r/(s+1)`, these require

\[
(x+1)(x^2+2x-1)<0,
\quad\text{hence}\quad x<\sqrt2-1.
\]

For the actual word, fix the same enclosing `m`, set `j=m-1` and `Lambda=1+sqrt(2)`. Then

\[
r=ac^j,\qquad s=2^j-1+a\frac{c^j-2^j}{c-2},
\]
\[
\Lambda-\frac{s+1}{r}
=\frac{\varepsilon}{\beta(c-2)}
\left[\beta\Lambda^2-
(2\Lambda+3+\Lambda^2\varepsilon)(2/c)^j\right]>0
\]

for every `m>=5`. The exact uniform comparison is first bracket term `>9/2` and second term `<9/2`, using `epsilon<=1/20`, `beta>9/10`, `12/5<Lambda<5/2`, and `(2/c)^j<(5/6)^4<1/2`. Thus `x>sqrt(2)-1`, a contradiction.

## Scope and routing

The old `h_i h_j(u+v 1_(i>j))` kernel is a different family; its old exclusion was not used. Chikhi's paper is prior literature, not a new discovery or a variable-step theorem imported here.

This is a multiplier-family obstruction, not a legal hard instance or a disproof of the desired GD upper bound. No target-level verification should be requested as if this resolved the minimax problem. If the parent selects this result for verification, the exact statement to audit is the incompatibility of the specified scalar cut, monotonicity, and diagonal conditions, with the prefix identity and strict root inequality as its principal dependencies.

Resources used: public text/source reads, manual reasoning, local Markdown writes, byte hashes only. Mathematical programs, arithmetic/recurrence/trajectory replays, solvers, numerical-array analysis, formal calls, models beyond this invocation, subagents, service/worker actions, and truth-store edits: zero. The application Goal was not changed and is still paused in the supplied current report. Current-turn research authorization came from the explicit human continuation.

TASK SHA-256: `06ad3ddf6b078cdee2ee8f9fd48fabedd2c8b56fe38c8c9aa050871d1e7a2e79`.

STOP. No automatic renewal.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002390 -->
# Dense two-sequence history certificates: exact family obstruction

**JOINT UNVERIFIED.** One direct analytical invocation on 2026-10-08. No verifier submission or accepted fact is claimed. The original exact-profile minimax problem remains OPEN.

The specified free monotone `A/D` family is infeasible on the actual same-enclosing-`m` hierarchy for every `m >= 5`, hence for every requested `m >= 1536`. This conclusion allows arbitrary nonnegative diagonal slack and arbitrary terminal penalty. It follows from the cut equations, monotonicity, and physical diagonal signs alone, before imposing the target terminal penalty.

The decisive new estimate is an exact prefix identity. It yields a necessary central-bridge bound on every palindromic word in this family. The actual hierarchy violates that bound. No explicit law needs to be tested after this family-level exclusion; the number of instantiated laws is zero, within the two-law ceiling.

## 1. Scope, sources, and fixed notation

For one fixed enclosing integer `m`, define

\[
\Lambda=1+\sqrt2,\qquad \varepsilon=\frac1{4m},\qquad
\beta=1-\varepsilon,\qquad c=\Lambda(1+\varepsilon),\qquad a=\frac\beta c,
\]
\[
H_0=(),\qquad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j),\qquad
h=H_m,\quad n=2^m-1,\quad S=\sum_{i<n}h_i.
\]

Every child in this report retains these same parameters. The index `n` is the number of physical updates; the terminal gradient is not an extra update.

The assignment and `ROOT_DERIVATION_UNVERIFIED.md` define the candidate family. Its source motivation is Section 4 of [Chikhi, arXiv:2609.13765v2](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.13765v2), a constant-schedule construction. That source was already inspected in October 1 project work. The present variable-step calculations are independently derived here; no theorem about the source's constant schedules or proximal objective is imported as an upper bound for this hierarchy.

The earlier public draft `research/shared_evidence/dd4045b39aa0c257.md`, Sections 1-2, uses the prefix kernel

\[
K_{ij}=h_i h_j\bigl(u+v\mathbf1_{i>j}\bigr)
\]

and a prescribed minimizer/terminal completion. Its scoped obstruction is not a premise here. The present `A/D` family has independent sequences, a different history inequality, and diagonal slack. The proof below excludes this new family on its own terms.

The verification depth is source-bound and manual analytical derivation. “UNVERIFIED” means not independently accepted by the Danus verify service; it does not denote numerical evidence.

## 2. Independent check of the sufficient interface

Normalize `L=1`. Let `h_i>1`, `r_i=h_i-1`, and let an actual convex 1-smooth GD trajectory satisfy

\[
x_{i+1}=x_i-h_i g_i,\qquad g_i=\nabla f(x_i).
\]

For distinct physical or terminal indices use the positive interpolation orientation

\[
Q_{ij}=2(f_i-f_j)-2\langle g_j,x_i-x_j\rangle
-\|g_i-g_j\|^2\ge0.
\]

Take finite real sequences satisfying

\[
A_{-1}=0<A_0\le\cdots\le A_{n-1},\qquad
D_1\ge\cdots\ge D_n\ge D_{n+1}=0.
\]

The bookkeeping value `r_n=0` supplies no physical step. For `0<=u<v<=n`, set

\[
\lambda_{uv}=(A_u-A_{u-1})(D_v+r_vD_{v+1}),
\qquad
\lambda_{vu}=(A_{u-1}+r_uA_u)(D_v-D_{v+1}).
\]

All these multipliers are nonnegative. The cumulative cut from indices `0,...,k` to indices `k+1,...,n` is

\[
F_k=A_k\left[D_{k+1}+\sum_{b=k+2}^{n}h_{b-1}D_b\right]
-D_{k+1}\left[r_kA_k+\sum_{u<k}h_uA_u\right].
\tag{2.1}
\]

Writing `T_k=sum_{i<k}h_i`, the constraints `F_k=T_{k+1}` are exactly the required divergence equations: the divergence is `h_i` at every physical index and `-S` at the terminal index. Consecutive cuts give the individual physical divergences, including the first one; total divergence gives the terminal one.

### Mixed coefficients

For `u<v`, the coefficient of `2<g_u,g_v>` in `sum lambda_ij Q_ij` is

\[
\lambda_{uv}+\lambda_{vu}
-h_u\sum_{i\le u}\lambda_{iv}
+h_v\sum_{i>v}\lambda_{iu}.
\]

At `v=n` the final sum is empty and no `h_n` is used. The two sums that occur are

\[
\sum_{i\le u}\lambda_{iv}=A_u(D_v+r_vD_{v+1}),\qquad
\sum_{i>v}\lambda_{iu}=(A_{u-1}+r_uA_u)D_{v+1}.
\]

Substitution gives zero, using only `h_u=1+r_u` and `h_v=1+r_v` at physical indices. At the terminal index it gives the same cancellation with the empty last sum. Thus the mixed cancellation is valid for the variable steps.

### Diagonal coefficients

Define

\[
q_0=1,\qquad q_k=1+2A_{k-1}D_k\quad(1\le k\le n),
\qquad \delta_k=q_k-r_k^2q_{k+1}\quad(0\le k<n).
\]

At a physical index `k`, incoming earlier mass is

\[
A_{k-1}(D_k+r_kD_{k+1}),
\]

and incoming later mass is

\[
(A_{k-1}+r_kA_k)D_{k+1}.
\]

Using divergence `h_k`, the coefficient of `||g_k||^2` is consequently

\[
-h_k-2A_{k-1}(D_k+r_kD_{k+1})
+2r_k(A_{k-1}+r_kA_k)D_{k+1}
=-h_k+2r_k^2A_kD_{k+1}-2A_{k-1}D_k.
\]

The term involving `A_{-1}` is zero at `k=0`. At the terminal index the coefficient is `S+1-q_n`. Therefore the root identity is correct:

\[
\begin{aligned}
&2\sum_{k<n}h_k(f_k-f_n)
+\sum_{k<n}h_k(1-h_k)\|g_k\|^2-\Gamma\|g_n\|^2\\
&\quad=\sum_{i\ne j}\lambda_{ij}Q_{ij}
+\sum_{k<n}\delta_k\|g_k\|^2
+(q_n-1-S-\Gamma)\|g_n\|^2.
\end{aligned}
\tag{2.2}
\]

Thus all cut equations, monotonicity, `delta_k>=0`, and `q_n>=1+S+Gamma` are a sufficient interface. This audit identifies no sign or index correction to that interface.

### Terminal normalization and the `17/16` threshold

Let the left side of (2.2) be `H_Gamma`. Summing the distance identity and the valid minimizer interpolation inequality gives

\[
R^2\ge\|x_n-x_\star\|^2+2S(f_n-f_\star)
+\Gamma\|g_n\|^2+H_\Gamma,
\qquad R=\|x_0-x_\star\|.
\]

If `H_Gamma>=0`, set `Delta=f_n-f_star`. For `g_n!=0`, terminal interpolation and Cauchy-Schwarz yield

\[
R^2\ge(2S+1)\Delta
+\frac{\Delta^2}{\|g_n\|^2}
+\left(\Gamma+\frac14\right)\|g_n\|^2.
\]

For `Gamma+1/4>=0`, the last two terms are at least
`2 Delta sqrt(Gamma+1/4)`. If `g_n=0`, convexity gives `Delta=0`. With `W=2 Delta/R^2`, this is the full-class implication

\[
\mathcal W_n(h)\le
\frac1{S+\frac12+\sqrt{\Gamma+\frac14}}.
\]

The sufficient terminal threshold is therefore exactly

\[
\Gamma\ge\frac{225}{289}\left(S+\frac12\right)^2-\frac14
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\mathcal W_n(h)\le\frac{17}{16(1+2S)}.
\tag{2.3}
\]

This implication keeps the original chosen minimizer and its distance. It remains valid as a conditional statement despite the family obstruction below.

## 3. Exact prefix identity with arbitrary diagonal slack

For `0<=k<=n`, define the two nonnegative weighted sums

\[
B_k=\sum_{i<k}h_iA_i,\qquad
E_k=\sum_{i=k}^{n-1}h_iD_{i+1},\qquad
\Delta_k^{\rm slack}=\sum_{i<k}\delta_i.
\]

The empty sums are `B_0=0`, `E_n=0`, and `Delta_0^slack=0`. The symbol `Delta_k^slack` is a sum of certificate residuals, unrelated to the function gap in Section 2.

**Prefix identity.** Every solution of all the cut equations satisfies

\[
\boxed{q_k+\Delta_k^{\rm slack}=(T_k+1)^2-2B_kE_k,
\qquad 0\le k\le n.}
\tag{3.1}
\]

This identity does not assume any diagonal equality or prescribed product law.

To prove it, fix `k<n` and abbreviate `h=h_k`, `r=r_k`, `A=A_k`, and `D=D_{k+1}`. Equation (2.1) becomes

\[
AE_k-DB_k-2rAD=T_k+h.
\tag{3.2}
\]

The exact updates of the finite sums are

\[
B_{k+1}=B_k+hA,\qquad E_{k+1}=E_k-hD,
\qquad q_{k+1}=1+2AD.
\]

Using (3.2), the increment of the right side of (3.1) is

\[
\begin{aligned}
&2h(T_k+1)+h^2-2h(AE_k-DB_k)+2h^2AD\\
&\qquad=h(2-h)(1+2AD)=(1-r^2)q_{k+1}.
\end{aligned}
\]

The increment of the left side is also

\[
q_{k+1}-q_k+\delta_k=(1-r^2)q_{k+1}.
\]

Both sides equal one at `k=0`, proving (3.1) by symbolic induction.

If every `delta_i>=0`, (3.1) gives the quantitative prefix bound

\[
q_k\le(T_k+1)^2.
\tag{3.3}
\]

In fact the inequality is strict for `1<=k<n`. The last cut is

\[
S=D_n\left[(1-r_{n-1})A_{n-1}-B_{n-1}\right].
\]

Since `S>0`, it forces `D_n>0`; monotonicity then gives every `D_i>0`. Thus `B_k>0` and `E_k>0` at every such interior index. The strict version follows from (3.1), even if all preceding diagonal slacks vanish.

At the terminal index, (3.1) specializes to

\[
q_n+\sum_{k<n}\delta_k=(S+1)^2.
\tag{3.4}
\]

Consequently, if the largest compatible penalty `Gamma=q_n-1-S` were used, the threshold (2.3) would require and be equivalent to

\[
\sum_{k<n}\delta_k\le
\frac{64}{289}\left(S+\frac12\right)^2.
\]

The exclusion below uses the strictly stronger prefix information in (3.1), rather than treating this global slack budget as the only constraint.

## 4. A necessary central-bridge inequality for the entire family

Consider any palindrome with a nonempty left block of total mass `s`, central physical step `1+r` with `r>0`, and right block of the same total mass `s`. Let `k` be the central physical index. Thus

\[
T_k=s,\qquad T_{k+1}=s+1+r.
\]

Assume the full free `A/D` family is feasible, including every physical diagonal sign. This implies

\[
r^2q_{k+1}\le q_k<(s+1)^2.
\tag{4.1}
\]

In the central cut, `D_{i+1}<=D_{k+1}` for every right-block index `i>k`. Therefore

\[
E_{k+1}\le sD_{k+1}.
\]

Writing `z=A_kD_{k+1}>0`, equation (2.1) and `B_k>0` give

\[
\begin{aligned}
s+1+r
&=A_kE_{k+1}+(1-r)z-D_{k+1}B_k\\
&<z(s+1-r).
\end{aligned}
\tag{4.2}
\]

It follows first that `s+1-r>0`; if this denominator were nonpositive, feasibility would already be impossible. Since `q_{k+1}=1+2z`, (4.2) then implies

\[
q_{k+1}>1+\frac{2(s+1+r)}{s+1-r}
=\frac{3(s+1)+r}{s+1-r}.
\tag{4.3}
\]

Set `x=r/(s+1)`, so that `0<x<1`. Combining (4.1) and (4.3) gives

\[
x^2\frac{3+x}{1-x}<1.
\]

The numerator after bringing the right side to the left factors exactly:

\[
x^3+3x^2+x-1=(x+1)(x^2+2x-1).
\]

Thus every feasible member of this family necessarily satisfies

\[
\boxed{\frac r{s+1}<\sqrt2-1=\frac1\Lambda.}
\tag{4.4}
\]

The proof allows arbitrary dependence of `A` and `D` on the whole word, arbitrary nonnegative diagonal slack, and every terminal value. No child certificate, local saturation equality, deleted interpolation row, or fixed multiplier array was assumed.

## 5. The actual same-`m` hierarchy violates the necessary inequality

Let `m>=5` and `j=m-1`. At the central bridge of `H_m`,

\[
r=ac^j,\qquad s=\sum H_j.
\]

The exact finite geometric identity for the left mass is

\[
s=2^j-1+a\frac{c^j-2^j}{c-2}.
\tag{5.1}
\]

It follows either by counting the `2^(j-1-l)` copies of the level-`l` step or by symbolic induction on the defining concatenation. No recurrence was executed. Dividing (5.1) by `r` gives

\[
\frac{s+1}{r}
=\frac1{c-2}
+\left(\frac1a-\frac1{c-2}\right)\left(\frac2c\right)^j.
\tag{5.2}
\]

Use `Lambda^2=2 Lambda+1`. Direct symbolic expansion yields

\[
\Lambda(c-2)-1=\Lambda^2\varepsilon,
\]
\[
c-2-a=\frac{\varepsilon}{c}
\left(2\Lambda+3+\Lambda^2\varepsilon\right).
\]

Consequently (5.2) gives the exact gap formula

\[
\Lambda-\frac{s+1}{r}
=\frac{\varepsilon}{\beta(c-2)}
\left[
\beta\Lambda^2-
\left(2\Lambda+3+\Lambda^2\varepsilon\right)
\left(\frac2c\right)^j
\right].
\tag{5.3}
\]

Every prefactor in (5.3) is positive. The bracket is strictly positive uniformly for every `m>=5`, as shown by the following rational bounds:

\[
\varepsilon\le\frac1{20},\quad
\beta\ge\frac{19}{20}>\frac9{10},\quad
\frac{12}{5}<\Lambda<\frac52,\quad \Lambda^2>5.
\]

Thus

\[
\beta\Lambda^2>\frac92,
\qquad
2\Lambda+3+\Lambda^2\varepsilon
<8+\frac5{16}<9.
\]

Also `j>=4` and `c>Lambda>12/5`, so

\[
\left(\frac2c\right)^j
<\left(\frac56\right)^4
=\frac{625}{1296}<\frac12.
\]

The second term in the bracket of (5.3) is therefore smaller than `9/2`, whereas the first is larger than `9/2`. Equation (5.3) proves

\[
\frac{s+1}{r}<\Lambda,
\qquad\text{equivalently}\qquad
\boxed{\frac r{s+1}>\frac1\Lambda.}
\tag{5.4}
\]

Equations (4.4) and (5.4) contradict one another. Hence no monotone nonnegative `A,D` can satisfy all the cut equations and all the nonnegative physical diagonal conditions on these words for any `m>=5`. In particular, no terminal `Gamma` in the specified family can reach the `17/16` target at any requested `m>=1536`.

## 6. Consequence and limits

The family-level exclusion is complete within this direct analytical draft. There is no remaining inequality for an explicit `A/D` law to solve on the requested hierarchy: the necessary root condition already fails. Reopening the same family with another scalar law or another allocation of nonnegative diagonal slack cannot evade (3.1)-(5.4).

This proof supplies neither an interpolation-feasible hard function nor a lower bound on `W_n(H_m)`. It does not assert that the history inequality `H_Gamma>=0` is false when certified by a different multiplier family. It does not exclude a certificate with a non-diagonal positive semidefinite residual, another dense kernel, or a different potential. Accordingly it cannot be combined with the task-supplied accepted benchmark comparison to conclude separation. The original objective upper bound and original minimax target remain OPEN.

No mathematical program, symbolic engine, arithmetic or trajectory replay, solver, numerical-array analysis, formal call, subagent, other model, consult, worker/service action, truth-store edit, publication, finalization, revocation, or shutdown was used. Only public text and primary-source reads, manual analysis, local Markdown writes, and byte hashes were used. The application Goal was not changed and remains paused under the supplied live-state report; this invocation was authorized by the explicit current-turn continuation.
